30 May 2026 — **Spec B7 — RFQs shipped**. Seventh Phase-2 buyer spec. New migration `0028_rfqs.sql` adds two tables (`public.rfqs` with `target_supplier_ids uuid[]` cardinality 1..50, `quantity_unit`, `currency`, `ship_to_country`, `ship_by`, `status rfq_status enum`, `accepted_quote_id`; `public.rfq_quotes` with `unit_price`, `currency`, `lead_time_days`, `moq`, `valid_until`, `notes`, `status rfq_quote_status enum`, `unique(rfq_id, supplier_id)`) + two enums (`rfq_status('open','accepted','closed','cancelled')` + `rfq_quote_status('submitted','accepted','rejected','withdrawn')`) + five SECURITY DEFINER RPCs (`rfq_create`/`rfq_list`/`rfq_get`/`rfq_quote_submit`/`rfq_quote_accept`). RLS SELECT-only policies on both tables (no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE — every write goes through an RPC). Buyer visibility = `buyer_id = auth.uid()`; supplier visibility = `exists (suppliers s where s.claimed_by = auth.uid() and s.id = any(target_supplier_ids))`. `rfq_create` validates buyer role via `profiles.role in ('buyer','admin')`, checks every target supplier is `is_published=true and is_sanctioned=false`, then inserts the RFQ and materialises one per-supplier `message_threads` row keyed by the new `rfq_id` (re-using the column reserved in B6's 0027 migration and seeding the buyer + claimed-supplier `thread_participants` rows inline so the RFQ is the parent of its own conversation thread). `rfq_quote_submit` (supplier-side) auto-resolves the caller's claimed supplier inside the target set, upserts on `(rfq_id, supplier_id)`, and rejects on RFQ status ≠ 'open'. `rfq_quote_accept` (buyer-side) flips the chosen quote to 'accepted', sibling quotes to 'rejected', and the parent RFQ to 'accepted' + `accepted_quote_id` set in a single transaction. New API route `app/api/v1/rfqs/route.ts` (GET list/get + POST `{action:create|submit_quote|accept_quote}`, in-handler auth via `supabase.auth.getUser()`). New buyer pages `app/(app)/app/rfqs/page.tsx` (inbox grouped by status with `viewer_role` discriminator), `app/(app)/app/rfqs/[id]/page.tsx` (spec + targets + quotes + per-target message-thread link), `app/(app)/app/rfqs/new/page.tsx` (single-supplier compose form gated on `is_published+!is_sanctioned`). New client islands `components/rfq-create-form.tsx` + `components/accept-quote-button.tsx`. "Request a quote" primary button added beside `SaveButton` in the supplier profile header. Dashboard "Active RFQs" tile wired to live `select count(*) where status='open'` (was hardcoded 0 muted). Production smoke (`ops/_b7_smoke.py`) verifies (a) cross-buyer isolation (buyer B2 sees zero rows via rfq_list / rfq_get / direct SELECT), (b) targeted-supplier visibility + submit, (c) buyer cannot submit quote, (d) non-targeted supplier cannot submit, (e) accept_quote transitions S1→accepted, S2→rejected, RFQ→accepted with `accepted_quote_id` set, (f) submit / accept on a closed RFQ both blocked, (g) `message_threads` materialised with `rfq_id` for both targets, (h) recursive forbidden-key leak diff `[]` against `{email_primary, phones, contact_name, contact_role, website, sbi, sbi_total, total_score, pillar1_legal, pillar2_safety, pillar3_certs, pillar4_market}`. Smoke prints `B7 SMOKE PASS`. `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` zero warnings; `pnpm build` ✓ 41 s (28 routes total; new `/app/rfqs` 181 B / 106 kB, `/app/rfqs/[id]` 2.51 kB / 115 kB, `/app/rfqs/new` 3.57 kB / 116 kB, `/api/v1/rfqs` 163 B). Architectural decisions logged below.

29 May 2026 — **Spec B6 — Messages shipped**. Sixth Phase-2 buyer spec. New migration `0027_messages.sql` adds `public.message_threads` (one row per buyer×supplier×rfq via `unique nulls not distinct`) + `public.thread_participants` + `public.messages` with `body_ciphertext bytea`. Encryption-at-rest via `pgcrypto::pgp_sym_encrypt` (cipher-algo=aes256), key sourced from `current_setting('app.messages_key')` so the app process never sees it. Body-ciphertext column is `revoke select … from authenticated` + the metadata columns are individually granted, so the Supabase Realtime payload carries only `{id, thread_id, sender_id, body_len, created_at}` and clients must round-trip through the `thread_messages` RPC for server-side decryption. All writes are `security definer` RPCs (`thread_open`, `thread_send_message`, `thread_messages`, `thread_list`); no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE RLS policies exist on any of the three tables. RLS SELECT on `messages` + `message_threads` checks participant membership via `thread_participants`; the participants policy itself is `using (user_id = auth.uid())` to avoid recursive policy planning. New `lib/supabase/browser.ts` memoised client. New `/api/v1/messages` route handler (POST `open`/`send`, GET list/thread). New `/app/messages` server-rendered inbox + `/app/messages/[thread]` server-rendered thread with a `'use client'` realtime island that subscribes to `messages:thread=<id>` Postgres-Changes INSERT events. Production smoke (`ops/_b6_smoke.py`) validates idempotent thread_open, encrypted send/decrypt round-trip, non-participant denial, body_ciphertext column-grant denial, no insert-policy bypass, `body_len` matches plaintext lengths, and recursive forbidden-key leak diff `[]` across the full payload.

26 May 2026 — **OEKO-TEX F4b production re-scrape attempted, then aborted by user**. Kicked off the deferred F4b re-scrape per the 22 May close-out item (code + view + image already deployed; only thing pending was the actual production write). Wrapper script `ops/_oeko_rescrape.sh` uploaded to VPS, run inside `tmux new-session -d -s oeko_rescrape "bash /opt/sourcebd/_oeko_rescrape.sh"` invoking `docker compose run --rm etl run oeko_tex` with output tee'd to `/opt/sourcebd/logs/oeko_rescrape_20260526_0330.log`. Container `sourcebd-etl-run-33af7b3f9181` started cleanly at 03:30 UTC; first POST to the OEKO Buying Guide returned `f_count=2556 / pages=52` for STANDARD 100 (matches the pre-existing DB state of 2,856 OEKO rows / 2,576 source_records from prior scrapes — confirming this was a re-enrichment pass, NOT a first scrape). All 11 events emitted in the first ~40s were `supplier.enriched` (zero `supplier.created`), proving Pass-0 self-source idempotency (added to `etl/core/upsert.py::_find_existing` during Spec 16) works as designed for OEKO. Realised per-record runtime (~4.5s incl. profile fetch + 0.4s polite delay + parse + upsert) projects to ~3.5–4 h total wall-clock — the 22 May "~30 min" estimate had ignored the sequential per-record profile fetch cost. **User then asked for the session to be killed** after ~11 min of runtime. Cleanup: `tmux kill-session -t oeko_rescrape` (succeeded; tmux server then exited because it was the last session), followed by `docker stop sourcebd-etl-run-33af7b3f9181` (the tmux kill only reaped the bash/tee wrapper — the docker container holding the python process kept running until explicitly stopped). The unrelated long-running container `sourcebd-etl-run-aab9f805fe8d` (up 31 h, separate ETL job) was deliberately left untouched. **Partial side effects (intentionally retained, not rolled back)**: roughly the first ~150 OEKO records (those whose profile-page fetch completed in the ~11 min window) had `oeko_profile_address` / `oeko_profile_phone` / `oeko_profile_email` / `oeko_profile_website` written into `source_records.fields`, and corresponding `suppliers.email_primary` / `phones` / `website` / `address_raw` lifted via the `_enrich_supplier` COALESCE path (never overwrites non-null). All writes are idempotent on re-run via the Pass-0 self-source lookup, so a future re-kick of the full re-scrape will pick up where this left off without duplication. F4b status in the main summary remains "code + view + image deployed; full re-scrape deferred" — the ≈2,400 OEKO records that did not get profile enrichment in this aborted run still need a future production execution. No changes committed to repo this session (the `ops/_oeko_rescrape.sh` wrapper is a local-only ops helper consistent with the existing `ops/_*.py` / `ops/_*.sh` gitignored-by-convention pattern). No new architectural decision logged because no policy or design changed — this was operational execution + abort only.

22 May 2026 — **Moat deep-audit findings F1 + F2 + F4b shipped**. Triggered by three concrete supplier complaints (Naheed Fine Tex, Charming Trim & Packaging, Nafisa Apparels) followed by a corpus-wide audit (`ops/_moat_deep_audit.py`) that ranked seven findings. User approved F1 + F2 + F4b for this session. **F1 — EPB address-view branch (migration 0017)**: audit showed 43 EPB-only suppliers had zero rows in `v_supplier_addresses` despite 472/472 EPB `source_records` carrying `epb_factory_address` + `epb_office_address`; same shape of bug as the BGAPMEA gap fixed by 0016. Migration 0017 recreates the three-view chain with two new EPB branches (`factory` ← `epb_factory_address`, `registered` ← `epb_office_address`, phone/email NULL because EPB public surface doesn't publish them). Pure view fix, additive over 0016, reversible. Result: `suppliers_with_address` 8,419 → **8,473** (+54); EPB-tagged suppliers with addr 0 → **465** (7 still missing reflect non-published / sanctioned filter); 472 `EPB.factory` + 472 `EPB.registered` rows materialised, +21 each as `*_inherited` via the RSC sibling leg. **F2 — entity_type upgrade for BTMA / BGAPMEA / RSC / EPB + missing `*_verified` flags**: audit's unknown×tag matrix showed 183 suppliers stuck at `entity_type='unknown'` despite carrying a Tier-1/2 register tag (128 BTMA, 54 EPB, 1 RSC; 5 BGMEA edge cases that lacked `bgmea_reg_number` in payload; the BGMEA/BKMEA upgrade branches in `etl/core/upsert.py::_apply_source_specific` already covered the "normal" path). Also found 422 BTMA-tagged and 1,074 BGAPMEA-tagged suppliers with `btma_verified=false` / `bgapmea_verified=false` — the post-insert verified-flag write was missing entirely for both. Extended `_apply_source_specific` with four new branches: BTMA / BGAPMEA set their `*_verified=true` and upgrade `unknown→factory`; EPB and RSC upgrade `unknown→factory` only (no `*_verified` column exists for those two). One-time backfill (`ops/_entity_type_backfill.py`) applied to live DB: 422 BTMA rows + 1,074 BGAPMEA rows got `*_verified=true`; 182 rows upgraded `unknown→factory`. Result: `entity_type` distribution `[factory 7,539 / buying_house 1,434 / unknown 1,213]` → **`[factory 7,721 / buying_house 1,434 / unknown 1,031]`** (-182 unknowns); residual register-tagged-but-unknown rows: **0**. **F4b — OEKO-TEX profile enrichment (scraper + migration 0018)**: per the 21 May Pillar-3 NULL-expiry follow-up, OEKO records carried only 5 list-payload keys and no contact / address / expiry data despite the Buying Guide exposing a per-customer profile page. Discovery probe found profile URLs are signed with a time-limited key (`Profile key has expired` if reused later), so post-hoc enrichment of the 2,576 existing OEKO records from the stored URLs is impossible — profile fetch must happen inline during the search pass. `etl/scrapers/oeko_tex.py::OekoTexScraper.fetch` now GETs each freshly-issued profile URL (0.4s polite delay), parses the public contact block (address / phone / email / website) via the `Customer Profile` text marker + label-walk, and writes four new payload keys (`oeko_profile_address`, `oeko_profile_phone`, `oeko_profile_email`, `oeko_profile_website`) plus populates `ScrapedRecord.address_raw` / `email` / `phone_raw` / `website` so the existing `_enrich_supplier` path lifts them into `suppliers.email_primary` / `phones` / `website` / `address_raw` via COALESCE (never overwrites non-null). Migration 0018 adds an OEKO branch to `v_supplier_addresses_direct` (`kind='factory'` reading `fields->>'oeko_profile_address'` with phone/email attached). Smoke-tested end-to-end on VPS (3 records, `STANDARD 100` page 1): all 3 emitted parsed address + email; 2/3 also had phone; 2/3 had website. Docker image rebuilt and pushed (`sourcebd-etl:latest`). The full re-scrape (≈2,576 records × 0.4s ≈ 30 min plus search-page time) is **not started in this session** — it's a long-running production write that needs explicit kick-off authorisation per ops discipline (tmux session on VPS, single `etl run oeko_tex`). Audit script + helper probes (`ops/_moat_deep_audit.py`, `ops/_oeko_*.py`, `ops/_entity_type_backfill.py`) kept as gitignored ops scripts.

21 May 2026 — **Moat audit P3 + P4 closed**. Query rewritten to `select "list", count(*) from public.sanctions_list_entries group by "list" order by "list"`. 21 May 2026 — **Moat audit P2 fix: SBI Pillar-3 stops counting OEKO-TEX certs with NULL `expires_on` as active**. Read-only probe (`ops/_oeko_expiry_probe.py` + `ops/_cert_expiry_dist.py` on VPS) found the inflation source: `public.certifications.expires_on` distribution per `kind` is **`oeko_tex` 2,856 rows / 0 with expiry / 2,856 NULL**, while `wrap` 434/434, `gots` 884/884, `sa8000` 7/7 all carry expiry dates. The OEKO-TEX Buying-Guide list endpoint (`etl/scrapers/oeko_tex.py`) writes only 5 fields keys (`country`, `oeko_standard`, `oeko_standard_label`, `oeko_customer_id`, `oeko_profile_url`) — the list payload has no dates at all, so the gap cannot be back-filled from existing raw rows. The actual moat lie was in the **SBI calculator**: `etl/scoring/sbi.py::compute_pillar3_certs` used `if expires_on is not None and expires_on < today: continue`, which treated NULL as "active forever" and credited every OEKO-TEX cert +7 Pillar-3 points without evidence. **Fix**: condition flipped to `if expires_on is None or expires_on < today: continue` — NULL `expires_on` is now treated as **unknown**, not active. No claim without evidence. Tests in `etl/tests/test_sbi.py` updated for the new semantic (3 tests that previously asserted "NULL counts as active" rewritten to use future dates; new `test_pillar3_null_expiry_not_active` added). Logic verified end-to-end on VPS via `ops/_sbi_verify.py` (7/7 cases pass). Full rescore (`docker compose run --rm etl sbi --force`) processed 10,186 / 10,186 suppliers. **Result**: Pillar-3 non-zero count **2,722 → 1,007** (−1,715 suppliers no longer claim active certs they cannot prove); total-SBI avg **10.49 → 8.81**, median **7 → 6**, max **61 → 54**; ≥30 bucket **66 → 13**; ≥50 bucket **1 → 0**. SBI remains admin-only per the 2026-05-20 receipts doctrine — no buyer-facing surface changes. Follow-up parked: future spec to extend `etl/scrapers/oeko_tex.py` to drill each `oeko_profile_url` for per-customer validity dates from the OEKO portal; until that ships, OEKO-TEX contributes 0 Pillar-3 points by design.

21 May 2026 — **Moat audit P1 fix: BGAPMEA address-view bug fixed (migration 0016)**. Read-only audit (`ops/_moat_audit.py` + `ops/_moat_followup.py` on VPS) found **2,722 / 10,186 suppliers (26.7%) had no row in `v_supplier_addresses`** despite 10,118 being published. Probe of the BGAPMEA-only missing-address cohort (`ops/_addr_payload_probe.py`) showed all 1,096 BGAPMEA `source_records` carry the address verbatim — under keys `bgapmea_company_address` (100%) + `bgapmea_factory_address` (99.6%) — while the view recreated by migration 0015 only reads `fields->>'address'` / `fields->>'raw_address'` (keys the BGAPMEA scraper never writes). Pure view-bug, not a missing-data problem. **Migration 0016** (`supabase/migrations/0016_bgapmea_address_branches.sql`) recreates `v_supplier_addresses_direct` with two additional BGAPMEA branches (`factory` ← `bgapmea_factory_address`, `registered` ← `bgapmea_company_address`) and attaches `bgapmea_phone` + `bgapmea_email_raw` to the `registered` row only (BGAPMEA payload has a single contact pair per company, not per-location split — attaching to both rows would imply a distinction the source does not document). No writes to base tables. View-only, additive over 0015; reversibility = re-apply 0015. Applied on VPS via stdin pipe into the etl container's psycopg (the existing `ops/_apply_one.py` pattern, kept inline for this 11 KB file). **Result**: `suppliers_with_address` 7,464 → **8,419** (+955, matches the BGAPMEA gap exactly); 22 additional rows surfaced as RSC-sibling-inherited BGAPMEA addresses (11 factory + 11 registered) via the existing 0014 inheritance leg. Total BGAPMEA address rows: 1,233 factory + 1,237 registered + 22 inherited = 2,492. Spot-check of 5 random rows confirmed real Bangladesh addresses + working phones + valid emails — zero fabricated values. The RSC-only 677 missing-address cohort was **deliberately not touched**: probe showed `rsc_location` is uniformly NULL in payload (741/741) and no `address`/`raw_address` keys exist — the Accord JSON API simply does not publish factory addresses for those rows; surfacing anything there would violate the no-guesswork rule. Audit-only debugging task (per AGENTS.md hard rule 3), not a new spec.

21 May 2026 — **Spec 16 (BTMA Spinning-Mills register) shipped end-to-end on VPS — 528 source_records / 422 distinct BTMA suppliers (cross-merge 25.0%) + ETL framework Pass-0 self-source idempotency landed in `etl/core/upsert.py`**. Curated JSON extraction of the BTMA spinning-mill PDF (528 rows across 4 sections: General Member 371 / Associate Spinning 65 / Mills Under Suspension 79 / Suspension+Implementation 13) ingested via new `etl/scrapers/btma_spinning.py` (`BtmaSpinningScraper(BaseScraper)`, `source_code='BTMA'`, `code='btma_spinning'`, source_ref scheme `f"spinning-{section_slug}-{sl_no}"`). Migration `0015_btma_address_branches.sql` (view-only extension) exposes BTMA `factory_address` + `mailing_address` payload keys as `address_kind in ('factory','mailing')`, mirroring the BGMEA/BKMEA pattern. **Two cleanup passes were required to converge to 528 exact** because the 4-pass dedup engine's Pass-3 (phone-overlap) is non-deterministic for sibling mills that share a head-office switchboard: a single BGMEA/BKMEA supplier acts as a phone magnet that pulls in multiple BTMA siblings, then on re-run the cleanup-created suppliers shift the phone-overlap graph and flip *different* mis-merges to *different* (still wrong) siblings. Pass-1 cleanup (`ops/spec16_cleanup_dup_phone_matches.py`) created 5 correct mill suppliers (Israq Rotor, Matam Spinning, Nassa Spinning, NZ Spinning, Purbani Yarn Dyeing) by detaching them from their phone-magnet parents. The first idempotency check then surfaced 4 *new* dups (Arif Knitspin, Echo Cotton, Nice Spun, Noman Spinning) — Pass-2 cleanup (`ops/spec16_cleanup_dup_phone_matches_pass2.py`) created those as standalone suppliers. **Root cause fix (Path B — framework-level)**: `etl/core/upsert.py::_find_existing` now runs a **Pass-0 self-source idempotency lookup** before slug/email/phone/fuzzy — given `(source_code, source_ref)`, it queries `source_records` for an existing `supplier_id` and returns it directly. This guarantees that any subsequent re-ingest of the same `(source_id, source_ref)` always resolves to the same `supplier_id`, bypassing Pass-3's non-determinism entirely. Benefits every scraper, not just BTMA. Post-Path-B re-run on VPS (tmux `btma_idem`): all 528 rows emitted `supplier.enriched` (zero `supplier.created`), `source_records` stayed at 528 exact, 0 duplicate source_refs, BTMA-tagged suppliers stable at 422, factory addresses 522, mailing addresses 527 — acceptance criterion (d) idempotency PASSED. SBI backfill on the affected supplier set: seen 10,186 / computed 10,186 / **upserted 422** / skipped_hash 9,764 (every newly-BTMA-tagged supplier picked up +2 Pillar 1 via `_REGISTER_TAGS`, capped at 25). Cross-merge = 422 BTMA-tagged suppliers / (422 BTMA + 1,265 expected fresh) ≈ 25% attachment onto existing register-source rows (BGMEA/BKMEA/EPB/RSC) — above the ≥10% acceptance bar. View extension applied on VPS via `ops/_apply_one.py`. Acceptance criteria (a)–(f) all green.

21 May 2026 — **Phase-0 cleanup: RSC sibling-factory address inheritance shipped (migration 0014)**. Diagnosis (`ops/_contact_coverage.py` + `etl/logs/_rsc_addr_diag2.py` + `etl/logs/_addr_recovery_audit.py` on VPS) found **789 / 9,932 suppliers (7.9%) had no address in `v_supplier_addresses` AND no `suppliers.address_raw`**, of which **825 are RSC factories whose Accord JSON payload simply omits every address key** (`rsc_location` present-but-null in 825/825, raw blob has zero address keys, `has_city=16`, `has_district=0`, `has_lat=0`). Of the 789, ≈171 are RSC sibling/extension records ("X (Extension)", "X (Expansion Building)", "X (New Building)", "X Unit-N", "X - N") whose parent factory **already exists in our DB with a verified Tier-1/2 address** — extension and parent are the same physical compound under RSC's own data model. New migration `supabase/migrations/0014_rsc_inherited_addresses.sql`: (a) helper SQL fn `public.rsc_extension_base_name(p_name text)` strips the seven recognised sibling-suffix patterns (Extension / Expansion ± Building[s] / New Building / New Location / Unit-N / trailing " - N", plus a `(Previously ...)` annotation pre-pass) and returns the parent name or NULL; (b) view recreated as `v_supplier_addresses_direct` (the existing source-records-driven UNION) ∪ `v_supplier_addresses` = direct ∪ inherited(direct) so an extension supplier inherits its parent's address row with `address_kind = <kind> || '_inherited'`, `source_ref = <parent_ref> || '#inherited:<parent_slug>'`, and the parent's original `source_code/source_tier` preserved (no fabricated source). View-only fix — no writes to `suppliers.address_raw`, no new `source_records` rows, no new `sources` row; provenance lives inside the view and is reversible by dropping `v_supplier_addresses_direct`. Applied on VPS via `ops/apply_sql.py` (`docker compose run --rm --entrypoint python -e PYTHONPATH=/app etl etl/logs/_apply_sql.py etl/logs/0014_rsc_inherited_addresses.sql`). Re-run of `_contact_coverage.py`: **missing-address suppliers 789 → 605 (-184)**; recovered rows still lack `contact_name` / `phones` so they remain in the "missing ANY contact field" bucket, but now show a verified parent-factory address in the profile. 605 residual = 596 RSC-only + 15 WRAP-only suppliers with no name-matchable parent in DB; these are genuine unknowns at every source we ingest and stay in the verification queue. Spec 08b GOTS remains the active spec; this was a Phase-0 cleanup ops task, not a new spec.

20 May 2026 — **SBI positioning decision shipped (α now, β later, γ never) — SBI demoted to internal-only sort signal; public surfaces are receipts-first**. Triggered by the founder's observation that Foursource, Alibaba, Globalsources, Made-in-China, Indiamart, ThomasNet, Maker's Row, and every other marketplace-class B2B sourcing platform deliberately does **not** publish a proprietary supplier score — they show third-party-issued badges / tier status / cert receipts and let buyers do their own diligence. The platforms that do publish numeric ratings (EcoVadis, Higg/Worldly, MSCI ESG, ISS, D&B) are **paid raters** whose business model is selling the rating to the rated entity, not marketplaces. The two roles are economically incompatible: marketplaces borrow trust from named authorities; raters own a methodology committee + appeals process + capital + regulatory posture (NRSRO-equivalent). SourceBD has neither, and publishing a numeric score creates platform liability under CSDDD / UFLPA / LkSG (buyer diligence duties are non-delegable; a published score can be relied on then sued over). **Decision (α now, β later, γ never)**: (α) keep computing SBI; use it server-side for default sort + admin alerting + change-detection; **never render it as a number in any buyer / supplier / marketing / public surface**. RLS on `public.sbi_scores` restricted to `role='admin'`. Buyer-facing UI shows **receipts** — cert badges (issuer + cert ID + expiry + document_url), register links (BGMEA / BKMEA / EPB / RSC reg numbers), RSC remediation %, brand-disclosure attributions, completeness badge, source-pill provenance per field. (β) Phase 1+ may add a **tier label** (Bronze / Silver / Gold / Platinum) derived from counts of independent Tier 1–3 sources — categorical, not numeric — design pending real buyer-usage evidence; not committed. (γ) **Never** become a paid rater; do not pursue an EcoVadis-style standalone rating product. The Score Ring brand visual is retained but renamed **Receipts Ring**; v1 payload is the integer source-count (saturated at 5+) with the noun "sources" — the SBI integer, pillar values, A/B/C/D grade letters, and the strings "SBI" / "Score" / "Rating" are forbidden in the centre label. Context updated in 6 files: `project-overview.md` (pitch / core flow / features-in-scope / success criteria reframed receipts-first), `architecture.md` (new invariant: `sbi_scores` is admin-only and never serialised to non-admin clients; new "NOT in the architecture" entry: marketplace-vs-rater boundary), `ai-workflow-rules.md` (new hard prohibition: never render SBI numeric in user-facing surface; sort-by-SBI is allowed because order is not a number), `code-standards.md` (security section: RLS deny + PR-review gate on any non-admin select of `sbi_scores.total`), `ui-context.md` (Score Ring → Receipts Ring with source-count payload; "SBI Verified" tag renamed "Source-verified · N sources"; data sensitivity section bans SBI render; marketing section "SBI methodology" → "How we verify methodology"), `phases.md` (F1 base components updated; B1 filter "SBI grade" → "source-count tier"; B1 explicit server-side-sort-only allowance; S2 supplier dashboard explicitly omits SBI per buyer/supplier parity; M1 landing page methodology copy revised; cross-cutting principle #2 revised; new cross-cutting principle #7 "Receipts, not opinions"). `logos.lock.md` unchanged — it governs external-mark rendering, not SourceBD-own metrics. **Zero code changes** — `etl/scoring/sbi.py`, `etl/scoring/runner.py`, `public.sbi_scores` schema, and the `etl sbi` CLI all stay as-is; the calculator still backfills nightly (Phase 0 Spec 11 contract preserved). RLS tightening + admin-only API exposure shipped at the first Phase-1 spec that touches `public.sbi_scores` (no Phase-0 spec needs it; the table is currently only written by `etl sbi`, never read by any user-facing surface that exists yet).

19 May 2026 — **Spec 08e (SA8000 certification harvester) shipped on VPS — 7 / 7 SA8000 BD certificates landed across 6 distinct suppliers (4 cross-merged onto existing register-source rows, 2 net new from SA8000-only)**. New `etl/scrapers/sa8000.py` walks the WordPress plugin `cwp-sa8000-search` on `sa-intl.org`: one list call `GET admin-ajax.php?action=cwp_get_results&Country=Bangladesh&...` returns a 7-row `<tr class="pages">` HTML table, then per-row `POST admin-ajax.php { action=get_popup_content, id=<data-pop> }` returns a JSON envelope whose `data` field is a `<ul><li><b>Label:</b> value</li>…</ul>` fragment with 10 detail fields (incl. `Initial / Latest Certification Date`, `Expiration Date`, optional `Withdrawal Date`, `Description of Operations`, full `Address`). Final DB state: **7 source_records, 7 certifications, 6 distinct suppliers (SQ Celsius Limited has 2 SA8000 certs), 4/6 (67%) cross-merge attachment to existing register-source rows** (Lenny Apparels, Yes Apparel-Tech, Shabab Fabrics, SQ Celsius all matched existing suppliers; Dutch-Bangla Pack Ltd + SQ Celsius were the 2 net new). All 7 certs issued by Bureau Veritas Certification. Migration `0013_seed_sa8000_source.sql` (`public.sources` row at `tier3_cert`) + `_TIER_MAP['SA8000']='tier3_cert'` were the only Phase-0 wiring gaps closed — `cert_kind` enum already had `'sa8000'` and `_CERT_POINTS['sa8000']=5` already in `etl/scoring/sbi.py`. 11/11 unit tests pass (`etl/tests/test_sa8000.py` — list HTML parser, popup UL parser, certificate-no slug, `_extract_city`, ISO-date parser). **Cloudflare on `sa-intl.org` blocks Python TLS fingerprints (httpx → 403 regardless of headers); fetch path uses Playwright's `BrowserContext.request` for real Chromium TLS** — same pattern as `etl/scrapers/brand_disclosures.py`, zero new tools. Second-run idempotency confirmed (seen 7, upserted 7, 0 new source/cert rows). SBI backfill on the affected supplier set: seen 9,932 / computed 9,932 / **upserted 6** / skipped_hash 9,926 (Pillar 3 lifted +5 for each SA8000-attached supplier).

19 May 2026 — **Spec 08b (GOTS certification harvester) shipped on VPS** — **884 BD GOTS certificates landed across 856 distinct suppliers (28 cross-merged onto existing register-source rows; 170 net new from GOTS-only)**. New `etl/scrapers/gots.py` paginates the public GOTS DB (`https://www.global-trace-base.org/website-api/v2/certified-suppliers?country=BD&limit=100&offset=N`, envelope `{items, limit, offset, total}`) and per-row hits the detail endpoint to enrich with `gtb_license_number`, address, `certification_body`, `certificate_valid_until`, `scope_certificate_ref` (no auth, no token, no Playwright — clean JSON). `_write_certification` inserts into `public.certifications` with `kind='gots'`, `certificate_no=<gtb_license_number>` (fall-back `f"gots-{system_id}"`), `issuer=certification_body`, `expires_on=parse(certificate_valid_until)`, `scope="Operations: X | Products: Y"`, `document_url` joined to the API host for `scope_certificate_ref`. **Zero schema migration** — `cert_kind` enum already had `'gots'`, `_TIER_MAP` already mapped `GOTS → tier3_cert`, `public.sources` already seeded GOTS. 12/12 unit tests pass (`etl/tests/test_gots.py` — pure helpers: address compose, scope compose, expires parse, cert-no fallback, scope-url absolute). Registered in `etl/cli.py` (`etl run gots`). Run result on VPS: `gots_certs=884 / gots_source_records=884 / gots_suppliers=856`. SBI backfill on the affected supplier set: `seen=9930 / computed=9930 / upserted=1068 / skipped_hash=8862` (Pillar 3 lifted +5 per newly-certed supplier).

19 May 2026 — **Spec 10 (Cross-source dedup + merge engine refinement, data-engine portion) shipped on VPS**. Migration `0011_parent_groups.sql` adds `public.suppliers.parent_group_name TEXT` + partial b-tree index `idx_suppliers_parent_group_name WHERE parent_group_name IS NOT NULL` + extends `queue_type` enum with `group_parent_review`. Three new ops modules + one tiny helper land: `ops/spec10_seeds.py` (curated PARENT_GROUP_SEEDS for 6 known multi-factory groups: Ha-Meem `(^|\s)ha[\s-]?meem(\s|$)`, NASSA `(^|\s)nassa(\s|$)`, Mondol `^mondol(\s|$)`, Biswas `^biswas(\s|$)`, Standard `^standard\s+(stitches|group|knit|knitwear|knitwears)(\s|$)`, Palmal `(^|\s)palmal(\s|$)`; `STOP_TOKENS` ~60 generic industry words; `first_sig_token(norm)` returns the first len≥4 token not in the stoplist), `ops/spec10_requeue_fuzzy.py` (regenerates `fuzzy_match_review` from the current 8,773-row register pool against 987 cert-only suppliers using rapidfuzz dual-scorer `token_sort_ratio` + `token_set_ratio` ≥85; idempotency key `(supplier_a_id, source_data->>'target_supplier_id')`; auto-merge band ≥92 deliberately disabled per Spec 08 (e) decision after ~50% false-positive rate on short-prefix names), `ops/spec10_parent_groups.py` (PASS A — UPDATE `parent_group_name=seed.name` WHERE column is null for each seed with ≥3 matches, refuses to overwrite conflicts; PASS B — bucket all suppliers by `first_sig_token`, for clusters ≥3 not covered by a seed insert a single `group_parent_review` queue row per cluster_token), `ops/apply_sql.py` (psycopg autocommit + `SET statement_timeout=0; SET lock_timeout='5min'` to override the Supabase pooler's 58s ceiling for DDL). **Result on VPS**: parent_group_name populated for **41 suppliers** across 5 of 6 seed groups (NASSA 12, Mondol 10, Biswas 8, Standard 7, Ha-Meem 4 — Palmal had matched=2 < CLUSTER_MIN_SIZE=3 so deliberately skipped); 700 algorithmic clusters surfaced into `group_parent_review` for admin review; `fuzzy_match_review` regenerated to **264 open rows** (was 258 pre-spec — net +6 from new cert-only suppliers since Spec 08); both `requeue` and `parent_groups` confirmed fully idempotent on second run (`new_inserted=0`, `inserted=0`, `skipped_existing=700`); normalize regression 16/16 ✅. Admin UI for the new queue type is deferred to Phase 4 (Specs A3/A4); data-engine portion of Spec 10 is now complete.

19 May 2026 — **Spec 13 (RSC document mirror, Phase 0.5) shipped end-to-end on VPS** — **7,049 / 7,055 documents mirrored from `accord2.fairfactories.org` to Bunny CDN across 1,571 distinct factories (99.92% mirror success, 6 origin 404s skipped, 0 mirror failures, 7.1 GB total)**. New table `public.compliance_documents (supplier_id, doc_type, source, original_url, mirror_url, sha256, file_size, content_type, fetched_at)` with RLS policy `pol_compliance_docs_pub_read` (read-allowed only when the parent supplier is `is_published=true`) and idempotency key `unique (supplier_id, doc_type, sha256)`. New scraper `etl/scrapers/rsc_documents.py` reads URLs from the already-cached `public.rsc_remediation.{fire,structural,electrical,boiler}_inspection_url + cap_url` columns (populated by `etl/scrapers/rsc.py` from the Accord JSON API) — no second crawl of `rsc-bd.org`; one synchronous pass downloads → sha256 → infers ext (URL ext → response `Content-Type` → `bin` fallback) → HEAD-probes Bunny → uploads if missing → upserts the provenance row. Mirror path scheme `rsc-docs/<supplier_slug>/<doc_type>-<fetched_date>.<ext>`. Final per-type distribution in DB: boiler 859 (831 MB), cap 1,571 (197 MB), electrical 1,546 (2.05 GB), fire 1,538 (1.95 GB), structural 1,535 (2.09 GB). RSC SSL chain helper extracted to `etl/core/ssl_rsc.py::build_rsc_ssl_context` and reused by `rsc_reports.py` (Spec 15) — dedup per Hard Rule #4 (no new tools). Migration `0010_compliance_documents.sql` applied directly via `ops/_spec13_apply.sh` because `etl migrate` re-applies every SQL file from scratch and trips on the `entity_type` enum already existing from migration 0001 — a per-migration apply pattern is required until we add a version-tracking table (logged as known limitation, not in scope for this spec). Bunny Storage HEAD on missing objects returns 401 (not 404) — `bunny.exists()` correctly treats anything other than 200 as missing, so the dry-run + bulk runs proceeded to upload all 7,049 documents on first pass with zero spurious skips. Hot-link fallback (mirror_url=None) implemented but never fired — every download that succeeded also uploaded successfully. Inngest weekly cron deferred per spec (code-only Phase 0.5).

19 May 2026 — **Spec 15 (RSC industry benchmarks) shipped end-to-end on VPS — 33 / 40 monthly reports parsed, 4 / 4 program-area dashboards snapshotted, 298 metric rows in `rsc_industry_metrics` (294 monthly + 4 updates-page)**. Two infrastructure fixes shipped this session on top of the previously-committed Spec 15 code: (a) the RSC origin server (`rsc-bd.org`) misconfigures its TLS chain — it serves only the leaf cert and omits the Sectigo intermediate (`SectigoPublicServerAuthenticationCADVR36`), so default `verify=True` blows up with `unable to get local issuer certificate`. Worked around purely client-side without adding a tool: `_build_rsc_ssl_context()` fetches the intermediate from the AIA URL embedded in the leaf (`http://crt.sectigo.com/SectigoPublicServerAuthenticationCADVR36.crt`), converts the DER blob to PEM via stdlib `ssl.DER_cert_to_PEM_cert`, and `ctx.load_verify_locations(cadata=pem)`. On failure the code falls back to the default context and logs `warn` — no silent `verify=False`. (b) The `<a href>` URLs on `/reports/` are root-relative (`/wp-content/uploads/...`) so the raw href would 404 against the parsed `https://` base; fixed with `urljoin(REPORTS_INDEX_URL, href)`. (c) `rsc_reports.py::_NARRATIVE_PATTERNS` expanded from 5 → 19 regexes to cover the 2025 prose format (the 2025 monthlies dropped tabular summaries and now report metrics inline, e.g. "Covered factory account number: 1,881", "Total number of inspections: 517 in November", "341 factories are in different stages of the escalation process, with 246 in Stage 1 and 95 in Stage 2", "Number of factories having accepted (D)EA: 1386", "Stage-3 Escalation was issued to 3 factories", "10 reported incidents occurred at the RSC-covered factory"). Multi-group patterns handled via a `_multi_escalation_overview` sentinel key in the parse loop. The generic `"factories covered"` fallback was moved to the END of the regex list so the precise 2025 `"Covered factory account number"` capture wins when present. Added 3× exponential-backoff retry in `download_pdf` for `httpx.RemoteProtocolError / ReadError / ConnectError` (RSC's nginx occasionally `Server disconnected without sending a response`). (d) `rsc_updates.py` refactored: the four `/updates/` program-area modals no longer contain `<table>` rows — RSC switched to publishing the monthly numbers as JPG dashboards (`RPD-Data-Apr-2026.jpg`, `Boiler-Safety-Data-Apr-2026.jpg`, `OSH-Training-Data-APR-2026.jpg`, `OSH-Complain-Data-APR-2026.jpg`). Per Hard Rule #4 (no new tools) we don't OCR. Instead the scraper now snapshots the image URL per (section × month) as a provenance row in `rsc_industry_metrics` with `metric_key='dashboard_snapshot_image_url'`, `value_num=1`, `unit='count'`, `source='rsc_updates_page'`, `raw_label=<img src>`, `source_url=<img src>`. Re-runs are idempotent on the existing `(report_month, scope, metric_key, source)` UNIQUE. Discovery selector switched to per-widget iteration (`.elementor-widget-premium-addon-modal-box` → click `.premium-modal-trigger-container` → read `.premium-modal-box-modal-body` → close via `.premium-modal-box-modal-close`) so each modal is fully closed before the next opens (the previous text-locator approach caused pointer-event intercepts when two modals overlapped). Run result on VPS: `rsc_reports` seen 55 / upserted 33 / skipped 22 (the 22 are pre-July-2023 PDFs whose text extraction returns empty — they're a 2022-2023 image-only report era; cataloged as `parse_status='failed'` with `parse.empty` event for future OCR work); `rsc_updates` seen 4 / upserted 4 / skipped 0. **`rsc_industry_metrics` final: 298 rows** (was 19), spanning scopes `coverage 65`, `escalation 67`, `fads 10`, `incidents 4`, `inspection_remediation 92`, `structural 57`, plus 4 dashboard-snapshot rows under `boiler_safety / inspection_remediation / osh_complaints / osh_training`. Phase 0.5 Spec 15 ✅ shipped. `etl content monthly_digest <YYYY-MM|latest>` CLI unchanged and ready for blog-post generation as soon as Phase 1 templates land.

19 May 2026 — **Marks & Spencer bespoke OSH-embed scraper (Spec 09 follow-up #4) — SHIPPED, `BRAND_MS` activated under the narrow OSH extension; Primark (Spec 09 follow-up #5) closed `reserved`**. Per Option A architectural decision (this session): the per-factory authenticity rule is extended to grant a `BRAND_*` pill when (a) the brand is the named Open Supply Hub contributor AND (b) the OSH facility list is officially embedded on the brand's own corporate domain. Verified for M&S: contributor #10061 with 3 published lists (incl. "Fashion, Home and Beauty Tier 1 Facility List - February 2026", 1,853 global facilities) embedded at `corporate.marksandspencer.com/sustainability/interactive-supplier-map`. New `BrandMsScraper` + `_ms_verify_embed_and_fetch` in `etl/scrapers/brand_disclosures.py`: Playwright Chromium loads the M&S corporate page, asserts at least one outbound XHR to `opensupplyhub.org/api/*?contributors=10061` (runtime embed verification — fails fast if the on-domain embed is removed), then captures the per-contributor `x-oar-client-key` embed token from the live iframe request headers and replays it (with matching `Referer: https://opensupplyhub.org/facilities?contributors=10061&sort_by=name_asc&embed=1`) to paginate `/api/facilities/?contributors=10061&countries=BD&pageSize=50&embed=1&sort_by=name_asc` via `ctx.request.get`. Without the captured `x-oar-client-key` the OSH API returns HTTP 401; this was diagnosed via `ops/_spec09_osh_auth_probe.py` which intercepted every OSH XHR fired by the live M&S page and dumped headers + bodies (confirmed the static client key `bbd21248d…` for contributor 10061). Source-ref scheme is `ms-osh-<oar_id>` (stable). Snapshot of the raw OSH GeoJSON BD slice mirrored to `https://sourcebd-docs.b-cdn.net/brand-disclosures/brand_ms/<date>.json`. Run result on VPS (`docker compose run --rm etl run brand_ms`): **seen 68 / upserted 68 / skipped 0 / enqueued_for_review 18** (the 18 are M&S-only BD facilities with no Tier 1–3 corroboration yet, sent to `verification_queue.brand_disclosure_match_review` per the authenticity rule). `BRAND_MS` flipped `reserved` → `active` in `context/logos.lock.md`; Tier-4 header in `logos.lock.md` updated with the narrow OSH extension and the runtime-enforcement requirement. **Primark** does not satisfy either limb of the extended rule: prior 4-probe discovery established that `primark.com/.../modern-slavery-act` is narrative MSS only (0 BD tabular rows), `/global-supplier-map` is an Akamai-gated Next.js `__next_error__` 404, and no Primark-owned OSH contributor is identifiable — `BRAND_PRIMARK` stays `reserved` with the discovery summary recorded inline in `logos.lock.md`. **Brand supplier-list disclosures linked: 4 / 6 (H&M + ASOS + Next + M&S)**. Inditex and Primark remain `reserved`; no further brand work queued for Phase 0.

19 May 2026 — **Next plc bespoke per-factory scraper (Spec 09 follow-up #3) — SHIPPED, `BRAND_NEXT` activated**. Three-pass discovery on `nextplc.co.uk` (`ops/_spec09_next_probe{,2}.py` Playwright crawls + `ops/_spec09_next_pdf_probe.py` PDF deep-inspection on VPS) located Next's full Tier 1 + Tier 2 + Tier 3 supplier list publication at `https://www.nextplc.co.uk/corporate-responsibility/our-suppliers`. Probe 1 ruled out `/responsible-sourcing` (timber policy + MSS only) and 9 other 404-only paths; probe 2 confirmed the hub at `/corporate-responsibility/our-suppliers` (status 200, body text "2,402 Code of Practice audits completed in 2024/25 | Tier 1, 2 & 3 | Factory and supplier site lists published") with three current PDF links: `T1 2025.pdf` (388 kB, 13pp, Tier-1 = direct manufacturers), `T2 2025.pdf`, `2026 01 30 Tier 3 - Jan 25 to Dec 25.pdf`. PDF schema (page 1 text): `SUPPLIER NAME | MANUFACTURING SITE NAME | ADDRESS | PRODUCT TYPE | FEMALE EMPLOYEES | MALE EMPLOYEES | TRADE UNION IN FACTORY | FREELY ELECTED WORKERS COMMITTEE` (8 cols), with **country as a section-header row** rather than a column ("Bangladesh" appears alone in one cell, then all subsequent data rows belong to Bangladesh until the next country header). Bespoke `BrandNextScraper` added to `etl/scrapers/brand_disclosures.py` (replaces stale reserved-only stub) with: (a) strict regex `r"/T1[ %]+20\d{2}\.pdf"` that matches T1 only (excludes T2/T3 + the old `TIER 1 PLC LIST AUGUST 2024.pdf` in `Next-PLC-V2/`), (b) override `discover()`-equivalent inside `fetch()` to sort candidates by year-in-filename desc, (c) state-machine `parse_next_t1_pdf` that tracks `current_country` from marker rows (single-cell rows whose value is in `_NEXT_COUNTRY_NAMES`), emits when `current_country == "bangladesh"`, and uses col 1 (`MANUFACTURING SITE NAME`) as `company_name` — col 0 (`SUPPLIER NAME` = Next's UK vendor/importer) is preserved in `payload.next_supplier_vendor`, (d) disclosure date parsed from PDF page-1 text via `r"Produced\s+([A-Za-z]+)\s+(\d{4})"` → August 2025 → `2025-08-01` (filename-year is fallback), (e) Next CDN requires `Referer: https://www.nextplc.co.uk/corporate-responsibility/our-suppliers`, injected via per-scraper override of `_BROWSER_HEADERS`. Run result on VPS (`docker compose run --rm etl run brand_next`): **seen 153 / upserted 153 / skipped 0 / enqueued_for_review 8**; PDF mirrored to `https://sourcebd-docs.b-cdn.net/brand-disclosures/brand_next/2025-08-01.pdf`. The state-machine parser captured the full BD section (153 rows including multi-vendor duplicates of the same factory — e.g. Robintex (Bangladesh) Ltd appears under Bm Fashions UK + Brand Alliance + Cooneen By Design rows) vs. the probe's naive 77-row count which only matched rows containing the literal word "bangladesh". `BRAND_NEXT` flipped `reserved` → `active` in `context/logos.lock.md`. **Brand supplier-list disclosures linked: 3 / 6 (H&M + ASOS + Next)**. Moving sequentially to Primark.

19 May 2026 — **ASOS bespoke per-factory scraper (Spec 09 follow-up #2) — SHIPPED, `BRAND_ASOS` activated**. Four-pass discovery on `asosplc.com` (`ops/_spec09_asos_probe{,2,3}.py` + `ops/_spec09_asos_pdf_dump.py`) located a static, refreshed-quarterly factory list PDF at `https://www.asosplc.com/media/cmzk3m5n/factory-list-april-2026.pdf` (20pp, 631 kB, 7-column tabular schema: `Factory Name | Address Line 1 | Country | Department | Number of Workers | Male Workers | Female Workers`; BD on 15/20 pages). ASOS is also Open Supply Hub contributor #4302 (embedded iframe), cross-corroborating. Bespoke ASOS scraper added to `etl/scrapers/brand_disclosures.py` (`BrandAsosScraper` + `parse_asos_pdf` + `_asos_disclosure_date`): the shared `parse_pdf` could not be reused because the PDF puts the header row only on page 1 — pages 2..N have NO header and `t[0]` is already a data row, so header-row-detection would have silently dropped 19/20 pages. The ASOS-specific parser knows the fixed 7-column schema, treats page-1 row 0 as header (skip) and pages 2..N rows 0..N as data, then filters `Country == 'Bangladesh'`. Disclosure date is parsed from the filename (`factory-list-april-2026.pdf` → `2026-04-01`) rather than `date.today()`. Strict discovery regex `r"factory-list-[a-z]+-\d{4}\.pdf"` keeps MSS / code-of-conduct / policy PDFs on the same landing page from matching. Run result on VPS (`docker compose run --rm --entrypoint python etl -m etl.cli run brand_asos`): **seen 44 / upserted 44 / skipped 0 / enqueued_for_review 6**; PDF mirrored to `https://sourcebd-docs.b-cdn.net/brand-disclosures/brand_asos/2026-04-01.pdf`; 7 net-new suppliers created (`Consumer Knitex Limited (Ckl) – Unit 01`, `DNV - Ananta Apparels`, `Indochine Apparel (Bangladesh) Limited`, `Indochine Apparel Unit 02 (Bangladesh)`, `Seven Colours Washing`, `Southern Services Ltd`, +1) — the other 37 enriched existing Tier 1–3 supplier rows. The 6 enqueued = ASOS-only suppliers with no Tier 1-3 corroboration yet, sent to `verification_queue.brand_disclosure_match_review` per the per-factory authenticity rule. `BRAND_ASOS` flipped `reserved` → `active` in `context/logos.lock.md`. **Brand supplier-list disclosures linked: 2 / 6 (H&M + ASOS)**. Moving sequentially to Next.

19 May 2026 — **Inditex per-factory disclosure probe (Spec 09 follow-up #1) — closed as `reserved`, no policy change**. Two-pass discovery on `inditex.com` (`ops/_spec09_inditex_probe.py` Playwright crawl + `ops/_spec09_inditex_pdf_probe.py` PDF deep-inspection, both executed inside the VPS docker image): (a) `/sustainability` hub enumerates **38 publicly indexed PDFs** (policies, procurement criteria, water/energy, MSS, 2025 Sustainability Report, Workers at the Centre 2023, GRI Index, product safety standards, etc.) — **zero** titled "List of Suppliers / Factory List / Tier 1 Suppliers / Who Makes Our Clothes". (b) The 5 sibling URLs (`/transparency`, `/our-workers`, `/who-makes-our-products`, `/reporting`, `/people-in-our-supply-chain`) all return the same generic SPA shell with only the MSS link in the footer (87 anchors each, 1 interesting). (c) Deep-PDF inspection of the 3 most likely supplier-annex carriers — 2025 Sustainability Report (165pp, 16.9 MB), Workers at the Centre 2023 (84pp, 18 MB), MSS FY24 (15pp, 5.4 MB) — yields **0 keyword hits** for "list of suppliers / factory list / who makes our" across all 264 pages, and **0 tabular per-factory rows**: Bangladesh appears only in narrative paragraphs (11+21+0 pages respectively); the 5 flagged "BD/tier tables" are all sectional program tables (`Action / Description / Results`, `Living Wages`, `Respect`, `Health`), not supplier rows. **Conclusion:** Inditex's own-website disclosure pattern is narrative + policy + country aggregates; factory-level transparency for Inditex exists only via third-party Open Supply Hub. **Decision (Option C):** `BRAND_INDITEX` stays `reserved` in `context/logos.lock.md` with the probe finding logged inline; no policy change to the authenticity rule. If ≥2 of the remaining 4 brands (ASOS / Next / Primark / M&S) also fail their own-website bar, that's evidence-based justification to revisit an Open-Supply-Hub extension to the authenticity rule. Moving sequentially to ASOS.

19 May 2026 — **Spec 09 (Brand supplier-list disclosures miner) shipped — H&M only counts; 5 of 6 brands deferred under the per-factory authenticity rule**. Final state on VPS:
  - ✅ **H&M live**: 188 BD suppliers / 188 upserted / 43 enqueued for `brand_disclosure_match_review`; XLSX mirrored to `https://sourcebd-docs.b-cdn.net/brand-disclosures/brand_hm/<date>.xlsx`. `BRAND_HM` logo registry row activated in `context/logos.lock.md`. H&M's XLSX names individual BD factories — per-factory authenticity satisfied.
  - ⏸ **Inditex / ASOS / Primark / Next — reserved, no per-supplier attribution**. The MSS PDFs were mirrored to Bunny at `sourcebd-docs.b-cdn.net/brand-disclosures/brand_{inditex,asos,primark,next}/2026-05-18.pdf` (5.4 / 0.2 / 7.8 / 1.97 MB), but inspection (`ops/_spec09_pdfprobe.py`) confirms each is a narrative brand-wide compliance document with **zero per-factory rows**. Under the authenticity rule (Tier-4 brand pill requires the brand's own publication to name the specific factory), an MSS PDF cannot back any `supplier_id` and must not render on supplier profiles. The 4 brands are downgraded from "document-mirrored" → `reserved` in `context/logos.lock.md`. No DB rows were materialised against these brands (each scraper emitted `bd_rows=0`), so nothing to roll back; the mirrored PDFs remain in Bunny as brand-level archives only.
  - ⏸ **M&S reserved**: `/sustainability/interactive-supplier-map` serves supplier data only through an ArcGIS-style map-widget API; no static disclosure file. Scraper raises `RuntimeError` and exits cleanly. Same authenticity rule applies — the per-factory cards inside the map widget would satisfy it, but extracting them needs a bespoke map-API client.

  **Authenticity rule established this spec (now hard policy):** a `BRAND_*` source attribution may attach to a supplier row **only when the brand's own publication names that specific factory** (tabular supplier list, interactive-map detail card, or sustainability page mentioning the factory by name). A brand-wide Modern Slavery Statement is not per-factory evidence. Recorded in `context/logos.lock.md` §3 Tier 4 header.

  **Infrastructure built this spec that survives independent of brand coverage**:
  - `BrandDisclosureBase` framework (`etl/scrapers/brand_disclosures.py`): landing-page scrape → BeautifulSoup link extraction with per-brand regex → optional **Playwright Chromium fallback** (`_render_playwright`, headless `--no-sandbox`, locale=en-GB, 1366×900, `domcontentloaded` + `networkidle` + 4 s settle) when plain HTTP returns 0 candidates or 4xx — proven against Primark Cloudflare 403 and ASOS JS-rendered SPA. **Content-Type-based extension inference** (`_infer_ext`) HEAD-probes URLs without a file extension (e.g. `primark.a.bigcontent.io/v1/static/<slug>` serving PDFs) and maps Content-Type → ext.
  - **Bunny CDN async upload helper** (`etl/core/bunny.py`): idempotent `exists(path)` + `upload(path, content, content_type=None)` over the Bunny Storage REST API (`storage.bunnycdn.com/{zone}/{path}` with `AccessKey` header). Public reads at `https://sourcebd-docs.b-cdn.net/{path}`. Path scheme `brand-disclosures/{brand_code.lower()}/{disclosure_date.isoformat()}.{ext}`.
  - **`enforce_publish_tier` savepoint fix** (`etl/core/upsert.py::_maybe_publish`): wraps the trigger-protected UPDATE in `savepoint sp_publish` so a Tier-4-only refusal no longer poisons the surrounding upsert transaction. This was the latent Phase-0 bug surfaced by Spec 09 — first source to insert genuinely new suppliers without any Tier 1-3 backing. Without this fix every Tier-4 new supplier would have failed with `current transaction is aborted, commands ignored until end of transaction block`.
  - Migration `0009_brand_sources.sql` (applied): extends `queue_type` enum with `brand_disclosure_match_review`, seeds 6 `BRAND_*` source rows at `source_tier='tier4_brand'`, adds `brand_disclosures` table (supplier_id, brand_code, source_url, mirror_url, disclosure_date, raw_row jsonb).
  - CLI: 6 new commands `etl run brand_{hm,inditex,asos,primark,ms,next}`.

  **Scope outcome**: H&M ✅ live + visible on profiles (per-factory authenticity satisfied); Inditex/ASOS/Primark/Next/M&S ⏸ reserved — mirrored MSS PDFs are kept as brand-level archives but produce zero supplier attribution. Real Tier-4 work for the 5 reserved brands (bespoke per-brand supplier-list / map-API discovery) is re-queued as a single sharper follow-up under the authenticity rule.

Wired through `etl sbi` CLI command. 23/23 unit tests pass (`etl/tests/test_sbi.py`). Backfilled all **9,760 / 9,760 suppliers (100%)** on the VPS via `docker compose run --rm --entrypoint python etl -m etl.cli sbi` with `etl/` bind-mounted; second-run idempotency confirmed (seen 9,760, upserted 0, skipped_hash 9,760). Live distribution: avg total 10.24, median 7, max 56, buckets {0:155, 1–24:8702, 25–49:878, 50–74:25, 75+:0}. The low ceiling is expected for the current data state — Pillar 3 only counts cert kinds we actually ingest (WRAP+OEKO-TEX so far; BSCI/SEDEX/GOTS/ISO will land later), Pillar 2 needs an RSC row (8,143 suppliers have none → 0 on Pillar 2), and Pillar 1 caps at 25 even for register-tagged factories. Sanctioned-zero count = 0 (consistent with 0 active sanctions matches). **Phase 0 acceptance gate now passes all four exit criteria** (suppliers 9,760 ✅, RSC matched 1,617 ✅, sanctions screening 100% ✅, SBI scored 100% ✅) — Phase 0 is COMPLETE; phase-gate to Phase 0.5 (Specs 13–15) is now open.

19 May 2026 — **Spec 08 dedup follow-on shipped (normalizer overhaul + slug-collision retro merge backfill)**. Root cause of the 67-row WRAP-only RMG residual was diagnosed in `etl/core/normalize.py`: `_LEGAL_SUFFIX_RE` was stripping industry words ("Fashion", "Knit", "Wear", …) collapsing many real names to a 1–2 token stem and triggering bogus matches, while genuine variants weren't normalising the same way (typo "Lts", possessive `'s`, dotted initials "J.M.", "knit wear" vs "knitwear", "wears" vs "wear"). Normalizer rewritten so it now strips ONLY true legal forms (`ltd|lts|limited|pvt|private|co|company|corp|corporation|inc|incorporated|llc|llp`), applies a possessive-strip pass, an abbreviation/variant pass (`knit ?wear[s]? → knitwear`, `sweaters → sweater`, `wears → wear`, `inds|ind|indus|industry → industries`, `mfg|manf → manufacturing`, `bros → brothers`, `int|intl → international`, `fabs → fabrics`, `gmts|gar → garments`), then a `_collapse_initials` pass that joins runs of ≥2 single-letter tokens (so "j m fabrics" ≡ "jm fabrics"). Backed by 16/16 regression cases in `ops/_normalize_regression.py` (incl. Goumati Knit Wears↔GOUMATI KNITWEARS, JM↔J.M. Fabrics, Lyric Lts↔Lyric Ltd, "M&S"↔"M and S", Meghna≠Mega, Univogue Unit-1≠Unit-III). Retro-applied via `ops/_renorm_backfill.py` (per-group autocommit transactions, idempotent) executed from the VPS docker container after the local pooler route timed out at the Postgres handshake — TCP open but no startup-message reply, while the VPS reached the same pooler fine. Result: **9,915 → 9,760 suppliers** (−155 losers across 149 merge groups), `source_records` preserved at 15,750, `certifications` preserved at 3,290, 0 scratch slugs, 0 sanctions matches retained. WRAP-only RMG residual collapsed **67 → 30** at the deterministic-norm level; the remaining 30 have no exact-norm peer in BGMEA/BKMEA/etc. and fall to the Spec 10 fuzzy/manual verification queue. Source-record reassign was clean — zero `(supplier_id, source_id, source_ref)` UNIQUE conflicts.

18 May 2026 — **Spec 08 shipped (WRAP + OEKO-TEX certification harvester)**. Two new Tier-3 scrapers wired through the existing `BaseScraper` + `_TIER_MAP` (`tier3_cert`). Final counts on Supabase: **WRAP 423 suppliers / 435 certs**, **OEKO-TEX 2,453 suppliers / 2,856 certs**. Cross-source merge during live ingest already attached **1,440 / 2,453 OEKO-TEX (59%)** and **379 / 423 WRAP (90%)** suppliers onto register-source rows (BGMEA / BKMEA / RSC / EPB / BTMA / BGAPMEA) via the 4-pass dedup engine — confirmed by `ops/_spec08_show_crossmerged.py` (sample: ABONI KNITWEAR, ACTIVE COMPOSITE MILLS, AL ITTEFAQ TEXTILES, Aman Knittings, BENCHMARK APPARELS all carry 5–6 sources including BGMEA + EPB + BKMEA + RSC + WRAP/OEKO-TEX). Retroactive merge pass (`ops/spec08_retro_merge.py`) on the 1,037 cert-only residual: dual-scorer (`token_sort_ratio` + `token_set_ratio`) ≥92 dry-run produced 24 candidates but ~50% were false positives (short prefix + 3-4 generic industry tokens like "Packaging Industries", "Knit Composite Ltd" inflating scores), so auto-merge was dropped and all 258 candidates were enqueued into `verification_queue` as `fuzzy_match_review` for admin review (Spec §9). Zero retroactive merges applied. WRAP-only residual = 44 suppliers (mix of legit non-RMG: footwear / washing / trims, plus sub-unit naming and typos like "Lts" / "Wear's" awaiting normalize tweak).

Coverage analysis: only 928 / 8,879 suppliers (~10%) carry an http(s) `website` and only 699 distinct hosts; even at 100% extraction success that produces a column populated for <8% of the DB, which weakens rather than strengthens the data moat. Better to wait for the Phase-1 supplier-portal claim flow (claimed factories self-declare MOQ/lead-time directly) than to publish a sparse, low-confidence column now. ## Recently shipped (Phase 5 — Marketing)

- ✅ **Spec M4 — Public marketing SEO baseline** shipped 3 Jun 2026. Fourth Phase-5 spec per `context/phases.md`. Two new App-Router metadata-convention routes: `app/sitemap.ts` (`MetadataRoute.Sitemap`, `revalidate=3600`) emits 5 hard-coded static URLs (`/`, `/pricing`, `/legal/trademarks`, `/discover`, `/compliance`) + 5 `/compliance/{slug}` from `COMPLIANCE_PAGES` + paged `is_published=true` supplier slugs from the anon Supabase server client (1000-row pages, ordered by slug, total count logged as `[m4] sitemap suppliers: N`), entire SELECT wrapped in try/catch so the fallback is static-only and a transient Supabase outage cannot fail `pnpm build`; supplier rows carry `lastModified=updated_at`, `changeFrequency='weekly'`, `priority=0.6`. `app/robots.ts` (`MetadataRoute.Robots`) emits a single `User-agent: *` rule with `Allow: /` + 7 disallow entries (`/app/`, `/supplier/`, `/admin/`, `/api/`, `/auth/`, `/dev/`, `/suspended/`) and a `Sitemap: ${SITE_URL}/sitemap.xml` line — `/compliance` and `/pricing` deliberately NOT in the disallow set (smoke (b.2) guards). Marketing route-group layout (`app/(marketing)/layout.tsx`) gains a default `metadata` export with `metadataBase: new URL(SITE_URL)` plus `openGraph: { siteName:'SourceBD', locale:'en_GB', type:'website', title, description }` and `twitter: { card:'summary_large_image', title, description }` — root `app/layout.tsx` is intentionally untouched (audit confirmed it owns no `openGraph`/`twitter`/`metadataBase` keys, so the marketing layout is the sole owner of marketing defaults and per-page `openGraph` exports (home + compliance detail) merge over them). Inline JSON-LD via `<script type="application/ld+json" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{__html: JSON.stringify(...)}}>` injected into the SSR'd `<main>` of three pages: `/` carries an `Organization` block (`name='SourceBD'`, `url=SITE_URL`, description); each `/compliance/{slug}` carries an `Article` block (`headline=page.title`, `datePublished` AND `dateModified` both = `page.last_reviewed_at` — CompliancePage has no separate publish date, the page is a living summary; `mainEntityOfPage` = canonical URL; `author`/`publisher` = SourceBD Organization); each public `/suppliers/{slug}` carries either an `Organization` or a `LocalBusiness` block (LocalBusiness selected when `city || district` present), populated strictly from columns already in the page's SELECT (`name=company_name`, `url=canonical`, `sameAs=[website]` when present, `address` as a `PostalAddress` with `addressLocality=city`, `addressRegion=district`, `addressCountry=country` — no `telephone`, no `email`, no `contactPoint`, no gated PII; the page SELECT is NOT widened). Supplier page also picks up an async `generateMetadata` that re-fetches `company_name, city, district, country` for the canonical URL + title + description (description format: `${company_name}, ${loc}. Verified registers, certifications, and source receipts on SourceBD.` when locality present, else without `, ${loc}`). `next/script` was deliberately not used — inline `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` is the SSR-only, zero-runtime-cost path for JSON-LD on a server component (`next/script` is a runtime loader designed for behaviour, not metadata). New disk-based smoke `ops/_m4_smoke.py` runs 8 checks (a)–(h) + the (b.2) money-page regression guard against `.next/server/app/`: artefact finder globs `sitemap*` / `robots*` and prefers `.body` files (Next 15.5.18 emits `sitemap.xml.body` + `sitemap.xml.meta` + `robots.txt.body` + `robots.txt.meta` siblings; the `.meta` files are sometimes larger than `.body`, so naïve largest-file heuristic picks the wrong artefact — `.body`-preferred glob fixes this). Smoke (a) asserts sitemap contains all expected static URLs + the 5 compliance slugs; (b) asserts robots has `User-agent: *` (case-insensitive — Next emits `User-Agent` with capital A) + all 7 disallow lines + the trailing `Sitemap:` line; (b.2) asserts `Disallow: /compliance` and `Disallow: /pricing` are absent; (c) asserts `/pricing` HTML inherits `og:site_name=SourceBD` + `og:locale=en_GB` from the marketing layout (proves the layout-level default reaches a page that does not itself set `openGraph`); (d) asserts `/` and `/pricing` both carry `<meta name="twitter:card" content="summary_large_image">`; (e) parses the JSON-LD blocks on `/` and asserts an Organization with `name=SourceBD` + `url=SITE_URL`; (f) parses the JSON-LD on each compliance detail and asserts Article with non-empty `headline` + `datePublished` + `dateModified` + matching `mainEntityOfPage`; (g) inspects the supplier page source (`/suppliers/[slug]` is `force-dynamic`, no prerender on disk) for the `application/ld+json` script tag + both `"LocalBusiness"` and `"Organization"` branches; (h) recursive forbidden-token scan across all M4-touched HTML against the M3 FORBIDDEN_TOKENS set (`supplier_score_internal`, `internal_score`, `SBI`, `nid_number`, `trade_license_number`, `proprietor_nid`, `owner_phone`). Validation: `pnpm typecheck` clean, `pnpm lint` zero warnings, `pnpm build` green **83 routes** (was 81; exact +2 = `/sitemap.xml` (`ƒ` dynamic because the anon Supabase client touches cookies, ISR via `revalidate=3600`) + `/robots.txt` (`○` static)); `python ops/_m4_smoke.py` → "M4 smoke PASSED — 8/8 checks, sitemap=sitemap.xml.body, robots=robots.txt.body, compliance pages=5." No new tools, no new deps, no DB migration, no new API route. Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions logged 3 Jun 2026 (Spec M4)

1. **Sitemap is a single `app/sitemap.ts` using `MetadataRoute.Sitemap`, not a hand-rolled route handler emitting XML.** Next's metadata convention gives us correct `xmlns`, correct `lastmod` serialisation, correct `priority`/`changefreq` enums for free, and ISR caching built-in. A custom `app/sitemap.xml/route.ts` would re-implement all of this and lose the framework's static-or-dynamic-or-ISR routing intelligence. The cost is that the anon Supabase client touches cookies, which forces the route to `ƒ` dynamic — accepted because `revalidate=3600` still caches the body for an hour at the edge.
2. **Anon Supabase server client in `app/sitemap.ts`, never the service-role client.** The sitemap is public output served to crawlers — running it under the service-role key would let any RLS regression silently leak unpublished suppliers into the sitemap. `pol_suppliers_pub_read` already covers the `is_published=true` SELECT under the anon role, so there's nothing to be gained by elevating; defence-in-depth says don't.
3. **Supplier SELECT inside the sitemap is wrapped in `try/catch` with `[m4] sitemap suppliers: N` logged on success and an `[m4] sitemap suppliers fetch failed: …` on error.** A transient Supabase outage at build time (no env, network blip, RLS edit gone wrong) would otherwise crash `pnpm build` and block deploys. Fall-back is the static-only sitemap — the marketing pages, the compliance pages, and `/discover` are still discoverable; only the supplier-detail URLs go missing for the next ISR window. The build log line is the operational signal for entry count; production deploys surface it in the build output without any new observability tool.
4. **`metadataBase` lives on the marketing route-group layout, not the root layout.** Root layout intentionally owns no `openGraph`/`twitter`/`metadataBase` keys today (verified before edit). Centralising marketing defaults in `app/(marketing)/layout.tsx` keeps the marketing surface self-contained and lets future authenticated surfaces own their own OG defaults independently if needed (e.g. a `/app/*` layout could ship its own metadata without colliding with marketing's `siteName=SourceBD` + `locale=en_GB` baseline).
5. **`<script type="application/ld+json" dangerouslySetInnerHTML>` for JSON-LD, never `next/script`.** `next/script` is a runtime loader designed for behaviour (analytics, third-party widgets) and adds client-side cost on a static server-rendered page. JSON-LD is metadata that crawlers read out of the SSR'd HTML — putting it through a runtime loader would gate it on hydration and double-ship the same bytes. Inline `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` is the React-idiomatic, SSR-only, zero-runtime path.
6. **`Article.datePublished == Article.dateModified == page.last_reviewed_at` (locked-in JC #7).** The `CompliancePage` data model has a single `last_reviewed_at` field — the page is a living summary, not an article with an original publish date that diverges from edits. A future spec that revisits compliance copy bumps `last_reviewed_at`, which mechanically updates both schema.org fields and the rendered "Last reviewed: …" footer. The alternative — fabricating a separate `datePublished` from `git log` or the file's creation timestamp — would publish a date we cannot defend ("when did SourceBD first publish on UFLPA?" has no honest answer for a living summary).
7. **Supplier JSON-LD emits ONLY columns already in the page's SELECT, never widens the SELECT.** The page's current SELECT is `id, slug, company_name, entity_type, city, district, country, website, source_tags, bgmea_verified, bkmea_verified, bgapmea_verified, btma_verified, claimed_by, is_sanctioned`. JC #8 originally said "LocalBusiness when `city || district || address_raw` present" but `address_raw` is not in the SELECT — silently widening it to include `address_raw` would have grown the row payload for every public profile view, which is exactly the kind of drift the "don't widen the SELECT silently" rule exists to prevent. Decision: the LocalBusiness check uses only `city || district` from the existing SELECT, and the JSON-LD `address` block emits only fields from the same SELECT (no `streetAddress` because `address_raw` is not loaded). No PII fields are eligible for inclusion at all — `telephone`, `email`, `contactPoint` are forbidden by hard rule #1 carry-forward (M3 forbidden-token doctrine).
8. **Sanctioned suppliers stay in the sitemap.** The public profile page already renders for sanctioned suppliers (with a red banner); hiding from the sitemap would also hide the sanctions banner from buyers who Google a supplier name and land via SERP. Crawlability mirrors the public-profile gate (`is_published=true`) — the sanctions flag controls the banner, not visibility.
9. **`/discover` is in the sitemap (`priority=0.9`, `changeFrequency='daily'`) despite being `force-dynamic`.** It's the canonical entry point for buyers Googling "Bangladesh garment factories" and similar queries. Force-dynamic means it doesn't prerender, but it still serves a public, crawlable HTML response on demand — there's no reason to hide it from search.
10. **Smoke artefact finder prefers `.body`-suffixed files.** Next 15.5.18 emits `sitemap.xml`, `sitemap.xml.body`, `sitemap.xml.meta` (and the same trio for robots). The first is a 0-byte placeholder; `.body` holds the actual response body; `.meta` holds the cache metadata and is often LARGER than `.body` (e.g. robots: meta=206 B, body=192 B). A naïve `glob('sitemap*') | largest` picks `.meta` for robots and produces a phantom "robots missing User-Agent" failure cascade. The `.body`-preferred branch is the locked-in fix.
11. **(b.2) money-page regression guard is its own check.** `Disallow: /compliance` and `Disallow: /pricing` are the two regressions that would silently de-index our highest-intent buyer surfaces. A separate named smoke check (rather than burying the assertion inside (b)) makes the failure message immediately readable in CI logs.

- ✅ **Spec M3 — Compliance education pages** shipped 3 Jun 2026. Third Phase-5 spec per `context/phases.md`. New content module `lib/marketing/compliance-pages.ts` exports `Citation`, `Section`, `CompliancePage` types, the `COMPLIANCE_PAGES` array of 5 regulation entries (uk-msa, uflpa, eu-cbam, eu-eudr, eu-csddd) plus hub metadata, the `DISCLAIMER` constant, the `SECTION_ORDER` ordered list, `findCompliancePage(slug)`, `oldestReviewedAt()`, and `assertContentFresh(now)` (throws if any page's `last_reviewed_at` is >365 days old; called at the top of every compliance page render, so stale content fails `pnpm build` not just smoke). Every citation is Tier-1 (gov) or Tier-5 (regulatory): UK legislation.gov.uk + Home Office + statement registry for MSA; congress.gov + DHS + CBP for UFLPA; eur-lex.europa.eu + European Commission DG Taxation for CBAM; eur-lex + DG Environment for EUDR; eur-lex + Commission for CSDDD. Zero Tier-4 brand wordmark cites (smoke check (c) asserts). New `app/(marketing)/compliance/page.tsx` (hub, `dynamic='force-static'`, 5 tile cards linking to `/compliance/{slug}`, role-aware CTA strip via `try/catch`-wrapped `getServerRole()` matching the `top-nav.tsx` pattern, anchored disclaimer + last-reviewed footer, canonical = `${SITE_URL}/compliance`). New `app/(marketing)/compliance/[slug]/page.tsx` (dynamic detail, `dynamic='force-static'` + `dynamicParams=false`, `generateStaticParams()` returns the 5 slugs, `notFound()` on unknown, async `generateMetadata` per slug with absolute canonical + OpenGraph + robots index,follow, 6-section anatomy iterated in `SECTION_ORDER` then References, each external link `rel="noopener noreferrer external" target="_blank"`). M2 chrome extended: `components/marketing/top-nav.tsx` adds a `Compliance` link left of `Pricing` in all 3 `rightLinks` branches (supplier, buyer/admin, anon — anon variant inside the same `hidden … sm:inline` group as Pricing); `components/marketing/footer.tsx` inserts a `Compliance` link between Pricing and Trademarks. **Smoke** `ops/_m3_smoke.py` (8 checks, prints `M3 smoke PASSED — 8/8 checks, 6 compliance pages + 2 regression pages.` on success) reads disk-rendered HTML from `.next/server/app/` after `pnpm build`: (a) hub `<title>` carries 'Compliance' + disclaimer literal in body; (b) each detail `<title>` carries the regulation short name + a `<link rel="canonical">` matching the expected URL; (c) each detail body has the disclaimer + ≥1 gov-domain outbound link + zero Tier-4 brand hosts + exactly 6 anatomy `<h2>` headings in JC-#11 order; (d) top-nav `Compliance` link renders on all 6 M3 pages plus regression pages `/` and `/pricing`; (e) footer link order `Pricing → Compliance → Trademarks` on a detail page; (f) staleness checked statically by inspecting the content-module exports (the render-time `assertContentFresh()` is the live gate that fails the build); (g) recursive forbidden-token scan across all M3 HTML returns `[]` against `{supplier_score_internal, internal_score, SBI, nid_number, trade_license_number, proprietor_nid, owner_phone}`; (h) every M3 page carries `<meta name="robots" content="index, follow">` and an absolute `<link rel="canonical">`. Spec doc: `context/feature-specs/spec-M3-compliance-education.md`. **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean (no errors); `pnpm lint` zero warnings (Next 15.5.18 `next lint` deprecation warning unchanged from M2); `pnpm build` ✓ 81 routes (was 75, +6 = `/compliance` static + 5 prerendered `/compliance/[slug]`); shared first-load JS 102 kB unchanged. Zero new tools, zero new deps, zero migration, zero `etl|ops/_*|supabase` Phase-0 touches.

### Architectural decisions logged 3 Jun 2026 (Spec M3)

1. **Hybrid hub + detail pattern (JC #11) over a single mega-page.** Five regulations have meaningfully different bodies (CSDDD is 2× the length of CBAM; UFLPA's penalties section is structurally different from MSA's). A single page would either bury detail behind tabs (poor for SEO and link-sharing) or balloon to a 12k-word scroll. Per-regulation detail URLs are independently shareable, independently indexable, independently citable in buyer compliance manuals, and let us iterate copy regulation-by-regulation without touching the others.
2. **One TS constant module is the content store, not MDX.** Hard rule #4 forbids new tools. MDX would add `@next/mdx` + a remark pipeline + a content collection convention. The 5 pages are small, structured, and rarely edited; a typed `CompliancePage[]` gives the same auditability with zero new tools and full TypeScript exhaustiveness on the slug union. When a sixth regulation lands, append to the array — `generateStaticParams()` picks it up automatically.
3. **`assertContentFresh()` is called inline at render, not just from smoke (locked-in JC #8).** A smoke-only staleness gate ships a stale page if the smoke is skipped. The render-time throw makes `pnpm build` itself the gate: a stale page fails the build, which fails CI, which blocks deploy. Smoke check (f) is belt-and-braces module-shape verification.
4. **`getServerRole()` wrapped in `try/catch` returning `null` (locked-in JC #7).** Mirrors the same pattern already in `components/marketing/top-nav.tsx`. Static prerender during `pnpm build` runs without `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*` env in some environments; an unguarded call would crash the build (which is exactly what bit M2 the first time). Anon-variant fall-through is correct for the prerendered HTML — buyer/supplier/admin sessions are dynamic anyway.
5. **Tier-1 + Tier-5 only on citations; Tier-4 brand wordmarks banned from these pages.** The source trust hierarchy is law (hard rule #5). Compliance education is exactly where the hierarchy matters most: a brand's published MSA statement is *evidence about that brand*, not a normative source on the MSA. Smoke check (c) makes the ban structural — a regression that cites `hm.com` or `walmart.com` on a compliance page fails the build pipeline.
6. **Smoke runs against disk-rendered HTML, not prod over HTTP (override of spec's smoke plan).** Per `/memories/scraping-ops.md` single-SSH discipline and the M2 precedent in `ops/_m2_smoke.py`, hitting prod over HTTP from a developer machine adds flakiness (DNS, CDN cache, transient 502s) and requires the page to be deployed before it can be validated. Reading `.next/server/app/*.html` after `pnpm build` is deterministic, hermetic, and runs before deploy — catching regressions in PR rather than in production.
7. **Top-nav `Compliance` link sits left of `Pricing` in every role branch.** Compliance comes before pricing in the buyer mental model ("can this supplier even ship to me legally" precedes "how much does the platform cost"). The anon variant places it inside the same `hidden … sm:inline` group as Pricing so mobile collapses both into the Start-free button — unchanged mobile silhouette.
8. **Footer link order Pricing → Compliance → Trademarks rather than alphabetical.** Same product-priority logic as the nav: Pricing is the primary conversion link, Compliance is the trust-and-credibility link, Trademarks is the legal-housekeeping link. Smoke check (e) asserts the order so a future contributor cannot silently re-alphabetise.
9. **Zero new database state, zero new API route.** The whole spec is 1 content module + 2 page files + 2 chrome edits + 1 smoke. No migration, no RPC, no auth flow. This is the cheapest possible compliance-content surface and keeps every byte of it under version control and TypeScript review.

All twelve pre-existing keys preserved verbatim so the buyer `/app/messages` page is unaffected. `revoke all from public; grant execute to authenticated`. **API layer**: `app/api/v1/messages/route.ts` GET handler loosened from `requireBuyerOrAdmin` to a new `requireAnyAuth()` (`buyer|supplier|admin`); POST handler also gated by `requireAnyAuth()` but `action === "open"` keeps the buyer/admin-only check (`thread_open` writes `auth.uid()` as `buyer_id`, suppliers must not be able to spawn buyer-shaped threads). `action === "send"` is open to all three roles because `thread_send_message`'s own participant check is the security boundary. **New pages**: `app/(app)/supplier/messages/page.tsx` (server; calls `supabase.rpc("thread_list")`, filters `viewer_role === 'supplier'`, renders inbox with `buyerLabel(t) = display_name ?? email ??
