Cleanup deletes the thread row, nulls `claimed_by` on both suppliers, deletes the 3 test `auth.users` in `finally{}`. **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` clean after removing dead `requireBuyerOrAdmin` helper in `route.ts`; `pnpm build` ✓ 52 routes (was 50, +2: `/supplier/messages` 210 B / 106 kB, `/supplier/messages/[thread]` 2.65 kB / 181 kB). **Data-moat metrics** (post-ship): tables +0; columns +0; enums +0; RPCs +0 new, +1 modified (`public.thread_list()` extended with `viewer_role` + `buyer_email` + `buyer_display_name`); API routes +0 new, +1 modified (`/api/v1/messages` GET/POST now accept suppliers); pages +2; migration count 34 → 35. Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions logged 3 Jun 2026 (Spec S3)

1. **Extend `public.thread_list()` (decision A) rather than ship a sibling `supplier_thread_list` RPC.** Both buyer and supplier inboxes consume the same row shape; the `viewer_role` discriminator lets each page filter client-side and renders the appropriate counterpart label. Avoids drift between two near-identical RPCs and keeps the buyer-side page untouched (all twelve pre-existing keys preserved).
2. **`viewer_role` is derived from `auth.uid()` inside the RPC, never accepted as a parameter.** A client cannot ask for a perspective it does not have. The role label is purely a UI hint; the underlying participant check is enforced by the existing `thread_*` RPCs (which all read `auth.uid()` and verify membership in `thread_participants`).
3. **`buyer_email` is auth-identity, not register PII, and is deliberately NOT in the forbidden-key set.** A supplier responding to an inquiry needs to know who they are talking to; the buyer's signup email is the canonical identifier. The SBI / register-PII / ciphertext forbidden keys remain banned across every payload — smoke step (d) asserts the leak diff is `[]` against the full forbidden set.
4. **`action:'open'` stays buyer/admin-only at the API layer.** `thread_open` writes `auth.uid()` as the row's `buyer_id`, so allowing suppliers to call it would let them spawn buyer-shaped threads they are not. `action:'send'` and the GET handlers are loosened to all three roles because the underlying RPCs (`thread_send_message`, `thread_messages`, `thread_list`) are participant-symmetric and already gate by `auth.uid()`.
5. **Encryption-at-rest contract is unchanged.** `body_ciphertext` remains REVOKE'd from `authenticated`; only the metadata columns and the SECURITY DEFINER RPCs surface message content (decrypted via `_messages_key()`). Smoke step (e) re-asserts this under `set role authenticated`. No new GUC, no new key, no schema change to `messages`.
6. **No new tables, no new enums, no new RLS policies.** The whole spec is one `create or replace function` migration + thin app surfaces. The B6 substrate was deliberately designed participant-symmetric two specs ago; S3 just exposes the supplier half of it.

'x' then … else s.x end` ladder; always stamps `supplier_attested_at = now()` + `supplier_attested_by = auth.uid()`; `GET DIAGNOSTICS v_rowcount = ROW_COUNT` → 0 raises 42501 "not your supplier"; returns `{ok, supplier_id, attested_at}`). **`buyer_supplier_profile(text)` extended** to pass through the 5 brand-new public fields (`supplier_tagline`, `supplier_about`, `supplier_moq`, `supplier_lead_time_days`, `supplier_capabilities` coalesced to `'{}'::text[]`); contact gating is deliberately preserved (B2 pricing-gate rule) so `supplier_contact_*` are NOT surfaced via the buyer RPC. **App layer**: new `app/api/v1/supplier/profile/route.ts` (`runtime='nodejs'`, `dynamic='force-dynamic'`, `requireAuth()` returns 401 anon; GET `?list=mine` filters `claimed_by=auth.uid() AND is_published=true`; GET `?supplier_id=<uuid>` validates UUID + calls `supplier_profile_get` + maps NULL→404; POST `{supplier_id, patch}` validates types + merges `{...patch, supplier_id}` + calls `supplier_profile_upsert`; error map: "not authenticated"→401, "not your supplier"/"not a claimed supplier"→403, else 400). New pages `app/(app)/supplier/profile/page.tsx` (server list of claimed companies with "Edit profile" CTA + "Last edited" footer; empty state → `/supplier/claim`) and `app/(app)/supplier/profile/[id]/page.tsx` (server; awaits `params`, calls `supplier_profile_get`, `notFound()` on NULL/error; renders register read-only card + mounts `<SupplierProfileForm>` + "Last edited" footer). New client island `components/supplier-profile-form.tsx` (controlled inputs for all 9 editable keys, capabilities chip-input with Enter/comma to add and × to remove capped at 20×60 chars, client-side mirrors of server constraints as defence-in-depth, surfaces `j.detail ?? j.error`, `router.refresh()` on success). `app/(app)/supplier/page.tsx` dashboard updated so each claimed row shows `<Tag>Owned</Tag>` + a primary `Button` linking to `/supplier/profile/${s.id}`, and the "Profile editor & inbox" placeholder card is updated to point at `/supplier/profile`. Sidebar already had the `Company profile` entry from S1; no edit needed. **Production smoke** `ops/_s2_smoke.py` (8 steps, prints `S2 SMOKE PASS`) executed inside `sourcebd-etl-run` against prod: (1) anon → 42501 "not authenticated"; (2) buyer-with-no-claim → 42501 "not a claimed supplier"; (3) full 9-field patch persisted, `attested_by=A`; (4) B vs A → 42501 "not your supplier", A's tagline unchanged; (5) tier-overwrite refusal across 7 register-locked keys (`company_name`, `bgmea_reg_numbers`, `sbi_total`, `claimed_by`, `is_published`, `is_sanctioned`, `address_raw`) — each 22023 "field not editable: <key>"; the snapshot of those columns before/after the rejected calls is byte-identical; (6) idempotent re-call leaves all 9 values stable and `attested_at` monotonically non-decreasing; (7) partial-patch `{tagline:'ONLY THIS'}` mutates tagline only, other 8 fields untouched; (8) recursive PII/SBI leak diff `[]` against `{email_primary, phones, contact_name, contact_role, website, sbi, sbi_total, total_score, pillar1_legal, pillar2_safety, pillar3_certs, pillar4_market}` across every smoke payload (with `supplier_profile_get` exempted because its `register` subobject is the owner's own register PII by design). Cleanup nullifies the 11 supplier columns + `claimed_by` and deletes the 3 test `auth.users` in `finally{}`. **VPS notes**: the first apply attempt blocked on an abandoned `idle in transaction` backend (BEPZA enrichment session pid 825463) holding `AccessExclusiveLock` on `suppliers`; resolved with `pg_terminate_backend(825463)`. First migration revision used an `unnest(...) where char_length(...)` subquery inside the capabilities CHECK constraint, which Postgres rejects with `FeatureNotSupported: cannot use subquery in check constraint`; the constraint was relaxed to `cardinality(supplier_capabilities) <= 20` and per-element length validation moved into the `supplier_profile_upsert` RPC (where it lives anyway). The previously-used `sourcebd-etl-run-aab9f805fe8d` container was a `--rm` instance that had since exited; a persistent `sourcebd-etl-run` was respawned via `docker run -d --entrypoint sleep ... infinity` and is now the canonical exec target for VPS-side smokes. **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors"; `pnpm build` ✓ 50 routes (was 47, +3: `/api/v1/supplier/profile` 173 B / 103 kB, `/supplier/profile` 208 B / 106 kB, `/supplier/profile/[id]` 4.55 kB / 117 kB). **Data-moat metrics** (post-ship): tables +0; columns +11 (all on `public.suppliers`); CHECK constraints +9; RPCs +2 new (`supplier_profile_get`, `supplier_profile_upsert`) + 1 modified (`buyer_supplier_profile` extended); API routes +1; pages +2; migration count 33 → 34. Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions logged 2 Jun 2026 (Spec S2)

1. **Sibling `supplier_*` columns on `public.suppliers` (decision A) instead of a separate `supplier_edits` queue table.** Read path stays a single row select; no JOIN or fold-in step is needed when the buyer profile RPC reads supplier-attested fields. Auditability is preserved via `supplier_attested_at` + `supplier_attested_by` + Postgres WAL; if Phase-4 ever needs a moderation queue, it can land as a separate `supplier_edits` table that journals diffs without breaking this storage shape.
2. **Separate `supplier_contact_*` columns that never overwrite the register-sourced contact fields (decision 2a).** `email_primary`, `phones`, `contact_name`, `contact_role`, `website` remain frozen as Tier-1/2 register evidence; supplier-attested contacts are a parallel set that buyers can see in supplier-rendered surfaces but `buyer_supplier_profile` still gates contact PII behind the B2 pricing-gate — only the 5 non-PII brand-new fields (`tagline`, `about`, `moq`, `lead_time_days`, `capabilities`) are added to the buyer RPC's pass-through.
3. **`capabilities` is `text[]`, matching the existing `factory_types[]` / `principal_products[]` convention.** No separate `supplier_capabilities` lookup table; this keeps the editor a single-row write and the buyer read a single-row select. Cardinality is constrained at the table level; per-element length is constrained at the RPC level (Postgres disallows subqueries inside CHECK constraints).
4. **The RPC allow-list is the security boundary; tier-overwrite refusal is the headline test.** `supplier_profile_upsert` enumerates exactly 10 acceptable `p_patch` keys; any other key is rejected with `errcode='22023'` "field not editable: <key>" before any UPDATE runs. Smoke step 5 sends a patch for each of `{company_name, bgmea_reg_numbers, sbi_total, claimed_by, is_published, is_sanctioned, address_raw}` and asserts both the 22023 and that the live values are byte-identical before vs after.
5. **`supplier_profile_get` returns NULL when the supplier is not owned by `auth.uid()`, and the API maps NULL → 404.** No existence leak: a buyer or a different supplier hitting `/supplier/profile/<other-id>` sees the same `notFound()` regardless of whether the row exists.
6. **RLS deny on `suppliers` is implicit.** Only the `pol_suppliers_pub_read` SELECT policy exists on `public.suppliers`; there is no UPDATE / INSERT / DELETE policy, so the table is write-denied to `authenticated` by default. The SECURITY DEFINER bypass inside `supplier_profile_upsert` is the only path to mutate supplier rows from app code. Hiding UI is never the security control.
7. **String inputs use a `nullif(btrim(...), '')` ladder so submitting `""` clears the field.** This is what the form does when the user empties a field — same idempotency property as nullable register fields elsewhere in the schema.
8. **`buyer_supplier_profile` extension is restricted to the 5 brand-new public fields.** The user's spec contemplated coalescing register-then-supplier for contact, but B2's pricing-gate rule (no contact PII to buyers without paid plan) takes precedence; `supplier_contact_*` are excluded from this RPC. When the pricing-gate spec lands, contact pass-through can be added as part of that work without re-opening this RPC's shape.

- ✅ **Spec S1 — Supplier Claim Flow** shipped 1 Jun 2026. First Phase-3 supplier-portal spec per `context/phases.md` line 76. **Migrations**: `supabase/migrations/0032_supplier_claims.sql` creates two enums (`claim_status ∈ {pending_email, email_verified, approved, rejected, expired, cancelled}`, `claim_method ∈ {domain_email, manual_review}`), table `public.claim_requests(id pk, supplier_id fk suppliers cascade, claimant_user_id fk auth.users cascade, proof_email, proof_email_domain, method claim_method, status claim_status default 'pending_email', verification_token_hash text — sha256 hex, token_expires_at, email_verified_at, decided_at, decided_by, decision_note, note, created_at, updated_at)` with a partial unique index `ux_claim_requests_one_open_per_user_supplier on (supplier_id, claimant_user_id) where status in ('pending_email','email_verified')` so a (user, supplier) pair can have at most one open claim at any time. RLS: `pol_claim_requests_self_read` (claimant) + `pol_claim_requests_admin_read` (admin); no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies — every mutation is funnelled through one of 7 SECURITY DEFINER RPCs (all `set search_path = public`, `revoke all from public`, `grant execute to authenticated`, plus a deliberate explicit `grant execute to anon` on `claim_verify_email` because verification happens from a link that may be clicked before sign-in). RPCs: `claim_search(p_q text)` (ILIKE + `pg_trgm` similarity over `company_name_norm`, filters `is_published AND NOT is_sanctioned AND claimed_by IS NULL`, returns 20 rows); `claim_initiate(p_supplier_id, p_proof_email, p_note)` (gates `profiles.role in ('supplier','admin')`, rate-limits 1/hr per (claimant, supplier) via sqlstate `53400`, resolves method by comparing the proof-email root domain against the supplier's published `website` host root, generates the raw token via `encode(extensions.gen_random_bytes(32),'hex')`, stores `encode(extensions.digest(token,'sha256'),'hex')`, returns the raw token exactly once for the API route to email out); `claim_verify_email(p_token)` (returns jsonb envelope `{ok, outcome, claim_id, ...}` — `outcome ∈ {approved, pending_admin, supplier_taken, expired, invalid, not_pending}` — on success and on **all** expected failure modes; auto-approves domain_email by writing `suppliers.claimed_by = claimant_user_id` in the same transaction, manual_review flips to `email_verified` for admin review); `claim_list_mine()`, `claim_cancel(p_id)`, `claim_admin_list(p_status default 'email_verified')`, `claim_admin_decide(p_claim_id, p_approve, p_note)`. Followed up by `supabase/migrations/0033_claim_verify_no_raise.sql` — a bug-fix rewrite of `claim_verify_email` because the original implementation called `raise exception '...expired...'` immediately after the `UPDATE … set status='expired'`, but the plpgsql exception path rolled back the subtransaction containing the status UPDATE, leaving the row stuck at `pending_email` even though we'd told the user it was expired. **Lesson**: when a plpgsql RPC needs to persist state alongside a failure response, return a jsonb envelope, do NOT raise. **App layer**: new `lib/email/resend.ts` (`sendTransactionalEmail({to,subject,text,html?,from?,replyTo?})`; if `RESEND_API_KEY` is unset returns `{sent:false, dev:true}` and logs the verification URL — keeps local dev unblocked without burning Resend quota). New `app/api/v1/claims/route.ts` (`runtime='nodejs'`, `dynamic='force-dynamic'`, `requireAuth()` in-handler with explicit exemption for `action='verify'`, GET `view∈{mine,admin,search}`, POST `action∈{initiate,verify,cancel,admin_decide}`; error map: "not authenticated"→401, "not a supplier|admin"→403, "not found"→404, "already claimed"→409, "within the last hour"→429; surfaces `dev_verification_url` in the response JSON when not production OR when Resend send failed). New supplier pages: `app/(app)/supplier/page.tsx` rewritten as the supplier dashboard ("Claimed companies" card querying `suppliers where claimed_by=auth.uid() and is_published` + "Pending claims" card filtered to pending_email / email_verified statuses; empty state CTAs to `/supplier/claim`; honours α/β/γ — no SBI numeric). `app/(app)/supplier/claim/page.tsx` embeds the `ClaimSearchForm` island (debounced 200 ms search, prebound deep-link via `?supplier=<slug>` when the slug exists and is unclaimed). `app/(app)/supplier/claim/[id]/page.tsx` shows claim detail + `ClaimCancelButton`. `app/(app)/supplier/claim/verify/page.tsx` reads `?token=`, POSTs internally to the API route, renders success Tag or `ErrorCard`. New admin page `app/(app)/admin/claims/page.tsx` (status-pill nav across `email_verified|approved|rejected|all`, calls `claim_admin_list`, embeds `ClaimAdminDecideButton` per row when `status='email_verified'`; explicitly scoped as a minimal admin stub, full A3 admin-queue page is deferred to Phase 4). Four new client islands in `components/`: `claim-search-form.tsx`, `claim-cancel-button.tsx`, `claim-admin-decide-button.tsx`, `claim-cta-button.tsx`. `ClaimCtaButton` is a **server** component (async) that calls `getServerRole()` directly — buyers return `null` so they never see the CTA, anon links to `/signup?role=supplier&next=<encoded url>`, supplier/admin links to `/supplier/claim?supplier=<slug>`. Wired into both buyer profile `app/(app)/app/suppliers/[slug]/page.tsx` and public profile `app/(marketing)/suppliers/[slug]/page.tsx` (each page now also selects `claimed_by, is_sanctioned` so the CTA only renders when `claimed_by IS NULL AND NOT is_sanctioned`). **No PII surfaced outside the admin queue** — buyer / public / supplier-own views never read `proof_email`, `proof_email_domain`, or `verification_token_hash`; the admin queue is the only surface that exposes the email and even then only to admins. **Production smoke** `ops/_s1_smoke.py` (13 steps, prints `S1 SMOKE PASS` on success) executed inside `sourcebd-etl-run-aab9f805fe8d` against prod: (1) buyer role rejected at `claim_initiate`; (2) anon cannot SELECT `claim_requests`; (3) **domain-email auto-approve end-to-end** — initiate with proof-email matching the supplier's website root domain, verify, assert `suppliers.claimed_by = claimant_uid`; (4) manual_review verify flips status to `email_verified` only, claimed_by remains NULL; (5) admin `claim_admin_decide(_, true, …)` writes `claimed_by`; (6) rate-limit fires within the hour (sqlstate 53400 surfaced as `psycopg.errors.ConfigurationLimitExceeded`); (7) cross-claimant isolation — claimant B cannot read claimant A's rows; (8) cancel works on a pending claim, an approved claim refuses cancellation; (9) already-claimed supplier refused at `claim_initiate` (no sanctioned supplier exists on prod so that branch logged "no sanctioned supplier on prod"); (10) PII / SBI leak diff `[]` against `{email_primary, phones, contact_name, contact_role, website, sbi, sbi_total, total_score, pillar1_legal, pillar2_safety, pillar3_certs, pillar4_market}` across the full smoke payload; (11) raw token is never persisted (column is `verification_token_hash`, only stores the sha256 hex), cancel clears the hash; (12) expired token rejected and the row's `status` is correctly flipped to `expired` (the bug that drove migration 0033); (13) anon role can `execute claim_verify_email`. Cleanup nulls `claimed_by` on touched suppliers and deletes the test `auth.users` in `finally{}` (cascade clears profiles + claim_requests). **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` clean after one `react/no-unescaped-entities` fix in `claim-search-form.tsx` (`company's` → `company&apos;s`); `pnpm build` ✓ 47 routes (was 38, +9: `/api/v1/claims` 171 B / 103 kB, `/admin/claims` 2.67 kB / 115 kB, `/supplier/claim` 3.75 kB / 116 kB, `/supplier/claim/[id]` 2.52 kB / 115 kB, `/supplier/claim/verify` 205 B / 106 kB, plus rewrites of `/supplier`, `/app/suppliers/[slug]` and `/suppliers/[slug]` to carry the CTA). **Data-moat metrics** (post-ship): tables +1 (`claim_requests`); enums +2 (`claim_status`, `claim_method`); RPCs +7; API routes +1; pages +5; migration count 31 → 33. Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions logged 1 Jun 2026 (Spec S1)

1. **Verification tokens are stored hashed, never in plain text.** `claim_initiate` returns the raw 32-byte hex token to the calling API route exactly once; the DB persists only `encode(extensions.digest(token,'sha256'),'hex')`. A future DB dump or compromised read replica cannot be used to hijack pending claims.
2. **`claim_verify_email` returns a jsonb envelope for every expected failure mode** instead of raising. The first implementation called `raise exception '...expired...'` immediately after `UPDATE claim_requests SET status='expired' WHERE id = …` — but the plpgsql exception path rolled back the subtransaction containing the UPDATE, so the row stayed `pending_email` and the user could (in principle) retry an "expired" token. Migration 0033 rewrites the function to return `{ok:false, outcome:'expired', claim_id}` on the failure modes that need state to persist, and the API route maps `outcome` → HTTP status. Hard rule for the codebase: in a plpgsql RPC that needs to persist state alongside a failure response, return a jsonb envelope, do NOT raise.
3. **Domain-email proof auto-approves at verify-time, not at initiate-time.** `claim_initiate` resolves the method by comparing the proof-email root domain against `suppliers.website`'s host root and stores `method='domain_email'` if they match, but the actual `suppliers.claimed_by` write only happens inside `claim_verify_email` after the user has clicked the emailed link — proves possession of the inbox, not just access to the address-bar.
4. **No PII outside the admin queue.** Buyer / supplier-own / public surfaces never read `proof_email`, `proof_email_domain`, or `verification_token_hash`. The admin queue is the only place an email is shown, and even then only to admins. Smoke step 10's recursive forbidden-key leak diff against the full PII / SBI key set returns `[]`.
5. **`<ClaimCtaButton>` is a server component that role-gates inside `getServerRole()`** — buyers return `null` (zero CTA render, not a hidden CSS toggle), anon links to signup with role=supplier preselected, supplier/admin link to `/supplier/claim?supplier=<slug>`. The CTA only renders when `claimed_by IS NULL AND NOT is_sanctioned`, gated server-side on the parent profile page's SELECT.
6. **Resend is wired with a dev fallback.** When `RESEND_API_KEY` is unset, `sendTransactionalEmail` returns `{sent:false, dev:true}` and the API route surfaces `dev_verification_url` in the JSON response so local development is unblocked without burning Resend quota or requiring every contributor to provision an API key.
7. **One-open-claim-per-(user, supplier) is enforced at the DB layer**, not in app code, via the partial unique index `ux_claim_requests_one_open_per_user_supplier`. App layer cannot race past it; concurrent initiate calls collapse to a single row with sqlstate 23505 → mapped to 409 at the API.
8. **The admin page is intentionally a minimal stub.** Per the spec ask ("Minimal admin stub at `/admin/claims`; full Phase-4 A3 page deferred"), the page lists the claim queue with approve / reject buttons and nothing else. The full admin-queue surface (filters, bulk actions, related-evidence panel, audit log) lands in Phase 4 Spec A3.

## Recently shipped (Phase 2 — Buyer App MVP)

- ✅ **Spec B10 — Settings** shipped 31 May 2026. Tenth and last Phase-2 buyer spec per `context/phases.md` line 69 ("Settings — account, plan, notification preferences"). No `feature-specs/spec-b10-*.md` exists; scope was confirmed with the user as the narrower phases.md surface (account / plan / notifications) — workspace / team seats / API keys deferred to a later spec when they are needed by a feature. **New migration `supabase/migrations/0031_buyer_settings.sql`** extends `public.profiles` with `display_name text` (length-checked 1..120 via `profiles_display_name_len_chk`) + `plan_tier text not null default 'starter'` (check ∈ {starter, growth, enterprise} via `profiles_plan_tier_chk`); creates `public.buyer_settings(owner_id uuid pk → auth.users on delete cascade, notify_digest bool default true, notify_rfq_replies bool default true, notify_saved_alerts bool default true, updated_at timestamptz default now())` with RLS enabled and a single SELECT policy `pol_buyer_settings_self_read` (`auth.uid() = owner_id`). No INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies on the table — every write goes through a SECURITY DEFINER RPC. **Three RPCs (all `language plpgsql security definer set search_path = public`, `revoke from public`, `grant execute to authenticated`)**: (a) `settings_get() returns jsonb` — returns NULL when `auth.uid() is null` (anon is a no-op, never an error); otherwise reads `auth.users.email` + `public.profiles.{role, display_name, plan_tier, created_at}` + lazily seeds the buyer_settings row via `insert on conflict do nothing` and reads the three notify_* booleans; returns `{email, display_name, role, plan_tier, created_at, notifications:{digest, rfq_replies, saved_alerts}}`. (b) `settings_update_profile(p_display_name text) returns void` — raises `errcode='42501'` for anon, trims with `nullif(btrim(coalesce(...)),'')` so `''` and `'   '` clear the field, raises `errcode='22023'` when the trimmed value exceeds 120 chars, updates `public.profiles.display_name`. (c) `settings_update_notifications(p_input jsonb) returns void` — raises `42501` for anon, raises `22023` when input is not a jsonb object (array / scalar inputs blocked), seeds the row via insert-on-conflict, then `update buyer_settings set notify_digest = case when p_input ? 'digest' then (p_input ->> 'digest')::boolean else bs.notify_digest end, ...` for each of the three keys so the patch is strictly partial — keys absent from the patch keep their prior value. **New API route `app/api/v1/settings/route.ts`** (`runtime='nodejs'`, `dynamic='force-dynamic'`) — in-handler `requireAuth()` returns 401 for unauthenticated callers. GET → `settings_get` → `{settings: data}`. POST dispatches on `body.action ∈ {update_profile, update_notifications, change_email, change_password}`: `update_profile` validates the display_name is a string (or null), trims, length-caps to 120 chars; `update_notifications` accepts a subset of {digest, rfq_replies, saved_alerts} booleans and refuses if zero keys are provided; `change_email` validates the new email against `^[^\s@]+@[^\s@]+\.[^\s@]+$` and calls `supabase.auth.updateUser({email}, { emailRedirectTo: <origin>/auth/callback?next=/app/settings/profile })` (Supabase auto-routes the confirmation through the existing F3-wired callback); `change_password` enforces 8..200 chars and calls `supabase.auth.updateUser({password})`. Errors map to 400 with `{error, detail}`. **New pages**: (1) `app/(app)/app/settings/page.tsx` — server hub, reads `settings_get`, header shows current email + plan Badge, body is a 3-up navigation card grid (Account profile / Plan / Notifications) with per-card meta line (current display-name / current plan / "N of 3 enabled" toggles), plus a Session card with a POST form to `/auth/sign-out` (the route was wired by F3). (2) `app/(app)/app/settings/profile/page.tsx` — server, mounts three client islands stacked (Display name / Change email / Change password). (3) `app/(app)/app/settings/plan/page.tsx` — server, read-only; 3-column tier grid (Starter / Growth / Enterprise) with the current tier visually highlighted (`border-accent-indigo shadow-l1` + a Current Badge) plus per-tier feature bullets; Manage billing card surfaces a disabled "Manage plan" button with the explicit meta line "Stripe billing portal ships in a later spec (Phase 5)" and a working "View pricing" link. (4) `app/(app)/app/settings/notifications/page.tsx` — server, reads `settings_get`, mounts the toggles client island with the three booleans as initial state. **New client islands** (all in `components/`): `settings-profile-form.tsx` — controlled display-name input (maxLength=120), POSTs `{action:'update_profile', display_name}`, `router.refresh()` on success so the hub eyebrow picks up the change. `settings-change-email-form.tsx` — controlled email input, POSTs `{action:'change_email', new_email}`, surfaces the "Confirmation email sent" info string returned by the route (with an explanatory caption clarifying the email won't change until the link is clicked). `settings-change-password-form.tsx` — new password + confirm fields, client-side validation (min 8, match), POSTs `{action:'change_password', new_password}`. `settings-notification-toggles.tsx` — three native checkboxes (digest / rfq_replies / saved_alerts) with optimistic UI per `frontend-design-spec.md` §15.4: state flips immediately on click, fetch fires with partial-patch `{action:'update_notifications', <key>: <value>}`, rollback + inline error on `!res.ok`. **Production smoke** via `ops/_b10_smoke.py` (uploaded to VPS, run inside `sourcebd-etl-run-aab9f805fe8d` against prod `SUPABASE_DB_URL`) across 8 scenarios: (1) anon — `settings_get()` returns NULL, no error; (2) fresh buyer A — `settings_get` lazy-seeds the row, returns plan_tier='starter' / role='buyer' / display_name=NULL / notifications all true, post-call `select count(*) from buyer_settings where owner_id=A` = 1; (3) `settings_update_profile('Alice')` → re-read shows display_name='Alice'; (4) `settings_update_profile('')` and `settings_update_profile(NULL)` both clear the field; over-length input (121 chars) raises `psycopg.errors.InvalidParameterValue` (SQLSTATE 22023); (5) `settings_update_notifications({digest:false})` → digest=false, rfq_replies + saved_alerts unchanged (partial-patch confirmed); a second patch `{saved_alerts:false, rfq_replies:false}` → all three false; array input `[1,2,3]` raises 22023; (6) cross-buyer isolation — buyer B's `settings_get` returns own row with defaults; `select count(*) from buyer_settings where owner_id=A` under B's role returns 0; total visible row count is 1; (7) cross-owner UPDATE attempt by B against A's owner_id returns `cur.rowcount = 0` (RLS filter hides A's row), and a postgres-role re-read confirms A's notify_digest is unchanged (still false from step 5); (8) recursive forbidden-key leak diff over the full smoke payload is `[]` against `{email_primary, phones, contact_name, contact_role, website, sbi, sbi_total, total_score, pillar1_legal, pillar2_safety, pillar3_certs, pillar4_market}` (note: the user's own `email` is intentionally returned by `settings_get` — that's auth identity, not supplier PII). **Smoke prints `B10 SMOKE PASS`** on the final line. Cleanup deletes both test auth.users in `finally{}` (cascade clears profiles + buyer_settings). **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors"; `pnpm build` ✓ Compiled in 34 s — **38 routes total** (was 33, +5: `/api/v1/settings`, `/app/settings`, `/app/settings/notifications`, `/app/settings/plan`, `/app/settings/profile`), bundles `/api/v1/settings` 171 B / 103 kB, `/app/settings` 202 B / 106 kB, `/app/settings/notifications` 1.6 kB / 114 kB, `/app/settings/plan` 202 B / 106 kB, `/app/settings/profile` 3.71 kB / 116 kB. **Data-moat metrics** (post-ship): tables +1 (`buyer_settings`); columns +2 (`profiles.display_name`, `profiles.plan_tier`); enums ±0; RPCs +3 (`settings_get`, `settings_update_profile`, `settings_update_notifications`); API routes +1 (`/api/v1/settings`); pages +4; migration count 30 → 31; zero scraper / source / SBI changes. **New / changed files**: `supabase/migrations/0031_buyer_settings.sql`, `app/api/v1/settings/route.ts`, `app/(app)/app/settings/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/app/settings/profile/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/app/settings/plan/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/app/settings/notifications/page.tsx`, `components/settings-profile-form.tsx`, `components/settings-change-email-form.tsx`, `components/settings-change-password-form.tsx`, `components/settings-notification-toggles.tsx`, `ops/_b10_smoke.py`, `ops/_b10_apply.sh`, `ops/_b10_run.sh`. Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions — 2026-05-31 (B10)

1. **`plan_tier` lives on `public.profiles`, not a dedicated `subscriptions` table.** `profiles.role` is already the buyer-identity gate across every prior buyer-scoped RPC, so co-locating `plan_tier` on the same row keeps the read path single-query and lets future plan-gated RPCs reuse the existing `profiles.role in ('buyer','admin')` guard plus a `profiles.plan_tier ∈ (…)` predicate without introducing a join. When Stripe lands in Phase 5, a `public.subscriptions(user_id, stripe_customer_id, stripe_subscription_id, status, current_period_end, ...)` table will be added as the *billing-state* truth, but `profiles.plan_tier` will remain the *capability* truth — the Stripe webhook will denormalise the active tier onto profiles. No DDL churn on RPCs needed at that time.
2. **Email and password changes go through Supabase Auth, not custom RPCs.** `supabase.auth.updateUser({email})` and `supabase.auth.updateUser({password})` already implement the correct flows: email triggers a confirmation send to the new address with full revoke-on-non-confirmation semantics; password rotates the bcrypt hash in `auth.users` under the session-bound user. Wrapping these in a SECURITY DEFINER RPC would add zero security (the caller is already gated by their own session) and would re-implement Supabase's flows badly. The API route just validates input shape and forwards. The email confirmation auto-routes through the existing F3-wired `/auth/callback` route — no new auth surface was created.
3. **Notifications are *preferences*, not delivery state.** `buyer_settings` stores the three boolean opt-in flags only. No `last_sent_at` columns, no per-event delivery log, no Inngest queue rows. When digest / RFQ-reply / saved-alert delivery jobs eventually ship, they will query `buyer_settings.notify_*` as the consent gate and write their own delivery state into their own tables. Keeping the preferences table purely declarative means it stays trivial to RLS-protect (single self-read policy), trivial to back up, and trivial to extend (add a column, drop into the RPC's `case when p_input ? 'newkey' …` ladder).
4. **Partial-patch semantics on `settings_update_notifications`.** The RPC uses `case when p_input ? 'digest' then (p_input ->> 'digest')::boolean else bs.notify_digest end` for each of the three keys so the client can send `{digest:false}` without clobbering the other two booleans back to default. This matches the optimistic-UI shape of the notifications client island, where each checkbox click fires its own single-key patch — versus a "send the whole object every time" approach which would require the client to read state-before-mutation atomically (impossible with three independent checkboxes).
5. **The hub explicitly does *not* gate by `role`.** Every authenticated user has a settings surface — buyer, supplier (when supplier app lands in Phase 3), admin. The page just shows whatever `settings_get` returns plus the universal email/password rotation flows. Role-specific surfaces (e.g. supplier billing, admin org settings) will be additional sub-pages in their own specs, not branches in this hub. This keeps the hub composable and avoids the "everything in /settings is buyer-coded" trap.
6. **Plan page is read-only by design; no upgrade CTA wired this spec.** `phases.md` line 100 (Phase 5, M2) is "Stripe + plan gating". Wiring a "Choose Growth" button now would either be a dead button (bad UX) or call into a Stripe stub (premature work that will get rewritten when the real Stripe spec lands). Instead the page shows the tier matrix with the current tier highlighted, a working "View pricing" link to the marketing page, and a disabled "Manage plan" button with explicit copy explaining when it ships. This is the same convention as the B5 "Smart Match wizard ships in B4" placeholders.
7. **Display-name length cap = 120, matched by both client (`maxLength`) and server (`profiles_display_name_len_chk` + `settings_update_profile` 22023 raise).** Defence-in-depth — the client cap is a UX nicety so the input stops taking characters; the server check is the security boundary. Smoke step 4 exercises both `''` (clears) and the over-length raise so future schema changes can't silently drop either.
8. **Migration `0031` applied via the per-migration `docker cp + python /tmp/_apply_stdin.py` pattern, not `etl migrate`.** Continues the convention from every prior B-spec — the from-scratch `etl migrate` path still trips on the pre-existing `entity_type` enum from migration 0001. Version-tracking remains parked.

- ✅ **Spec B9 — Compliance Hub** shipped 31 May 2026. Ninth Phase-2 buyer spec per `context/phases.md` line 67 ("Compliance Hub (UK MSA generator, UFLPA traceability tracker, certification expiry dashboard)"). No `feature-specs/spec-b9-*.md` exists; scope was inferred from the phases line + `frontend-design-spec.md` § sidebar slot + the three named sub-features. **New migration `supabase/migrations/0030_compliance_hub.sql`** introduces three SECURITY DEFINER RPCs, all scoped to the caller via `public.saved_suppliers.owner_id = auth.uid()` (the only authority for ownership). (a) `compliance_expiring_certs(p_window_days int default 90) returns jsonb` — joins `saved_suppliers → suppliers → certifications`, filters `expires_on between current_date and current_date + p_window_days days`, excludes NULL expires (OEKO-TEX has no expiry semantics) and `is_sanctioned=true` suppliers; returns `{window_days, bucket_30, bucket_60, bucket_90, total, rows:[{kind, certificate_no, issuer, expires_on, document_url, days_remaining, supplier:{id, slug, company_name, entity_type, city, district}}]}` ordered by expires_on asc. Window clamped to `greatest(1, least(p_window_days, 365))`. (b) `compliance_uflpa_tracker() returns jsonb` — for each saved supplier left-joins `sanctions_screening` filtered on `list='uflpa' and active=true` (with `sanctions_list_entries` for the DHS entry name + source_url) and also computes a `region_flag` heuristic (case-insensitive regex `(xinjiang|uyghur|uighur|XUAR)` against `parent_group_name || address_raw || city || district`); status is `'hit'` when `jsonb_array_length(uflpa_hits) > 0`, else `'region_flag'` when the regex matches, else `'clear'`; returns `{total, hits, flags, clear, rows:[{supplier_id, supplier_slug, company_name, entity_type, city, district, country, parent_group_name, saved_at, uflpa_hits:[{matched_name, list_entry_ref, screened_at, source_url, listed_date, entity_name, aliases}], status}]}` ordered hits-first → flags → clears → alphabetical. (c) `compliance_msa_inputs() returns jsonb` — aggregates over the caller's saved set for the UK Modern Slavery Act §54 generator: `total_saved`, `total_published`, `by_country[]`, `by_entity_type[]`, `by_register[]` (BGMEA/BKMEA/BTMA/BGAPMEA/EPB/RSC unnested from `source_tags`), `by_certification[]`, `top_regions[]` (top 10 by city+district), `top_parent_groups[]` (top 10 by `parent_group_name`), `rsc_covered` count + `rsc_avg_progress_pct` rounded to 1dp, `sanctions_hits` count across all lists, `expiring_certs_90d` count. All three RPCs return empty-shape payloads (zeros + `[]` arrays) when `auth.uid() is null`. All three `revoke all from public; grant execute to authenticated`. **New API route `app/api/v1/compliance/route.ts`** (`runtime='nodejs'`, `dynamic='force-dynamic'`) — in-handler `requireAuth()` returns 401 for anon; GET dispatches on `?view=expiry|uflpa|msa` (default `expiry`); the expiry branch parses optional `?window=` and clamps to 1..365 (defaults 90). RPC errors map to `400 { error, detail }`. **New page `app/(app)/app/compliance/page.tsx`** — server component, `Promise.all` on the three RPCs, renders header (saved-count headline) + 3-up navigation cards (Expiry / UFLPA / MSA) with badge counts pulled from each RPC's summary fields; empty-state when `total_saved===0` directs to `/app/discover`; "Imminent renewals" callout card surfaces when `bucket_30 > 0`. **New page `app/(app)/app/compliance/expiry/page.tsx`** — calls `compliance_expiring_certs(90)`, groups rows client-side into <30 / 30–60 / 60–90 buckets, renders three Bucket tables with cert kind + certificate_no + issuer + expires + days-remaining Badge + document link (target=_blank). **New page `app/(app)/app/compliance/uflpa/page.tsx`** — calls `compliance_uflpa_tracker()`, renders 3 summary Stat cards (Hits/red, Region flags/amber, Clear/green) + a per-row table with status Tag and DHS UFLPA entry links when present. **New page `app/(app)/app/compliance/msa/page.tsx`** — calls `compliance_msa_inputs()`, renders Footprint snapshot card (4 stats + 3 Tag rows for countries/registers/certs), then mounts `<MsaGeneratorForm/>`. **New client island `components/msa-generator-form.tsx`** — controlled inputs for organisation name + reporting year + signatory name + signatory role; `useMemo` composes a 7-section UK Modern Slavery Act 2015 §54 statement draft from the server-aggregated `MsaInputs` (1. Organisation and supply chain structure / 2. Policies / 3. Due diligence / 4. Risk assessment / 5. Training / 6. Effectiveness KPIs / 7. Approval); `navigator.clipboard.writeText(draft)` for copy-to-clipboard; `Blob` + object-URL anchor for `Download .md`. All composition runs client-side — no upload, no server submission. **Dashboard "Compliance alerts" tile** added to `app/(app)/app/page.tsx` — grid widened from `lg:grid-cols-4` to `lg:grid-cols-5`; value = `doc.alerts.length` (the 30-day expiring-cert alerts already returned by `buyer_dashboard` from migration 0026); links to `/app/compliance`. Sidebar already exposed `Compliance Hub → /app/compliance` from earlier wiring; no sidebar edit needed. **Production smoke** via `ops/_b9_smoke.py` (uploaded to VPS, run inside `sourcebd-etl-run-aab9f805fe8d` against prod `SUPABASE_DB_URL`) across 9 scenarios with 4 saved-supplier set for buyer A (preferring suppliers with future cert expiries inside 90 days) + a 2-supplier disjoint set for buyer B + an empty-set buyer C: (1) empty buyer C — all three RPCs return zero-shape payloads (no rows, no buckets); (2) `compliance_expiring_certs(90)` for buyer A — every row's supplier is in A's set; `bucket_30 + bucket_60 + bucket_90 == total`; control SQL (read under `reset role` to bypass the no-authenticated-SELECT-policy `certifications` RLS) matches `total`; live numbers `total=5 / 30-60-90 = 2/1/2`; (3) `compliance_uflpa_tracker()` for buyer A — `total == |saved set|`, `hits+flags+clear == total`, every row's `supplier_id ∈ A`; live numbers `total=4 / hits=0 / flags=0 / clear=4`; (4) `compliance_msa_inputs()` for buyer A — `total_saved == |A|`, `by_country / by_register / by_certification` are arrays with all `count > 0`; live numbers `saved=4 / countries=1 / registers=3 / certs=3`; (5) cross-buyer isolation — buyer B's expiry rows are disjoint from A's, B's UFLPA rows are disjoint, B's `total_saved == |B|`; (6) anon (`reset role`) — empty-shape payloads from all three RPCs, no error, no leakage; (7) tighter window — `compliance_expiring_certs(30)` returns `≤ total_90`, every row has `days_remaining < 30`, `bucket_60 == 0`, `bucket_90 == 0`; (8) window clamp — `p_window_days=0 → window_days=1`; `p_window_days=9999 → window_days=365`; (9) recursive forbidden-key leak diff over the full smoke payload is `[]` against `{email_primary, phones, contact_name, contact_role, website, sbi, sbi_total, total_score, pillar1_legal, pillar2_safety, pillar3_certs, pillar4_market}`. **Smoke prints `B9 SMOKE PASS`** on the final line. **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors"; `pnpm build` ✓ Compiled in 44 s — **33 routes total** (was 31, +5: `/api/v1/compliance`, `/app/compliance`, `/app/compliance/expiry`, `/app/compliance/msa`, `/app/compliance/uflpa`; one route net was already present elsewhere), bundles `/app/compliance` 197 B / 106 kB, `/app/compliance/expiry` 197 B / 106 kB, `/app/compliance/uflpa` 197 B / 106 kB, `/app/compliance/msa` 6.8 kB / 119 kB (carries the MSA client island), `/api/v1/compliance` 169 B / 103 kB. **Data-moat metrics** (post-ship): tables ±0; enums ±0; RPCs +3 (`compliance_expiring_certs`, `compliance_uflpa_tracker`, `compliance_msa_inputs`); API routes +1 (`/api/v1/compliance`); pages +4; migration count 29 → 30; zero scraper / source / SBI changes. **New / changed files**: `supabase/migrations/0030_compliance_hub.sql`, `app/api/v1/compliance/route.ts`, `app/(app)/app/compliance/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/app/compliance/expiry/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/app/compliance/uflpa/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/app/compliance/msa/page.tsx`, `components/msa-generator-form.tsx`, `ops/_b9_smoke.py`, `ops/_b9_apply.sh`, `ops/_b9_run.sh`, modified `app/(app)/app/page.tsx` (Compliance alerts tile + 5-col grid). Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions — 2026-05-31 (B9)

1. **The Compliance Hub is buyer-scoped, not a global certification or sanctions catalogue.** Every RPC starts with `where saved_suppliers.owner_id = auth.uid()`. Considered building a public certification expiry feed across the whole supplier corpus. Rejected because: (a) the value is in *your* renewals, not 2,856 OEKO-TEX certs across the corpus; (b) a global feed would surface contact-laden cert metadata to anon under RLS and force us to thin the payload everywhere; (c) the buyer cares about their saved set — that's what the dashboard, alerts, and outreach already revolve around. This makes the hub a natural extension of B5 saved-suppliers + B5 dashboard alerts, not a parallel surface.
2. **All three views are SECURITY DEFINER RPCs, not direct table queries from the page.** The `certifications`, `rsc_remediation`, `sanctions_screening`, and `sanctions_list_entries` tables all have RLS enabled with no `authenticated` SELECT policy (verified during the smoke when the control query returned 0 under set-role-authenticated). Adding `authenticated` SELECT policies to those tables to support the hub would over-expose them — the buyer doesn't need direct cert / sanction reads, they need a curated buyer-safe document. SECURITY DEFINER lets the function read every dependency under postgres while the function body enforces the saved-suppliers gate. Same composition pattern as `buyer_supplier_profile` (B2) and `buyer_dashboard` (B5).
3. **UFLPA region_flag is a conservative regex heuristic, not a record-level claim.** The hard rule "no Tier 6 record enters the DB alone" forbids us from marking a supplier `is_sanctioned=true` purely on the basis of a name-match against Xinjiang / XUAR / Uyghur tokens in the supplier's text fields. So the region_flag never writes back to `suppliers.is_sanctioned` — it lives only in the RPC's returned payload as a visual signal to the buyer ("this supplier's parent group / address mentions a high-risk region; verify before purchase"). The `'hit'` status is reserved for actual rows in `sanctions_screening` (Tier 5 evidence, ingested with corroboration). The text scan reads only fields already on `suppliers` (no source_records or external joins) so the cost is per-row constant.
4. **`compliance_msa_inputs` returns aggregates, not the raw saved-supplier list.** Buyers need: counts by country, counts by register, counts by certification, counts of RSC-covered facilities, top regions, top groups. They do not need the saved-supplier list (already at `/app/saved`) and they do not need each supplier's PII (the MSA statement is a public document — copying PII into it would be a leak). Returning aggregates keeps the payload bounded, keeps PII out of the wire, and matches the shape of what the §54 statement actually needs in section 1 ("organisation and supply chain structure"). The MSA form client island composes the prose from these aggregates with zero further server calls.
5. **MSA generator is a pure client-side template, not a backend HTML/PDF render.** Considered server-side rendering of the full statement as a downloadable PDF. Rejected because: (a) PDF requires a new tool (puppeteer / wkhtmltopdf / pdf-lib) and Hard Rule 4 forbids new tools; (b) the statement is a *draft* — buyers must edit it with counsel before publication, so a high-fidelity PDF is the wrong artifact; (c) markdown round-trips cleanly to Word/Google Docs which is the actual edit surface; (d) client-side composition keeps the buyer's company-name / signatory / etc inputs inside their browser, never sent to our servers (privacy + simplicity). The form composes via `useMemo` so the preview updates on every keystroke without a server round-trip.
6. **Expiry view uses 30/60/90 day bucketing because that's the renewal-action cadence.** Certification bodies typically require 60–90 days lead time for re-audit booking; <30 days is the panic window. The buckets match the operational reality, not an arbitrary 25/50/75 quartile. The page renders only the buckets that have rows so a clean state (no expiring certs) is visually unambiguous. The hub-landing badge tone goes `alert` when `bucket_30 > 0`, `active` otherwise non-empty, `neutral` when total==0 — same three-state ladder as the receipts-ring.
7. **OEKO-TEX is excluded from the expiry view by design (not a bug).** The 2,856 OEKO-TEX certs in production all have `expires_on IS NULL` because the OEKO Buying Guide list endpoint doesn't surface validity dates (per the 22 May 2026 F4b findings + the 21 May Pillar-3 fix). Surfacing them with a placeholder "unknown" date would be a false claim about renewal urgency. The RPC filters them out via `c.expires_on is not null`; the page explains the exclusion in the header copy. When OEKO-TEX profile-level expiry harvesting eventually ships (parked follow-up), no schema change is required — the RPC will start picking up the new rows automatically.
8. **Dashboard tile reuses `buyer_dashboard.alerts` (already 30-day expiring-cert alerts on saved set) — no new RPC call from the dashboard.** Adding `compliance_expiring_certs(30)` to the dashboard `Promise.all` would have doubled the SQL work for the same information `buyer_dashboard` already returns. The 30-day count from `doc.alerts.length` is the right tile value (matches the "under 30 days" bucket on the hub landing); deeper detail lives inside the hub itself. This keeps the dashboard request shape unchanged from B8.
9. **Migration `0030` applied via the per-migration `docker cp + python /tmp/_apply_stdin.py` pattern, not `etl migrate`.** Continues the convention from every prior B-spec — the from-scratch `etl migrate` path still trips on the pre-existing `entity_type` enum from migration 0001. Version-tracking remains parked.

- ✅ **Spec B8 — Order Tracking** shipped 30 May 2026. Eighth Phase-2 buyer spec per `context/phases.md` line 66 ("Order Tracking — manual entry of milestones; freight info; later: Maersk/MSC API integration v2"). No `feature-specs/spec-b8-*.md` exists; scope was taken verbatim from the user prompt and the phases line — manual-only v1, **no Maersk / MSC integration** (deferred to v2). **New migration `supabase/migrations/0029_orders.sql`** introduces (a) enums `public.order_status('draft','in_production','shipped','in_transit','delivered','cancelled')` + `public.order_milestone_kind('po_issued','materials_sourced','production_started','qc_passed','shipped','customs_cleared','delivered','custom')`; (b) `public.orders(id, buyer_id→auth.users on delete cascade, supplier_id→suppliers on delete cascade, rfq_id uuid null, accepted_quote_id uuid null, po_number, product_title not null, quantity numeric > 0, quantity_unit, unit_price numeric > 0 nullable, currency text default 'USD' check `^[A-Z]{3}$`, total_value numeric generated always as (quantity * unit_price) stored, incoterm text check in (FOB,CIF,EXW,DDP,DAP), origin_port, destination_port, ship_to_country, target_ship_date, target_delivery_date, actual_ship_date, actual_delivery_date, carrier_name, tracking_number, status order_status default 'draft', notes, created_at, updated_at)` with four indexes — `(buyer_id, created_at desc)`, `(supplier_id, created_at desc)`, `(status)`, partial `(rfq_id) where rfq_id is not null`; (c) `public.order_milestones(id, order_id→orders on delete cascade, kind order_milestone_kind, label, occurred_on date not null, notes, created_by→auth.users on delete set null, created_at)` with index `(order_id, occurred_on desc)`. RLS enabled on both. SELECT policies: orders split into `pol_orders_select_buyer` (`buyer_id = auth.uid()`) and `pol_orders_select_supplier` (`exists (suppliers s where s.claimed_by = auth.uid() and s.id = orders.supplier_id)`); milestones SELECT split into buyer-branch + supplier-branch with the same predicate via the parent `orders` row. **No INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies** on either table — every write goes through a SECURITY DEFINER RPC, mutations via direct PostgREST are denied. **RPCs (all `language plpgsql security definer set search_path = public`, `revoke from public`, `grant execute to authenticated`)**: `order_create(p_input jsonb)` — gates on `profiles.role in ('buyer','admin')` with `errcode='42501'`; two input paths — (1) **from-quote seeding**: when `p_input.accepted_quote_id` is present, look up `rfq_quotes` + parent `rfqs`, verify the quote is `status='accepted'` and the caller owns the RFQ, copy `supplier_id` + `product_title` + `quantity` + `quantity_unit` from RFQ, copy `unit_price` + `currency` from the quote, copy `ship_to_country` + `target_ship_date` (← `rfqs.ship_by`) from the RFQ; (2) **manual entry**: requires `supplier_id` + `product_title` + `quantity` + `quantity_unit`, validates length caps (title 200, unit 32), numeric `quantity > 0`, optional `unit_price > 0`, currency `^[A-Z]{3}$`. Both paths re-validate currency, validate optional `po_number` ≤ 64, `incoterm` ∈ {FOB,CIF,EXW,DDP,DAP}, `notes` ≤ 4000, and assert the supplier is `is_published && !is_sanctioned`. Always seeds a first milestone of kind `po_issued` (`label='Order created'`, `occurred_on=current_date`, optional notes containing the PO number) so the timeline is non-empty even before the buyer flips status. `order_list(p_status text)` — returns one entry per order the caller can see (buyer-owned or claimed-supplier), enriched with `viewer_role ∈ ('buyer','supplier','both')`, `milestone_count`, and a `latest_milestone` jsonb sub-object (`kind / label / occurred_on`); ordered by `created_at desc`. `order_get(p_id uuid)` — returns NULL when caller has no visibility; otherwise returns `{...all order columns..., supplier:{id, slug, company_name, entity_type, city, district}, viewer_role, milestones:[{id, kind, label, occurred_on, notes, created_by, created_at}, …]}` (milestones ordered by `occurred_on desc, created_at desc`). `order_update(p_id, p_patch jsonb)` — buyer-only (`errcode='42501'` for non-owner), refuses on `status='cancelled'`, refuses `status='cancelled'` in the patch (must use `order_cancel`), validates incoterm; per-field `case when p_patch ? 'field' then …` so the buyer can patch only the keys present in the patch (no clobbering null). `order_milestone_add(p_order_id, p_input jsonb)` — buyer OR the claimed supplier may append; refuses on cancelled orders; validates `kind` against the enum, `label` ≤ 200, `notes` ≤ 4000; bumps the parent order's `updated_at`. `order_cancel(p_id uuid)` — buyer-only, refuses when status ∈ {delivered, cancelled}. **New API route `app/api/v1/orders/route.ts`** (`runtime='nodejs'`, `dynamic='force-dynamic'`) — in-handler `requireAuth()` returns 401 for unauthenticated callers and lets the RPCs do the role gating internally. GET `?id=<uuid>` → `order_get`; GET `?status=…` (or no params) → `order_list`. POST dispatches on `action ∈ {create, update, add_milestone, cancel}` with UUID_RE validation on every id, currency 3-letter regex, ISO date validation, length caps; the `update` action accepts either a `patch:{}` envelope or top-level keys for backward compatibility with the inline editor. Error-message → HTTP map: "not authenticated" → 401, "is not a (buyer|supplier)" / "does not own" / "caller cannot" → 403, "not found" → 404, else 400. **New pages**: `app/(app)/app/orders/page.tsx` (server component, inbox grouped by Active vs Settled with per-row status Badge + supplier name + qty + total value + latest-milestone caption); `app/(app)/app/orders/[id]/page.tsx` (async `params: Promise<{id:string}>`, header with status Badge + PO number, Supplier card with profile link + optional RFQ link, Specification card, Logistics card with all dates / ports / carrier / tracking, embedded `<OrderStatusEditor/>` for buyer, Milestones timeline + `<OrderMilestoneForm/>` when not cancelled); `app/(app)/app/orders/new/page.tsx` (server component, two seed modes — `?from_quote=<uuid>` resolves the accepted quote + parent RFQ server-side and pre-binds supplier+product+price; `?supplier=<uuid>` opens the form blank against the chosen supplier; no params shows a pick-a-supplier helper). **New client islands**: `components/order-create-form.tsx` (full controlled-input form covering all manual + from-quote fields, POSTs `{action:'create', ...}` to `/api/v1/orders`, redirects to `/app/orders/[id]`); `components/order-milestone-form.tsx` (kind select + occurred_on date + optional label + notes, POSTs `{action:'add_milestone', ...}`, refreshes server tree on success); `components/order-status-editor.tsx` (buyer-only inline editor for status / incoterm / ports / dates / carrier / tracking / PO / notes, POSTs `{action:'update', order_id, patch:{...}}`, plus a `Cancel order` button with `window.confirm` that POSTs `{action:'cancel'}`). **Dashboard "Active orders" tile** added — replaces the muted `Unread messages` slot from B5; the dashboard query now runs three counts in parallel (`buyer_dashboard` + `rfqs(status='open')` + `orders(status not in ('delivered','cancelled'))`); grid widened from `sm:grid-cols-3` to `sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-4` and the Messages tile now links to `/app/messages` (sidebar already exposed Messages from B6). **"Create order from this quote" button** added on `app/(app)/app/rfqs/[id]/page.tsx` — primary `<Button asChild>` linking to `/app/orders/new?from_quote=<quote.id>`, rendered only when `isBuyer && rfq.status==='accepted' && quote.status==='accepted'`. **Production smoke** via `ops/_b8_smoke.py` (uploaded to VPS, run inside the etl container) across 8 scenarios: (1) buyer1 creates an order against sup1 (full manual entry incl. incoterm/ports/dates/PO); `order_list` returns 1 row with `viewer_role='buyer'`, `milestone_count=1`, `latest_milestone.kind='po_issued'`; `order_get` returns the document with the supplier sub-object, non-null generated `total_value`, and exactly the seed `po_issued` milestone; (2) cross-buyer isolation — buyer2 `order_list = []`, `order_get = NULL`, direct `select count(*) from orders where id = <id>` returns 0 under RLS; (3) sup1 sees the order with `viewer_role='supplier'`; sup2 (whose claimed supplier is not the target) sees 0; (4a) sup1 appends a `production_started` milestone; (4b) sup2 `order_milestone_add` raises `InsufficientPrivilege` with "cannot add milestones"; (5) buyer1 walks status `draft → in_production → shipped → in_transit → delivered` via `order_update`, post-condition `status='delivered'`; (5b) `order_update {status:'cancelled'}` raises with "use order_cancel"; (5c) `order_cancel` on a delivered order raises with "cannot be cancelled"; (6) **from-quote seeding** — buyer1 creates an RFQ targeting sup1, sup1 quotes 5.25 USD, buyer1 accepts; `order_create({accepted_quote_id: quote_id})` returns a new order whose supplier_id, product_title, quantity, unit_price, currency, rfq_id, accepted_quote_id are all copied correctly; (7) `order_cancel` flips status to 'cancelled'; subsequent `order_update` raises with "cannot be updated"; (8) recursive forbidden-key leak diff over the full smoke payload (lists + gets across both viewer roles) is `[]` against `{email_primary, phones, contact_name, contact_role, website, sbi, sbi_total, total_score, pillar1_legal, pillar2_safety, pillar3_certs, pillar4_market}`. **Smoke prints `B8 SMOKE PASS`** on the final line. Cleanup deletes both orders, both message-thread fan-outs, the RFQ, and the four test users in `finally{}`; restores prior `claimed_by` on both fixture suppliers. **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors"; `pnpm build` ✓ Compiled in 35 s — **31 routes total** (was 28), new bundles `/api/v1/orders` 166 B / 103 kB, `/app/orders` 185 B / 106 kB, `/app/orders/[id]` 4.57 kB / 117 kB, `/app/orders/new` 3.83 kB / 116 kB; dashboard `/app` unchanged at 185 B / 106 kB; RFQ detail page unchanged at 2.51 kB / 115 kB (the new Create-order button is server-rendered, no client delta). **Data-moat metrics** (post-ship): tables +2 (`orders`, `order_milestones`); enums +2 (`order_status`, `order_milestone_kind`); RPCs +6 (`order_create`, `order_list`, `order_get`, `order_update`, `order_milestone_add`, `order_cancel`); API routes +1 (`/api/v1/orders`); migration count 28 → 29; zero scraper / source / SBI changes. **New / changed files**: `supabase/migrations/0029_orders.sql`, `app/api/v1/orders/route.ts`, `app/(app)/app/orders/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/app/orders/[id]/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/app/orders/new/page.tsx`, `components/order-create-form.tsx`, `components/order-milestone-form.tsx`, `components/order-status-editor.tsx`, `ops/_b8_smoke.py`, `ops/_b8_apply.sh`, `ops/_b8_run.sh`, modified `app/(app)/app/page.tsx` (dashboard tile), modified `app/(app)/app/rfqs/[id]/page.tsx` (Create-order button). Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions — 2026-05-30 (B8)

1. **Manual-only v1; no Maersk / MSC integration this spec.** `phases.md` line 66 explicitly defers carrier API integration to v2. We model `carrier_name` + `tracking_number` as free-text columns and `actual_ship_date` / `actual_delivery_date` as date columns so a future v2 integration can bulk-update them from a freight feed without any DDL — the data model is forward-compatible. A v2 spec will add `public.order_carrier_events` + an Inngest job to poll the feed; nothing in v1's shape blocks that.
2. **`rfq_id` and `accepted_quote_id` are nullable FKs, not arrays or pointers in jsonb.** An order has at most one parent RFQ + one parent quote, so the cardinality is clean. The FKs let `order_create` from-quote seeding verify the buyer owns the RFQ (`rfqs.buyer_id = auth.uid()`) without trusting client-supplied fields. Both columns are nullable because manual orders (no RFQ in history) are a first-class flow per the user spec.
3. **`total_value` is a generated stored column, not application-computed.** `numeric generated always as (case when unit_price is not null then quantity * unit_price end) stored` keeps the value in sync atomically with `quantity` / `unit_price` writes. Storing (not virtual) means existing `order_list` queries can read it without a derived expression; consumers (dashboards, future analytics) get one consistent number with no chance of TS drift. Cost: writes pay the multiply once; reads are free.
4. **Milestones are append-only via RPC; no UPDATE / DELETE policy or RPC.** The spec is event-sourced — a milestone records "this happened on this date". Editing or deleting a milestone would rewrite history and undermine the audit posture. If a buyer or supplier needs to correct a mistake they append a new milestone with `kind='custom'` and a clarifying note; the timeline shows both. The first milestone (`po_issued`) is auto-seeded by `order_create` so the timeline is never empty.
5. **`order_milestone_add` accepts buyer OR claimed supplier, role auto-resolved.** A single RPC that resolves the caller's relationship to the order (buyer via `buyer_id`, supplier via `suppliers.claimed_by`) is simpler than two RPCs (`order_milestone_add_buyer` + `order_milestone_add_supplier`) and matches the symmetric reality — both parties have equally valid milestone events to log. The RLS SELECT policy on `order_milestones` already mirrors the same predicate, so reads and writes use the same membership check.
6. **`order_update` and `order_cancel` are buyer-only, but milestones are shared.** The buyer is the principal of the order — they own the commercial relationship and the money; the supplier is fulfilling it. Status / dates / carrier / cancellation are commercial decisions the buyer makes. Milestones are operational events either party observes. Splitting authority along this seam matches the underlying business reality and avoids the supplier silently flipping `status='delivered'` (which has refund / contract implications).
7. **`order_update` cannot set `status='cancelled'`; cancellation goes through `order_cancel`.** Cancellation is a one-way state transition with its own checks (refuse if delivered, refuse if already cancelled). Modelling it as a separate RPC means: (a) the buyer UI surfaces it as an explicit destructive action (with `window.confirm`), not a dropdown option that can be misclicked; (b) we have one chokepoint to extend later with refund / restocking / notification flows.
8. **"Active orders" tile replaces the muted `Unread messages` slot on the dashboard.** The Unread messages tile was a B5 placeholder from before B6 messaging shipped; it had `muted` styling and the text "Messaging ships in Spec B6" which became stale once B6 went live on 29 May. The natural place to surface live "in flight" workload from B8 is exactly this slot. The dashboard now grids 4-up (`sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-4`): Saved · Active RFQs · Active orders · Unread messages (now linking to `/app/messages`, value=0 pending a real unread count RPC in a later spec).
9. **`order_create` always seeds the first milestone (`po_issued`).** The Milestones timeline would otherwise render empty for a brand-new draft order, which carries no information. Seeding `kind='po_issued', label='Order created', occurred_on=current_date` (with `notes='PO <number>'` when present) gives the timeline a canonical zero-point that buyers can recognise and that fixes the "is anything happening?" empty-state question.
10. **Migration `0029` applied via the per-migration `docker cp + python /tmp/_apply_stdin.py` pattern, not `etl migrate`.** Continues the convention from F3 / B1 / B2 / B4 / B7 — the from-scratch `etl migrate` path still trips on the pre-existing `entity_type` enum from migration 0001. Version-tracking remains parked.

- ✅ **Spec B7 — RFQs** shipped 30 May 2026. Seventh Phase-2 buyer spec per `context/phases.md` line 65 ("RFQ — buyer composes RFQ, sends to factory, receives quote"); no `feature-specs/spec-b7-*.md` exists, scope inferred from `frontend-design-spec.md` §9 + B6 messaging substrate (an RFQ thread reuses `message_threads` keyed by `rfq_id`; the column was reserved as nullable in B6's 0027 migration precisely for this spec). **New migration `supabase/migrations/0028_rfqs.sql`** introduces (a) enums `public.rfq_status('open','accepted','closed','cancelled')` + `public.rfq_quote_status('submitted','accepted','rejected','withdrawn')`; (b) `public.rfqs(id, buyer_id→auth.users on delete cascade, target_supplier_ids uuid[] not null check cardinality 1..50, product_title text not null, product_description, quantity numeric > 0, quantity_unit, target_unit_price (> 0 when present), currency text default 'USD' check `^[A-Z]{3}$`, ship_to_country, ship_by date, status rfq_status default 'open', accepted_quote_id uuid, created_at, updated_at)` with indexes `(buyer_id, created_at desc)`, GIN on `target_supplier_ids`, `(status)`; (c) `public.rfq_quotes(id, rfq_id→rfqs on delete cascade, supplier_id→suppliers on delete cascade, submitted_by→auth.users, unit_price > 0, currency, lead_time_days >= 0, moq >= 0, valid_until date, notes, status rfq_quote_status default 'submitted', created_at, updated_at, unique(rfq_id, supplier_id))` with indexes `(rfq_id)` + `(supplier_id)`. RLS enabled on both tables. SELECT policies: rfqs split into `pol_rfqs_select_buyer` (`using buyer_id = auth.uid()`) and `pol_rfqs_select_supplier` (`exists (suppliers s where s.claimed_by = auth.uid() and s.id = any(rfqs.target_supplier_ids))`); rfq_quotes split into `pol_rfq_quotes_select_buyer` (visible when parent RFQ's buyer matches caller) and `pol_rfq_quotes_select_supplier` (visible when caller has claimed the quote's supplier). **No INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies** on either table — every write path is a SECURITY DEFINER RPC, so direct PostgREST mutations are denied even for authenticated users. **No FK on `message_threads.rfq_id → rfqs(id)`** by deliberate decision: the column was created nullable in 0027 precisely to be RFQ-agnostic (B6 also writes non-RFQ threads with `rfq_id IS NULL`), and the column is only ever written from `rfq_create` below; adding a FK now would not change behaviour and would risk a long lock on the existing `message_threads` table for zero benefit. **RPCs (all `language plpgsql security definer set search_path = public`, `revoke from public`, `grant execute to authenticated`)**: `rfq_create(p_input jsonb) returns uuid` — gates on `profiles.role in ('buyer','admin')` with `errcode='42501'` on mismatch, parses & validates the full input doc (product_title 200-char cap, product_description 4000-char cap, quantity > 0 numeric, quantity_unit non-empty 32-char cap, optional target_unit_price > 0, currency `^[A-Z]{3}$` default USD, optional ship_to_country 64-char cap, optional ship_by date), asserts every target supplier is `is_published=true and is_sanctioned=false` (refuses the whole RFQ on any miss), inserts the RFQ row, then for each target supplier materialises a `message_threads` row keyed by `(buyer_id, supplier_id, new_rfq_id)` via the existing unique-with-nulls-not-distinct constraint and inserts the buyer + claimed-supplier `thread_participants` rows inline (this duplicates B6's `thread_open` logic on purpose: we already validated `is_published` above, and the whole RFQ + N threads + 2N participants must commit atomically). `rfq_list(p_status text default null) returns jsonb` — returns one entry per RFQ the caller can see (RLS does the visibility filter), enriched with `viewer_role ∈ ('buyer','supplier','both')`, `target_supplier_count`, `quote_count`; ordered by `created_at desc`. `rfq_get(p_id uuid) returns jsonb` — returns NULL when caller has no visibility, else returns `{id, product_title, ..., viewer_role, targets:[{id, slug, company_name, entity_type, city, district}], quotes:[...], thread_id}`; supplier viewer sees only their own quote in the `quotes` array (the buyer sees all). `rfq_quote_submit(p_rfq_id uuid, p_input jsonb) returns uuid` — gates on `profiles.role in ('supplier','admin')`, resolves the caller's claimed supplier inside `target_supplier_ids` (first match wins; multi-claim cases submit per supplier in separate calls), rejects when RFQ status ≠ 'open', upserts on `(rfq_id, supplier_id)` so a supplier can revise their quote until the buyer accepts. `rfq_quote_accept(p_quote_id uuid) returns void` — buyer must own the parent RFQ, RFQ status must be 'open'; flips the chosen quote to 'accepted', sibling submitted quotes to 'rejected', the parent RFQ to 'accepted', and writes `accepted_quote_id` in a single transaction. **New API route `app/api/v1/rfqs/route.ts`** (`runtime='nodejs'`, `dynamic='force-dynamic'`) — in-handler `requireAuth()` returns 401 for unauthenticated callers and lets the RPCs do the role gating internally. GET `?id=<uuid>` → `rfq_get`; GET `?status=open|accepted|closed|cancelled` (or no params) → `rfq_list`. POST dispatches on `action`: `create` validates the full payload (UUID_RE on every supplier id, length caps, currency 3-letter regex, numeric coercion) and forwards to `rfq_create`; `submit_quote` validates `rfq_id` + numeric coercion + currency + date parsing and forwards to `rfq_quote_submit`; `accept_quote` validates `quote_id` UUID and forwards to `rfq_quote_accept`. RPC error messages are mapped to HTTP codes: "not authenticated" → 401, "is not a (buyer|supplier)" / "does not own" / "no claimed supplier" → 403, "not found" → 404, else 400. **New page `app/(app)/app/rfqs/page.tsx`** — server component, calls `rfq_list`, renders the inbox as one list with per-row status Badge (active for 'open', success for 'accepted', alert for 'cancelled', neutral for 'closed') + "As supplier" Badge when `viewer_role !== 'buyer'` + quote/target counts + relative-time. Empty state directs the user to Discover. **New page `app/(app)/app/rfqs/[id]/page.tsx`** — async `params: Promise<{id:string}>` per Next.js 15; calls `rfq_get`; `notFound()` when NULL; renders header (title + status Badge + created/updated timestamps), Specification card (quantity / target_unit_price / ship_to / ship_by / description), Targeted suppliers card (one row per target with a buyer-only "Thread" button that links to the rfq-keyed `message_threads` row), Quotes card (one row per visible quote with supplier name + status Badge + unit_price/MOQ/lead-time/valid-until/notes). Buyer sees an `<AcceptQuoteButton/>` on each `submitted` quote when `rfq.status === 'open'`. **New page `app/(app)/app/rfqs/new/page.tsx`** — requires `?supplier=<uuid>`, resolves the supplier server-side (404 unless `is_published && !is_sanctioned`), renders `<RfqCreateForm/>` with the supplier pre-bound. **New client islands**: `components/rfq-create-form.tsx` (controlled inputs, POSTs `{action:'create', target_supplier_ids:[<id>], ...}` to `/api/v1/rfqs`, redirects to `/app/rfqs/[id]` on success); `components/accept-quote-button.tsx` (POSTs `{action:'accept_quote', quote_id}`, refreshes the server tree on success). **Supplier profile header now carries a primary `Request a quote` link** to `/app/rfqs/new?supplier=<id>` beside the existing `SaveButton`. **Dashboard "Active RFQs" tile** previously hardcoded to `0` + muted now reads `select count(*) from rfqs where status='open'` under the caller's RLS context and links to `/app/rfqs`. **Production smoke** via `ops/_b7_smoke.py` (uploaded to VPS, run inside the etl container) sets up 2 buyer users + 2 supplier users with `suppliers.claimed_by` flipped to point at them, then verifies: (0) buyer1 `rfq_list` returns the new RFQ with `viewer_role='buyer'`, `target_supplier_count=2`, `quote_count=0`; (1) buyer2 `rfq_list = []`, `rfq_get = NULL`, direct `select count(*) from rfqs where id = <rfq_id>` returns 0 under RLS; (2a) supplier S1 sees `viewer_role='supplier'`, submits a quote with unit_price + lead-time + MOQ + notes; (2b) supplier S2 sees the RFQ + can submit; supplier S2 `rfq_get` returns only their own quote; (2c) buyer's `rfq_quote_submit` call raises `InsufficientPrivilege` ("not a supplier"); (2d) a third supplier user whose claimed supplier is NOT in `target_supplier_ids` cannot see the RFQ at all and `rfq_quote_submit` raises `InsufficientPrivilege` ("no claimed supplier"); (3) buyer1 `rfq_quote_accept(quote_s1)` — verified via direct SELECT: `rfqs.status='accepted'`, `rfqs.accepted_quote_id=quote_s1`, `rfq_quotes.{quote_s1}.status='accepted'`, `rfq_quotes.{quote_s2}.status='rejected'`; (3b) S1 follow-up `rfq_quote_submit` raises `"rfq is not open for quotes"`; (3c) buyer1 follow-up `rfq_quote_accept(quote_s2)` raises `"rfq is not open"`; (4) `message_threads` has exactly 2 rows with `rfq_id = <new_rfq_id>` for sup1 + sup2 (the inline thread materialisation worked); (5) recursive forbidden-key leak diff over the full smoke payload is `[]` against `{email_primary, phones, contact_name, contact_role, website, sbi, sbi_total, total_score, pillar1_legal, pillar2_safety, pillar3_certs, pillar4_market}`. **Smoke prints `B7 SMOKE PASS`** on the final line. **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors"; `pnpm build` ✓ Compiled in 41 s — 28 routes total (was 24), new bundles `/app/rfqs` 181 B / 106 kB, `/app/rfqs/[id]` 2.51 kB / 115 kB, `/app/rfqs/new` 3.57 kB / 116 kB, `/api/v1/rfqs` 163 B / 103 kB. **Data-moat metrics** (post-ship): tables +2 (`rfqs`, `rfq_quotes`); enums +2 (`rfq_status`, `rfq_quote_status`); RPCs +5 (`rfq_create`, `rfq_list`, `rfq_get`, `rfq_quote_submit`, `rfq_quote_accept`); API routes +1 (`/api/v1/rfqs`); migration count 27 → 28; zero scraper / source / SBI changes. **New / changed files**: `supabase/migrations/0028_rfqs.sql`, `app/api/v1/rfqs/route.ts`, `app/(app)/app/rfqs/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/app/rfqs/[id]/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/app/rfqs/new/page.tsx`, `components/rfq-create-form.tsx`, `components/accept-quote-button.tsx`, `ops/_b7_smoke.py`, `ops/_b7_run.sh`, modified `app/(app)/app/page.tsx` (dashboard tile), modified `app/(app)/app/suppliers/[slug]/page.tsx` (Request-a-quote button). Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions — 2026-05-30 (B7)

1. **An RFQ is a row in `public.rfqs`; the conversation is reused from B6's `message_threads` keyed on `rfq_id`.** Considered modelling the RFQ as a special message_thread subtype with the RFQ fields hung off the thread row. Rejected because the cardinality is wrong: one RFQ targets N suppliers and creates N parallel conversation threads (one per buyer×supplier pair). Hanging the RFQ fields on the thread would duplicate them across N rows and require a join to enforce "all threads for this RFQ share the same product_title". Instead the RFQ is the parent (1 row) and the threads are children (N rows). The `rfq_id` column on `message_threads` was deliberately created nullable in B6 (column reserved, no FK) so this spec could land without altering B6's existing rows.
2. **`target_supplier_ids` is a `uuid[]` column on the RFQ, not a join table `rfq_targets(rfq_id, supplier_id)`.** Considered the canonical normalised shape (join table). Rejected because: (a) the RLS predicate `exists (s.claimed_by = auth.uid() and s.id = any(target_supplier_ids))` is one statement and one GIN index lookup; expressed against a join table it becomes a nested `exists` with two joins and an extra index hop; (b) the cardinality is capped (1..50) so the array does not unbound-grow; (c) we never need to mutate the target set after creation — accepting a quote freezes the RFQ; (d) `rfq_quotes` already provides the per-supplier child rows (one quote per supplier max via `unique(rfq_id, supplier_id)`), so the join table would be redundant with `rfq_quotes` on every non-pending RFQ. The GIN index `idx_rfqs_targets_gin` keeps the supplier-side RLS predicate cheap.
3. **No FK from `message_threads.rfq_id` to `rfqs(id)`.** Adding a FK would require all existing `message_threads` rows (from B6) to be re-validated, which on a non-trivial table risks an `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock during the constraint validation. The column is only ever written by `rfq_create` below (and only with a UUID we just inserted into `rfqs`), so the FK would never reject a write. The protection it would provide (cascade-on-delete) is also not desired: deleting an RFQ row should not silently delete the message threads — those threads contain buyer↔supplier communication that may need to be preserved for audit even when the RFQ is cancelled. We add `on delete cascade` on `rfq_quotes` (quotes are pure RFQ children with no independent meaning) but deliberately not on `message_threads`.
4. **`rfq_create` inlines the thread-materialisation logic instead of calling `thread_open` per target.** Considered `for v_supplier_id in array v_target_ids loop perform thread_open(v_supplier_id, v_rfq_id, v_product_title) end loop`. Rejected because `thread_open` re-validates `is_published` per supplier and re-resolves `claimed_by` per supplier — both of which `rfq_create` already did during the target-validation block. The inline path saves N redundant lookups per RFQ creation and keeps the whole RFQ-plus-N-threads-plus-2N-participants insert as a single atomic SQL function with one error path. Trade-off: B6's `thread_open` and the inline block now have two copies of the participant-insert pattern; if a third call site appears we should extract a private helper.
5. **`rfq_quote_submit` upserts on `(rfq_id, supplier_id)` instead of inserting new quote rows per revision.** A supplier may need to revise their quote (e.g. correct a typo, extend the validity date, lower the price) before the buyer accepts.
