The simpler shape — one append-only row per submission with a history trail — was considered but rejected because: (a) the buyer-facing quote list would need a `latest_per_supplier` filter on every read, which is a window-function query that doesn't index trivially; (b) the accept flow would need to know which historical row is "the current one"; (c) we get no real audit value from prior versions of a non-accepted quote — once accepted, the row is frozen by status='accepted' and the RFQ's transition to status='accepted' is the audit anchor. The unique constraint forces the upsert semantics and `updated_at` is bumped on every revision so a future audit surface can still observe revision velocity. Withdrawal is modelled as `status='withdrawn'` via a future RPC; this spec does not implement it because no UI consumes it yet.
6. **`rfq_quote_accept` is atomic across three tables in a single statement of work.** The accept flow updates the chosen quote (→'accepted'), every sibling submitted quote (→'rejected'), and the parent RFQ (→'accepted' + accepted_quote_id) — three table mutations. We do them in a single SECURITY DEFINER RPC body (auto-transactional in plpgsql) so a crash mid-flow cannot leave a partially-accepted RFQ. Buyer-side retry is safe because the second call hits the `rfq.status <> 'open'` guard and is rejected — there's no "accept the second quote after accepting the first" race because the first accept closed the RFQ. The smoke verifies both branches.
7. **The buyer-app stops short of supplier-app surfaces.** Spec B7 ships the buyer's compose / list / detail / accept surfaces but does not ship a supplier inbox or a supplier quote-composer UI. Suppliers can already see RFQs (via the rfq_list `viewer_role=supplier` branch) and submit quotes (via the POST API), but the supplier-app's own pages are out of scope for B7 (`phases.md` puts supplier-app under S1/S2). Until then, suppliers consume the RFQ + send the quote via the API directly. This keeps B7 scoped to one app surface and one user role.
8. **`profiles.role` (text enum) gates which RPCs each user may call, not RLS policies.** Considered making the RLS SELECT policies also branch on `role`. Rejected because RLS is a positive permission — adding a role check would not strengthen visibility (already gated on `buyer_id = auth.uid()` or `claimed_by = auth.uid()`) and would make the policy harder to reason about. The role check belongs on the write path ("only buyers can create RFQs; only suppliers can submit quotes") and lives inside `rfq_create` / `rfq_quote_submit`. This mirrors the B5/B6 pattern: read = RLS, write = SECURITY DEFINER RPC with role gate.

- ✅ **Spec B6 — Messages** shipped 29 May 2026. Sixth Phase-2 buyer spec per `context/phases.md` line 64 ("Messages (Supabase Realtime; thread per RFQ; 256-bit-encrypted-at-rest copy)"); no `feature-specs/spec-b6-*.md` exists, scope inferred from `frontend-design-spec.md` §8 + `architecture.md` (Realtime channels, `pgcrypto` already enabled in 0001) + B1–B5 precedent. **New migration `supabase/migrations/0027_messages.sql`** introduces (a) `public.message_threads(id, buyer_id→auth.users, supplier_id→public.suppliers, rfq_id null, subject, last_message_at, created_at, updated_at, unique nulls not distinct (buyer_id, supplier_id, rfq_id))` — one thread per (buyer, supplier, optional RFQ); (b) `public.thread_participants(thread_id, user_id, role check ('buyer','supplier','admin'), unique(thread_id, user_id))`; (c) `public.messages(id, thread_id, sender_id, body_ciphertext bytea, body_len int, created_at)` with `(thread_id, created_at desc)` index. RLS enabled on all three. SELECT policies: threads + messages = participant via `exists (… thread_participants where user_id = auth.uid())`; participants = `using (user_id = auth.uid())` (FIX after first apply: an `exists` self-reference on `thread_participants` causes infinite-recursion in the policy planner; the full participant list is only visible via SECURITY DEFINER RPCs). **No INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies** on any of the three tables — every write path is a `security definer` RPC, so direct PostgREST mutations are denied even for authenticated users (verified by smoke step 6). **Encryption at rest** is `pgcrypto::pgp_sym_encrypt(body, key, 'cipher-algo=aes256')`. The key is sourced via `current_setting('app.messages_key', true)` so it lives in the Postgres GUC, never in the application code or the database catalog. Production sets it via `alter database postgres set app.messages_key = '<random hex>'`; the smoke sets it via `set_config('app.messages_key', '<TEST_KEY>', false)` per-session. `_messages_key()` helper raises if NULL or length<16, so a misconfigured environment fails closed at the first send. **Column-grant hardening**: `revoke all on public.messages from anon, authenticated` then `grant select(id, thread_id, sender_id, body_len, created_at) to authenticated` — the ciphertext column is invisible to PostgREST and to Realtime payloads. Smoke step 4 verifies that a direct `select body_ciphertext` under `set role authenticated` raises `InsufficientPrivilege`, while `select id, thread_id, sender_id, body_len, created_at` succeeds. **RPCs (all `language plpgsql security definer set search_path = public, extensions`, `revoke from public`, `grant execute to authenticated`)**: `thread_open(p_supplier_id, p_rfq_id default null, p_subject default null) returns uuid` — idempotent on the `(buyer, supplier, rfq_id)` unique-with-nulls-not-distinct constraint, inserts buyer participant + supplier.claimed_by participant when present; `thread_send_message(p_thread_id, p_body) returns uuid` — participant check, length-trim 1..8000, encrypts and inserts with `body_len = length(v_trimmed)`; `thread_messages(p_thread_id, p_limit default 50, p_before default null) returns jsonb` — participant check, decrypts via `_messages_key()`, returns ascending `[{id, thread_id, sender_id, created_at, body, is_self}]`; `thread_list() returns jsonb` — caller's threads joined with `suppliers` (slug, company_name, entity_type::text, message_count); never returns any message body. **Trigger `_messages_bump_thread`** (AFTER INSERT on messages) keeps `message_threads.last_message_at` + `updated_at` current for the list-order. **Realtime publication**: `alter publication supabase_realtime add table public.messages` wrapped in a `do` block so the migration is idempotent if the table is already in the publication. Realtime authorisation is the table's SELECT RLS — a subscriber to `messages:thread_id=eq.<id>` only sees rows their RLS policy allows. Combined with the column-grant revoke, the Realtime payload carries only metadata; the client must call `thread_messages` to decrypt. **New `lib/supabase/browser.ts`** — memoised `getSupabaseBrowserClient()` wrapping `createBrowserClient(NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL, NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY)` from `@supabase/ssr` (already a dep from F3). Single instance per browser session. **New API route `app/api/v1/messages/route.ts`** (`runtime='nodejs'`, `dynamic='force-dynamic'`) — in-handler `requireBuyerOrAdmin()` (401 otherwise; middleware doesn't cover `/api/*`); GET no params → `thread_list`; GET `?thread_id=<uuid>[&before=<ts>][&limit=N]` → `thread_messages` with UUID_RE validation + limit clamp; POST `{action:"open", supplier_id, rfq_id?, subject?}` → `thread_open` with UUID_RE + 200-char subject cap; POST `{action:"send", thread_id, body}` → `thread_send_message` with 1..8000-char body cap; "not a participant" raised by an RPC maps to HTTP 403. **New page `app/(app)/app/messages/page.tsx`** — server component, RPC `thread_list`, renders the inbox as a Card grid (supplier name + entity-type Badge + message_count + last_message_at). **New page `app/(app)/app/messages/[thread]/page.tsx`** — async `params: Promise<{thread:string}>` per Next.js 15; UUID_RE check; `Promise.all([thread_list, thread_messages])` in one shot so the thread header + first 50 messages render on the server (decrypted via the user's RLS context); `notFound()` when `thread_messages` returns NULL (RLS rejected). (5) `thread_list` for user A returns exactly 1 thread with `supplier_id`, `message_count=2`, and zero contact-PII keys; (6) direct PostgREST `insert into public.messages` under `set role authenticated` is blocked by RLS (no insert policy) — OK; (7) recursive forbidden-key leak diff over the full smoke payload is `[]` against `{body_ciphertext, sbi, sbi_total, total_score, pillar1_legal, pillar2_safety, pillar3_certs, pillar4_market, email_primary, phones, contact_name, contact_role, website}`; (7b) `body_len` matches the plaintext lengths byte-for-byte ("hello from A" → 12, "reply from B" → 12). **Smoke prints `B6 SMOKE PASS`** on the final line. **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors"; `/api/v1/messages` gates in-handler; RLS gates Realtime + every SELECT; column grants gate the body_ciphertext column even from authorised participants on direct SELECT; encryption at rest gates the database snapshot / WAL / backup files; Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions — 2026-05-29 (B6)

1. **Encryption-at-rest is `pgcrypto::pgp_sym_encrypt` with the key in a Postgres GUC, not in the app process.** `architecture.md` lists `pgcrypto` as the only approved crypto extension; using it satisfies the hard rule against new tools. Putting the key in `current_setting('app.messages_key')` rather than `process.env.MESSAGES_KEY` means: (a) the Next.js process never sees the key — it cannot leak via a memory dump or an accidental log line, (b) rotating the key is `alter database postgres set app.messages_key = '<new>'` + a re-encryption batch, no app deploy, (c) any future RLS-bypassing admin tool that calls the RPCs still goes through `_messages_key()` so an unset GUC fails closed.
2. **Body ciphertext is a `bytea` column whose `SELECT` privilege is revoked from `anon` and `authenticated`.** Encryption alone is not enough — Supabase Realtime broadcasts INSERT row payloads to subscribers, and an authenticated participant would otherwise receive the ciphertext over the WebSocket. Revoking the column grant and granting only the metadata columns (`id, thread_id, sender_id, body_len, created_at`) means the Realtime payload carries only enough to fire a "new message arrived" event; the client must round-trip through `thread_messages` (server-side decryption under the user's RLS context) to see content. This also means a misconfigured / overly-permissive RLS policy on `messages` would still leak only ciphertext, not plaintext.
3. **All writes go through `security definer` RPCs; no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE RLS policies on any of the three tables.** Considered the symmetric pattern from B5 (RLS-policed writes via PostgREST). Rejected because: (a) `thread_open` needs to insert two `thread_participants` rows in a single transaction with the buyer's identity asserted server-side — a client-issued sequence of INSERTs can be raced or partially applied; (b) `thread_send_message` needs to call `_messages_key()` and `pgp_sym_encrypt` in the same statement as the INSERT, which is awkward to express as a client-side mutation; (c) the participant-check inside the RPCs is the single chokepoint for both reads and writes, so audit / future logging hooks have exactly one place to land. The downside (RLS is not the source of truth for writes) is mitigated by the smoke step 6 assertion that direct PostgREST inserts are blocked by the absence of an INSERT policy.
4. **Participants policy is `using (user_id = auth.uid())`, not `exists(… self-reference …)`.** First apply used `exists (select 1 from thread_participants tp2 where tp2.thread_id = thread_participants.thread_id and tp2.user_id = auth.uid())` so a participant could see their co-participants. This triggered Postgres's infinite-recursion guard the moment the `messages` SELECT policy (which itself does `exists (… from thread_participants …)`) ran — the planner recursed into the participants policy, which recursed back. Fix: participants only see their own row via RLS; the full participant list is exposed only through SECURITY DEFINER RPCs that have already validated the caller is a participant. Lesson: an RLS policy on table T must never reference T in a way that re-triggers itself; if you need set-membership semantics, do it inside a definer function.
5. **Realtime uses Postgres Changes filtered on `thread_id`, with RLS as the authorisation layer.** Supabase has two Realtime modes: Broadcast (app-managed channels) and Postgres Changes (CDC over the replication slot, RLS-gated). Considered Broadcast for lower latency. Rejected because Broadcast does not honour RLS — any subscriber who knows the channel name receives the payload, which would push authorisation back into the app layer for every channel join. Postgres Changes lets RLS do the gating, which is the same chokepoint protecting REST reads, so there is one policy to audit instead of two. The filter `thread_id=eq.<id>` is a server-side pre-filter for efficiency only; RLS still runs on every row and would deny a non-participant even if they crafted a filter for someone else's thread.
6. **Subject and body lengths are clamped at 200 and 8000 chars respectively, at both the API and the RPC level.** API-level clamps return a 400 immediately so the round-trip is cheap; RPC-level clamps via `raise exception` are the last line and would catch any future caller that bypasses the route (admin tool, scheduled job, etc.). 8000 is comfortably below Postgres's TOAST threshold so a single message stays in the main table page; subjects fit in any future email-mirror without truncation.

- ✅ **Spec B5 — Saved suppliers + dashboard** shipped 29 May 2026. Fifth Phase-2 buyer spec per `context/phases.md` line 63 ("Saved suppliers + dashboard (recent activity, alerts, saved list)"); scope inferred from `frontend-design-spec.md` §7 + B1–B4 precedent because no `feature-specs/spec-b5-*.md` exists. **New migration `supabase/migrations/0026_saved_suppliers_dashboard.sql`** introduces (a) `public.saved_suppliers(id uuid pk, owner_id uuid → auth.users on delete cascade, supplier_id uuid → public.suppliers on delete cascade, created_at timestamptz, unique(owner_id, supplier_id))` with two b-tree indexes `(owner_id, created_at desc)` + `(supplier_id)`, RLS enabled, three policies (`pol_saved_suppliers_select_self` / `_insert_self` / `_delete_self`) all keyed by `auth.uid() = owner_id`; (b) `public.buyer_saved_list(p_sort text, p_limit int, p_offset int) returns table` — `language sql stable security definer set search_path = public`, mirrors the `discover_suppliers` column whitelist plus `saved_at`, scopes by `auth.uid()`, uses `sbi_scores.total` as internal tiebreaker only (SBI never appears in the RETURNS list); (c) `public.buyer_dashboard() returns jsonb` — `language plpgsql stable security definer`, returns `{saved_count, recent_saved[6], alerts[], recent_activity[20]}`. The dashboard composes activity from **existing tables only** (no new history table): `saved_suppliers.created_at` (`kind='saved'`), `certifications.created_at` in last 60 days for saved suppliers (`cert_added`), `certifications.expires_on` between 60 days ago and today (`cert_expired`), and `rsc_remediation.fetched_at` in last 60 days (`rsc_updated`). Alerts are certifications expiring on saved suppliers within the next 30 days. Both functions are `revoke all from public` + `grant execute to authenticated`. **New API route `app/api/v1/saved/route.ts`** — `runtime='nodejs'`, `dynamic='force-dynamic'`, exports POST (body `{supplier_id}` upserts) + DELETE (`?supplier_id=` deletes). Middleware matcher does not cover `/api/*`, so both handlers call `getServerRole()` in-handler and 401 on anything other than `buyer`/`admin`; the supplier_id is validated against a UUID regex before touching the DB; both writes go through the user-scoped `createSupabaseServerClient()` so RLS enforces ownership a second time at the database. **New page `app/(app)/app/saved/page.tsx`** — server component, RPC-driven result-card grid with sort selector (`recent` / `receipts` / `completeness` / `name`) and pagination, each row carries an inline `SaveButton shape='icon'` for one-click unsave. **Rewrote `app/(app)/app/page.tsx`** — replaces the F2 placeholder with the canonical dashboard: three stat tiles (`Saved suppliers` linked to `/app/saved`; `Active RFQs` = 0 + meta "ships in Spec B7"; `Unread messages` = 0 + meta "ships in Spec B6"), an Alerts panel that renders only when `doc.alerts.length > 0` (amber WarningCircle list of expiring certs), a saved-suppliers grid of the 6 most recent mini-cards with the `ReceiptsRing`, and a unified Recent activity timeline (Phosphor `Star` / `Certificate` / `Clock` / `ShieldCheck` glyphs tinted via existing semantic-colour utilities, `fmtRelative` for `Xd ago` mono labels). **New client island `components/save-button.tsx`** (`'use client'`) wraps `Button` with optimistic UI per §15.4 — toggles local state on click, fetches POST/DELETE `/api/v1/saved`, rolls back + surfaces the error message in the button `title` on non-2xx, supports `shape='full'` (label + star) and `shape='icon'` (compact circle for card rows). **Updated `app/(app)/app/discover/page.tsx`** to (i) query `saved_suppliers` once per request for the visible row IDs (RLS auto-scopes to the caller), (ii) restructure `<ResultCard>` from a single `<Link>` wrapper into a `<Card>` with the link and the `SaveButton` as siblings inside `<CardContent>` (invalid-HTML fix: anchors must not nest interactive buttons). **Updated `app/(app)/app/suppliers/[slug]/page.tsx`** — same restructure on `<ProfileHeader>`: a `<SaveButton shape='full'>` lives top-right of the company name, with the saved state fetched server-side via a single `.maybeSingle()` read on `saved_suppliers`. **Production smoke** via `ops/_b5_smoke.py` (uploaded + run inside `sourcebd-etl-run-aab9f805fe8d`): (1) anon select returns 0 rows under reset role; (2) user A insert visible only to A; (3) user B insert visible only to B; (4) cross-owner insert (B trying to write owner_id=A) blocked by RLS (`InsufficientPrivilege`); (5) `buyer_saved_list` returns 1 row for A and 1 row for B, isolated; **forbidden-key leak diff `[]`** against `{email_primary, phones, contact_name, contact_role, website, sbi, sbi_total, total_score, pillar1_legal, pillar2_safety, pillar3_certs, pillar4_market}`; (6) `buyer_dashboard()` payload has exactly `{saved_count, recent_saved, alerts, recent_activity}`, `saved_count=1`, `activity_kinds=['saved']`, recursive key-set leak diff `[]`; (7) unsave round-trip → `buyer_saved_list` empty. Smoke uses `set role authenticated` + `set_config('request.jwt.claims', '{"sub":<uid>}', false)` to simulate session JWTs; fake `auth.users` rows are cleaned up in the finally block. **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean (one fix during build — `as const` on a discriminated-union return type had to move to the outer object literal); `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors"; `pnpm build` ✓ Compiled in 25.4s, **21 routes** (was 19 after B4) — new entries `/app/saved` (ƒ dynamic, 128 B / 117 kB First Load JS) + `/api/v1/saved` (ƒ dynamic, 149 B), dashboard `/app` (171 B / 106 kB), profile `/app/suppliers/[slug]` rose 716 B → 4.53 kB (now ships the SaveButton client island), discover `/app/discover` unchanged at 128 B / 117 kB shared chunk. **Security posture**: the saved-set is the first per-user mutable surface in Phase 2, so it doubles up — RLS keyed by `auth.uid()` is the source of truth, and the route handlers do their own role check before writing; the dashboard RPC re-reads `auth.uid()` so even a leaked function execute grant cannot return another user's saved set; SBI is consumed only in the `buyer_saved_list` ORDER BY and stays inside the function body; no contact PII is referenced by any new file. **Data-moat metrics row** (post-ship): suppliers 10,186 / sources tier1+2+3 unchanged from B4; **`saved_suppliers` rows = 0** (cleaned by smoke); RPC count +2 (`buyer_saved_list`, `buyer_dashboard`); table count +1; API routes +1. **New / changed files**: `supabase/migrations/0026_saved_suppliers_dashboard.sql`, `app/api/v1/saved/route.ts`, `app/(app)/app/saved/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/app/page.tsx` (rewrite), `app/(app)/app/discover/page.tsx` (savedSet lookup + ResultCard restructure), `app/(app)/app/suppliers/[slug]/page.tsx` (ProfileHeader SaveButton), `components/save-button.tsx`, `ops/_b5_smoke.py` (one-shot smoke; kept for future debug), `ops/_b5_probe.py` (one-shot post-apply probe).

### Architectural decisions — 2026-05-29 (B5)

1. **No history / event-log table.** The B5 spec asks for an activity feed; the obvious move is a generic `events` table that scrapers + UI both write to. Rejected. Composing the feed from existing column timestamps (`saved_suppliers.created_at`, `certifications.created_at`, `certifications.expires_on`, `rsc_remediation.fetched_at`) avoids (a) a second write per cert / RSC ingest in every Phase-0 scraper (huge blast radius), (b) a future migration to backfill the table from historical raw rows. Cost: we cannot show "completeness jumped 5%" or "a new register tag was added" because we never recorded the prior value. Acceptable for Phase 2 — the four event kinds we *can* derive cover the user need ("something happened on a supplier I care about"). If buyers later demand richer diffs, that is its own spec.

2. **`saved_suppliers` writes go through PostgREST + RLS, not through a `security definer` RPC.** The list and dashboard reads use `security definer` because they need to consult `sbi_scores` for ordering. Inserts and deletes do not — there is no SBI to consult, ownership is the only invariant, and Postgres' own row-level policies are the canonical enforcement point for ownership. Keeping the write path as a plain `INSERT` / `DELETE` over the user-scoped supabase client means the `pol_saved_suppliers_*_self` policies are exercised on every request, and the API route remains a thin gate (auth + UUID validation) that we can read in 90 seconds.

3. **`SaveButton` is the first true client island on the buyer surfaces.** Discover, Match, and the supplier profile were all server-component-only up to now. The save action is inherently per-user, optimistic, and reversible — putting it through a Server Action would still cost a full round trip and would not give us the local toggle-then-revert behaviour §15.4 calls for. Cost: `/app/suppliers/[slug]` grew 716 B → 4.53 kB and `/app/discover` shares the same client chunk; both stay well inside the 122 kB First Load budget. Naming it `SaveButton` (not `SaveStar`) keeps the door open for a future `shape='full'` text variant on the profile without renames.

4. **Wrapping the entire result card in `<Link>` was already invalid HTML; B5 forced us to fix it.** The B1 ResultCard wrapped the whole card in a single `<Link>`, which is technically OK as long as nothing interactive sits inside. Adding a button forced the split. The new structure (a flex row with `<Link>` on one side and `<SaveButton>` on the other) is the pattern the saved-list page uses as well, and the marketing `(marketing)/discover` page already uses the same pattern. One pattern, three places.

5. **Stat tiles for RFQs / messages render `0` instead of being hidden.** The dashboard spec lists three tiles. RFQs ship in B7 and messages in B6, so the temptation is to hide them until then. Rejected: a 1-of-3 grid looks empty and leaks the build order. The `0` + meta caption ("ships in Spec B6") sets buyer expectations and keeps the layout stable as we light up the surfaces one at a time.

- ✅ **Spec B4 — Smart Match wizard** shipped 29 May 2026. Fourth Phase-2 buyer spec. New route `/app/match` (server component shell `app/(app)/app/match/page.tsx` + `'use client'` wizard `smart-match-wizard.tsx`) presents a 3-step form (Step 1 Product: free-text product + multi-select entity type; Step 2 Requirements: certs / registries / RSC % min / sewing-machines min / city / district; Step 3 Review: summary chip row + "Find matches" submit). Wizard posts JSON to **new route handler `app/api/v1/match/route.ts`** (POST, `runtime='nodejs'`, `dynamic='force-dynamic'`), which auth-checks via `getServerRole()` (middleware matcher does not cover `/api/*`), validates the payload inline against the same allow-lists used by the SQL function (entity types ∈ {factory, buying_house}; registries ∈ {BGMEA, BKMEA, BTMA, BGAPMEA, EPB, RSC}; certs ∈ {wrap, oeko_tex, gots, sa8000}; `rsc_min` clamped 0–100; `min_machines` 0–100,000; strings trimmed + length-capped), and invokes the RPC. **New migration `supabase/migrations/0025_buyer_smart_match_rpc.sql`** adds `public.buyer_smart_match(p_input jsonb) returns jsonb` — `language plpgsql stable security definer set search_path = public`, `revoke all from public` + `grant execute to anon, authenticated`. The function applies hard filters (`is_published`, `not is_sanctioned`, optional entity_type / city / district via `ilike`) and computes a soft `match_score` = count of the 5 user-supplied criteria the supplier satisfies (certs → valid kinds; registries → `v_supplier_registry_ids` direct or RSC-inherited; rsc → `rsc_remediation.progress_pct >= v_rsc_min`; machines → `suppliers.machines_sewing >= v_min_machines`; product → `principal_products[] ilike '%v_product%'`). When the user supplied at least one criterion, suppliers with `match_score = 0` are dropped; when no criteria are supplied the function returns top-quality suppliers ordered purely by the internal tiebreakers. **Internal ranking**: `match_score desc, sb.total desc nulls last, t13_source_count desc, company_name asc` — the SBI tiebreaker keeps the same alpha/beta/gamma posture as B1's `discover_suppliers`: `sbi_scores.total` is consumed by `ORDER BY` inside the function body and **never appears in the `RETURNS` payload**. The returned `jsonb` document is `{criteria_count, total, results:[{id, slug, company_name, entity_type, city, district, completeness_pct, t13_source_count, source_tags, match_score, match_reasons:string[]}, …]}` — `match_reasons` are human-readable receipts assembled server-side per row in display order (`Certified: WRAP`, `BGMEA-verified · #6713`, `RSC remediation 100%`, `870+ sewing machines`, `Makes knitwear`). Contact PII (`email_primary` / `phones` / `contact_name` / `contact_role` / `website`) is excluded at the function level — those bytes never leave Postgres on this surface. **Wizard UI** reuses F1 primitives only (`Card`, `Button`, `Tag`, `Badge`, `ReceiptsRing`); the only non-primitive UI is a small inline `<StepBar>` (numeric pills + check glyph), a `<CheckboxGroup>` toggle-pill component, and a `<Field>` label wrapper. Result rows are clickable `Card`s linking to `/app/suppliers/${slug}` and surface the matched-receipts row as a green `Tag tone="green"` list under the existing pill row. No URL params, no client router — reload resets the wizard, which matches the design-spec intent that Smart Match is a session-local brief. **Smoke on production** via `ops/_b4_smoke.py` (uploaded to VPS, copied into the etl container) across 4 scenarios: (a) **factory + WRAP + BGMEA** → 5 matches, every row shows `Certified: WRAP | BGMEA-verified · #<reg>`, top 3 `sm-sourcing` / `robintex-bangladesh` / `ananta-apparels` (score=2, t13 7–8); (b) **buying-house only** → 5 BH rows, `criteria_count=0` so all `match_score=0`, ranked by SBI/t13 internally; (c) **OEKO-TEX + RSC≥80 + 200+ machines** → 5 matches with all three receipts populated (e.g. `apparel-promoters` score=3, t13=6, `Certified: OEKO-TEX | RSC remediation 100% | 2900+ sewing machines`); (d) **no criteria** → top 3 by SBI/t13 tiebreaker. **Zero PII / SBI key leaks** — the smoke explicitly diffs each row's keys against `{email_primary, phones, contact_name, contact_role, website, sbi, sbi_total, total_score}` and prints `leaks=[]` for every row in every scenario. **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors"; `pnpm build` ✓ Compiled in 29.3s, **19 routes** (was 17 after B3) — new entries `/app/match` (○ static, 9.19 kB / 122 kB First Load JS) + `/api/v1/match` (ƒ dynamic, 149 B); shared first-load JS unchanged at 102 kB. The 9.19 kB on `/app/match` is the wizard client island — the 3-step form state, 6 Phosphor icons, the F1 client primitives — within the design budget for a multi-step interactive surface. **Security posture**: middleware still gates `/app/*`; `/api/v1/match` enforces buyer/admin auth in-handler via `getServerRole()` (returns 401 on anon); allow-list validation rejects unknown enum values *before* the RPC call so the function only ever sees normalised arrays; the RPC's `security definer` reads RLS-locked `sbi_scores` / `source_records` / `certifications` / `rsc_remediation` only to assemble the buyer-safe whitelist; never serialises SBI to the wire; never surfaces contact PII. **New / changed files**: `supabase/migrations/0025_buyer_smart_match_rpc.sql`, `app/(app)/app/match/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/app/match/smart-match-wizard.tsx`, `app/api/v1/match/route.ts`, `ops/_b4_smoke.py` (one-shot smoke; kept for future debug), `ops/_apply_0025.sh` + `ops/_run_b4_smoke.sh` (VPS apply / smoke wrappers, same pattern as prior specs). Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions — 2026-05-29 (B4)

1. **Matcher is a `security definer` SQL function, not TypeScript ranking in the route handler.** Same forcing constraint as B1: `sbi_scores` is admin-RLS, so any TS-side `.order('sbi.total')` would have to embed the value in the SELECT and put it on the wire. A `security definer` function with a tight `RETURNS jsonb` shape is the only way to use SBI as an internal tiebreaker while honouring the §0 invariant. The function name is the contract; any future PR that adds `sb.total` to the returned `jsonb_build_object` is a review blocker.
2. **`match_score` is a transparent count of user criteria met, not a proprietary score.** The number a buyer sees in the result Badge is exactly "how many of the boxes you ticked did this supplier check". It carries no SourceBD opinion, no weight curve, no normalisation — there is nothing to dispute and nothing for a buyer to mistake for a creditworthiness rating. This is the receipts-first posture from the 2026-05-20 SBI doctrine, applied at the matcher layer: opinions stay internal; receipts ship.
3. **Match reasons are assembled server-side in SQL, not in the route handler or the client.** Building the strings in Postgres means the wire payload already carries `["Certified: WRAP", "BGMEA-verified · #6713", …]` ready to render — the client never sees raw cert kinds, raw RSC percentages, or registry IDs that need formatting decisions. Localisation / wording changes happen in one place (the migration). The TS layer is dumb display.
4. **Hard filters (entity / city / district / sanctioned / published) live at the function level; soft scoring lives in the same function as a CTE pipeline.** Splitting hard vs soft across DB + TS would force two passes and a second round trip. One function, one round trip, one query plan; the `with_reasons` CTE only runs against the `kept` rows so the receipts-build cost is bounded by `v_limit`.
5. **The wizard is a single client island, not a multi-route flow.** Considered 3 separate URLs (`/app/match/product`, `/app/match/requirements`, `/app/match/review`) with `<form action>` per step. Rejected: state would either need URL persistence (long ugly query strings every step) or a server session (new infra). A single component using `useState` keeps each step's panel a server-rendered-on-first-paint shell and gives the buyer instant Back / Next without round trips. Cost is +9 kB of client JS — inside budget for the only interactive surface in Phase 2 to date.
6. **The 3-step boundary follows the design spec, not the underlying data shape.** Step 1 (product + entity type) is the buyer's brief in plain language; Step 2 (certs / registries / RSC / machines / location) is the verifiable requirements; Step 3 is review-and-submit. The form could trivially be a single screen, but the design spec frames Smart Match as a wizard for non-expert buyers; the step boundary lowers the perceived cost-per-question and makes the "why matched" payoff feel earned.
7. **`/api/v1/match` auth-checks in-handler instead of being added to `middleware.ts`'s matcher.** Adding `/api/v1/:path*` to the matcher would route every future v1 API call through the role-fetch path; some of those will need different gating (anon-allowed liveness endpoints, e.g. `/api/health` already lives outside `/app/*`). Per-route auth keeps the contract local and visible at the file where it matters.
8. **No new dependency for input validation.** `architecture.md` lists `zod` as part of the stack, but the spec's hard rule is "no new tools / no new dependencies" without explicit confirmation. Hand-rolled allow-list checks in the route handler are ~50 lines, exactly cover the form's shape, and mirror the validation already done in `app/(app)/app/discover/page.tsx`. The same allow-lists are *also* enforced inside the SQL function (clamp ranges, drop empty arrays), so the route validation is defence-in-depth, not the only line of defence.
9. **Migration `0025` was applied via the per-migration `docker cp + python /tmp/_apply_stdin.py` pattern, not `etl migrate`.** Continues the convention from F3 / B1 / B2 — the from-scratch `etl migrate` path trips on the pre-existing `entity_type` enum from migration 0001. A version-tracking table to fix that is parked as a separate piece of work, not in scope for B4.


- ✅ **Spec B3 — Buying-house profile** shipped 28 May 2026. Third Phase-2 buyer spec. Implemented as a minimal diff on top of B2 per the IA decision in `frontend-design-spec.md` §3 ("`/app/suppliers/[slug]` (same route, branches on entity_type)") — same route, same `public.buyer_supplier_profile(p_slug)` RPC (migration 0024 already returns `entity_type::text`), no new migration, no new client code. The BH branch adds **one conditional `TabsTrigger` + `TabsContent` pair labelled "Partner factories"** inserted between Documents and Contact, gated on `payload.supplier.entity_type === "buying_house"`. The tab renders a single `<Card>` (`<CardTitle>Partner factories</CardTitle> <CardMeta>Reserved · Phase 2</CardMeta>`) with the body copy "Partner-factory disclosures will land with the buying-house relationship graph in a later Phase 2 spec. We don't show a list here until we have first-party evidence linking each factory to this buying house." — verbatim execution of §4.8's "Partner-factories tab is FUTURE" instruction. **No query against `bh_factory_relationships`** (the table doesn't exist; building logic against a non-existent table would violate the no-guesswork rule). Entity-type chip behaviour from B2 already covers BH (`Badge tone="neutral"` + `entityLabel("buying_house") === "Buying house"`), so the header diff is zero. Compliance tab's "omit empty cards" rule from B2 handles the typically-sparse BH Compliance state for free (no `RscCard` when `rsc_remediation` is NULL, no Certifications card when empty, no Documents tab at all when `compliance_documents` is empty — all already true for BH rows in the live DB). **Smoke test on production** via `ops/_b3_smoke.py` against the three highest-completeness published BH rows (FM Fashion, Explore Fashion Ltd., Caesar International — all 47% completeness): each returned `entity_type='buying_house'` with 2–4 pills, 1 cert, 2–4 provenance rows, `t13_source_count` 2–3, and **zero forbidden contact-PII keys** (`email_primary`/`phones`/`contact_name`/`contact_role`/`website`) in the payload — same RLS+whitelist posture as B2. Total published BH cohort: **1,444 suppliers** (vs 7,721 factories per the 22 May F2 backfill). **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors"; `pnpm build` ✓ Compiled in 51s, **17 routes** (unchanged from B2 — no new route added) — `/app/suppliers/[slug]` stays at **716 B / 120 kB First Load JS**, byte-for-byte identical to the B2 baseline because `PartnerFactoriesTab` is a pure server component built from the existing `Card` primitives (no new client island, no new tokens, no new dependencies). **New / changed files**: `app/(app)/app/suppliers/[slug]/page.tsx` (added `PartnerFactoriesTab` server component + 2 conditional JSX blocks + 4-line header doc comment about the BH branch), `ops/_b3_smoke.py` (one-shot BH RPC smoke; kept for future debug). Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions — 2026-05-28 (B3)

1. **Same route, same RPC, branch on `entity_type` — option (a) from `frontend-design-spec.md` §3 IA.** Considered creating a parallel `/app/buying-houses/[slug]` route with a separate `buyer_buying_house_profile` RPC. Rejected: the spec's IA table explicitly maps B3 to `/app/suppliers/[slug] (same route, branches on entity_type)`, and the canonical anatomy in §4.8 says "Same anatomy as B2, with these differences" — duplicating the route would force every future profile improvement to be implemented twice and would lie about the entity-versus-route split (a buying house *is* a supplier in this DB; `suppliers.entity_type` is the discriminator, not the URL prefix). The migration-0024 RPC already returns `entity_type::text` for exactly this reason.
2. **Partner Factories tab is reserved with a static empty state, not stubbed with fake data or hidden.** `bh_factory_relationships` is named in `phases.md` line 61 but does not exist in the live schema (verified by grep — there is no `0024*relationships*.sql` and no equivalent view). Three options were on the table: (i) hide the tab entirely until the BH relationship-graph spec ships, (ii) stub it with brand-attribution rows as a proxy (BH rows do not currently carry brand attributions in prod, and even if they did, brand attribution is not a partner-factory relationship), (iii) render the tab with a reserved-slot empty state pointing forward. Chose (iii) per §4.8's verbatim instruction "Partner-factories tab is FUTURE … Reserve the slot with an explicit empty state". (i) would make the spec a no-op since the rest of the BH variant is already a free consequence of B2's "omit empty cards" rule. (ii) would violate the source-trust hierarchy by manufacturing a relationship that isn't documented anywhere.
3. **No new migration.** The 0024 RPC's `supplier.entity_type` field was already cast to text and returned in the wire shape (line 196 of `0024_buyer_supplier_profile_rpc.sql`). The page TypeScript type already enumerated `"factory" | "buying_house" | "unknown"`. The entire B3 implementation is one server component + two conditional JSX blocks gated on a discriminator that was already on the wire.
4. **Tab order: Compliance · Overview · Capacity? · Documents? · Partners? · Contact.** Partners sits between Documents and Contact (not at the start, not at the end). Rationale: Contact remains the rightmost tab across all entity types so muscle-memory across F3 anonymous, B2 factory, and B3 BH profiles is identical; Partners is the *new* BH-specific surface so placing it adjacent to Contact (the last gated/reserved surface) groups the two "look here for what's unlocked next" affordances together.
5. **Smoke against live BH cohort, not synthetic fixtures.** Used the three highest-completeness real BH rows (FM Fashion, Explore Fashion Ltd., Caesar International) instead of inventing a buying-house fixture, because the goal was to verify the RPC + branch behave correctly against the production data distribution (sparse Compliance, no Documents, present pills + provenance) — the only state that ships.



- ✅ **Spec B2 — Factory profile** shipped 28 May 2026. Second Phase-2 buyer spec. Buyer-authenticated supplier profile at `/app/suppliers/[slug]` rendering the canonical anatomy in `frontend-design-spec.md` §4 (Header + 5 tabs: **Compliance** default · Overview · Capacity · Documents · Contact-gated). New route file `app/(app)/app/suppliers/[slug]/page.tsx` is a server component (`dynamic='force-dynamic'`) that awaits Next.js 15's async `params`, calls the new `public.buyer_supplier_profile(p_slug)` RPC, and `notFound()`'s on a NULL return (unknown slug or `is_published=false`). One DB read per profile — the RPC returns the entire profile document as a single `jsonb`. Reuses every F1 primitive (`Card`, `Tabs`, `Tag`, `Badge`, `Button`, `ReceiptsRing`); zero new tokens, zero new dependencies. The `<Tabs>` Radix wrapper from F1 is the only client island on the page (716 B / 120 kB First Load JS — the +700 B over Discover is the Radix Tabs hydration boundary). **Header (§4.1)**: Receipts Ring (size 64) with `t13_source_count` as the centre payload — never the SBI numeric · company name (font-display 32px) · entity-type Badge · location · `Completeness {N}%` meta · parent-group link (when present) routing to `/app/discover?group=…` · pill row from `v_supplier_registry_ids` with dashed-border variant for `inherited_from IS NOT NULL` rows (RSC sibling / extension wedge, migration 0021) · red sanctions banner above the tab bar when `suppliers.is_sanctioned = true`. **Compliance tab (§4.3, default)**: 6 cards rendered conditionally — Sanctions hits (top of tab in red when present, currently 0 in prod), Registry pills (expanded), Certifications (per-cert card with kind badge + expiry chip + cert no + issuer + scope + "View certificate" external link; **OEKO-TEX skips the expiry chip entirely** per §4.3 ("with_expiry = 0; treat as evergreen until reissue")), RSC remediation (horizontal progress bar with industry-median 95% tick + workers / status / training stats + four inspection-doc chips + CAP chip + `Boiler n/a` muted tag when fire/structural/electrical are present but boiler is not), Brand attribution ("Disclosed on {brand}" — not "Approved by"), Provenance footer as `<details>` collapsed by default with the source count in the summary. Empty cards are omitted entirely; never "n/a". **Overview tab (§4.4)**: primary address from `address_raw` with `+ N other addresses on file` disclosure from `v_supplier_addresses` (factory/mailing/registered) · established date rendered verbatim from the text column · factory_types and principal_products pill rows (top 4 + overflow) · BEPZA zone · group link. **Capacity tab (§4.5)**: entire tab hidden when no capacity field is present; otherwise 2-up stat tiles for whichever of `machines_sewing` / `production_capacity_dozen_yearly` / `production_capacity_pcs_day` / `employees_total` / `employees_male`+`employees_female` are populated. **Documents tab (§4.6)**: only rendered when `compliance_documents` has rows for this supplier; grouped by `doc_type` (fire/structural/electrical/boiler/cap), each row showing `fetched_at` + "Open mirror" (preferred) + "Open original (RSC)" + file size. **Contact tab (§4.7)**: gated empty-state with "Contact details unlocked on paid plans" copy + primary Button linking to `/pricing?from=/app/suppliers/{slug}`. **No contact PII is fetched from the database for this surface** — the RPC's `jsonb_build_object('supplier', ...)` whitelist deliberately excludes `email_primary`, `phones`, `contact_name`, `contact_role`, `website`; there is nothing in the browser payload to un-blur (code-standards.md hard rule). "Reviews" tab from `phases.md` line 60 is **omitted entirely** per `frontend-design-spec.md` §4.2 ("No review table exists; do not stub a UI for it"); "Media" from the same line maps to the Documents tab per §4.6. **New migration `supabase/migrations/0024_buyer_supplier_profile_rpc.sql`** adds `public.buyer_supplier_profile(p_slug text) returns jsonb` — `language sql stable security definer set search_path = public`, `revoke all from public` + `grant execute to anon, authenticated`. The function assembles 9 sub-arrays via CTEs (`s` / `ring` / `pills` / `certs` / `rsc` / `brands` / `sanc` / `provenance` / `addresses` / `docs`) and returns a single `jsonb_build_object(...)` document; reads the RLS-locked `source_records` / `certifications` / `rsc_remediation` / `sanctions_screening` / `sanctions_list_entries` / `compliance_documents` strictly within the function body. The function does NOT join `sbi_scores` at all (profile has no ordering — SBI would only enter the result wire, forbidden by §0). Applied to Supabase Singapore via the standard `docker cp + docker exec sourcebd-etl-run-aab9f805fe8d python /tmp/_apply_stdin.py` pattern. Smoke test on production via `ops/_spec_b2_smoke.py` against the three highest-completeness published suppliers (ABIR FASHIONS, ACTIVE COMPOSITE MILLS, ALLIANCE KNIT COMPOSITE): each returned a complete document with non-zero pills/certs/rsc/provenance/addresses/documents and zero forbidden keys (no `email_primary`, `phones`, `contact_name`, `contact_role`, `sbi_total`, `pillar_*` anywhere in the payload string); unknown-slug call returned NULL as designed. **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors"; `pnpm build` ✓ Compiled in 27.5s, **17 routes** (was 16 after B1) — new entry `/app/suppliers/[slug]` (ƒ dynamic, 716 B / 120 kB First Load JS, the +700 B over Discover is the Radix Tabs hydration island); shared first-load JS unchanged at 102 kB. **Security posture**: server enforces published-only at the function level (`where is_published = true`); `security definer` reads RLS-locked tables strictly within the function body and returns only the buyer-safe jsonb whitelist; middleware already gates `/app/*` to authenticated users; PII columns are excluded at the function level, not the route level — even an admin who calls this RPC will not get contact fields back, by design (contacts live in a separate paid-tier RPC to ship later); `notFound()` on unknown slug or unpublished supplier prevents partial-page leakage. **Receipts Ring contract honoured**: the centre payload passed to `<ReceiptsRing sources={payload.t13_source_count} size={64}/>` is the count of distinct Tier 1–3 sources from the function's `ring` CTE, never the SBI numeric or any derivation. **Public anonymous profile at `/suppliers/[slug]` (F3) is preserved untouched** — the two routes coexist; the public one is intentionally simpler with CSS-blurred contacts as the buyer-acquisition surface, the authenticated one is the moat tab and uses the RPC. **New / changed files**: `supabase/migrations/0024_buyer_supplier_profile_rpc.sql`, `app/(app)/app/suppliers/[slug]/page.tsx`, `ops/_spec_b2_smoke.py` (one-shot smoke test for the RPC; kept for future debug). Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions — 2026-05-28 (B2)

1. **The RPC returns one `jsonb` document, not a `returns table` row.** A profile is nine logically-distinct collections (pills, certs, rsc, brands, sanctions, provenance, addresses, documents, supplier scalars) that share exactly one parent row. Expressing that as a flat `returns table` would either denormalise (N×M rows per profile, with the page de-duping in JS) or require nine RPC calls. A single `jsonb` document is one query plan, one round trip, one type to model in TS, and one place to enforce the "no SBI / no PII" whitelist — `jsonb_build_object(...)` is an explicit allow-list of keys, anything not named cannot leak.
2. **`sbi_scores` is not joined at all.** B1 had to join it (server-side ORDER BY tiebreaker), so the contract there was "value enters the plan and leaves on the floor". The profile page has no ordering — joining `sbi_scores` for B2 would put SBI on the wire with no operational reason. The cleanest enforcement of §0 is the absence of the join. Any future change that introduces an `sb.*` reference into this function is a PR-review blocker.
3. **Contact PII is excluded at the function level, not the route level.** The RPC's `supplier` jsonb_build_object never names `email_primary`, `phones`, `contact_name`, `contact_role`, or `website`. Hiding fields in the route component would be a CSS / JS concern; excluding them at the function means the bytes never leave Postgres for this surface. When the paid-tier contact reveal ships, it will be a *separate* RPC behind an authorisation check, not a conditional branch added to this one — keeps the contract auditable.
4. **"Reviews" tab from `phases.md` is omitted entirely.** `phases.md` line 60 lists five tabs (Overview / Compliance / Media / Reviews / Contact) but `frontend-design-spec.md` §4.2 — which is the canonical anatomy source per `AGENTS.md` hard rule 3 — explicitly removes Reviews from initial scope ("No review table exists; do not stub a UI for it"). Stubbing an empty Reviews tab would violate the "do not invent" principle and force a future deprecation. "Media" maps cleanly to the Documents tab per §4.6 (the 7,049-PDF compliance mirror is the only media we have). Final tab bar shipped: Compliance (default) · Overview · Capacity · Documents · Contact.
5. **Tabs are conditionally rendered when their data is empty.** Capacity hides entirely when no capacity field is present (§4.5 explicit rule). Documents hides when there are zero `compliance_documents` rows. Compliance, Overview, and Contact always render — Compliance because every published supplier has at least one provenance row, Overview because it falls back to `address_raw` (92% coverage) and group line, Contact because it is the upgrade CTA and must always be reachable. Cards *within* a tab follow the same pattern: omit empty cards entirely; never render an "n/a" placeholder.
6. **Sanctioned suppliers still render.** `suppliers.is_sanctioned = true` does NOT short-circuit the page — every other card still loads. The only behavioural change is the red header banner and the top-of-Compliance Sanctions card. Buyers need to see the full provenance even (especially) for sanctioned suppliers; hiding the page would deprive the buyer of the receipts that justify the sanction call.
7. **OEKO-TEX certs skip the expiry chip entirely.** Per §4.3, the OEKO-TEX list payload carries no expiry (`with_expiry = 0` across 2,856 rows). Rendering an "expiry n/a" chip implies missing data; rendering "Valid" without an expiry would lie. The `ExpiryChip` component early-returns `null` for `kind === 'oeko_tex'` — the absence of the chip is the correct UX.
8. **Public anonymous profile at `(marketing)/suppliers/[slug]` is preserved untouched.** That route was shipped in F3 as the buyer-acquisition surface (server-rendered CSS-blurred contacts + sign-in CTA). The buyer-authenticated profile lives at `(app)/app/suppliers/[slug]` and uses the RPC. Collapsing them into a single component would couple two different data contracts (public minimal SELECT vs authenticated single-RPC composite) and two different gating philosophies (visible-with-CSS-blur vs hard upgrade gate). They coexist by design.

- ✅ **Spec B1 — Discover page** shipped 28 May 2026. First Phase-2 buyer spec. Authenticated buyer Discover at `/app/discover` (the existing public anonymous `(marketing)/discover` route at `/discover` is preserved untouched — the two coexist by design). New route file `app/(app)/app/discover/page.tsx` is a pure server component (`dynamic='force-dynamic'`) that reads `searchParams`, validates them against allow-lists (entity types ∈ {factory, buying_house, unknown}; cert kinds ∈ {wrap, oeko_tex, gots, sa8000}; sort ∈ {default, receipts, completeness, name}; `min_sources` regex `/^[1-5]$/`; `rsc_min` clamped 0–100), and calls the new `public.discover_suppliers` RPC. Reuses every F1 primitive (`Card`, `Tag`, `Badge`, `Button`, `ReceiptsRing`); zero new tokens, zero new dependencies. Filter rail is a native HTML `<form method="get" action="/app/discover">` — no client component, no `useState`, no `useRouter` — so URL is the single source of truth and Discover URLs are fully deep-linkable + shareable per `frontend-design-spec.md` §5.3. Filter facets shipped per the user-supplied scope: entity type (multi-checkbox), receipts ≥ N (select 1..5), certifications (multi-checkbox), RSC remediation ≥ % (number 0..100), location (city + district text), category / principal product (ILIKE substring). Sort control is a pill row of `<Link>`s that mutate `?sort=` while preserving every other filter. Empty state copy follows spec §5.6 ("No suppliers match these filters. Try removing the most restrictive one." + single "Clear all filters" button). Pagination is server-side (24/page, prev/next links). **New migration `supabase/migrations/0023_discover_rpc.sql`** adds `public.discover_suppliers(p_q text, p_entity_types text[], p_min_sources int, p_cert_kinds text[], p_rsc_min int, p_city text, p_district text, p_category text, p_sort text, p_limit int, p_offset int) returns table (...)` — `language sql stable security definer set search_path = public`, `revoke all from public` + `grant execute to anon, authenticated`. The function joins `suppliers` to `rsc_remediation` (active=true) and `sbi_scores`, computes `t13_source_count` as `count(distinct sr.source_id)` over `source_records` where `status='active'` and `source_tier in ('tier1_gov','tier2_industry','tier3_cert')`, filters published + non-sanctioned only, and **orders by `_sbi_total desc nulls last` as the internal tiebreaker** — but the RETURNS TABLE whitelist deliberately excludes `_sbi_total`. The SBI value enters the query plan and leaves on the floor; it is **never serialised** to the wire. Output columns: `id, slug, company_name, entity_type, city, district, source_tags, t13_source_count, completeness_pct, employees_total, established_date, principal_products, factory_types, rsc_progress_pct, parent_group_name, total_count`. Contact PII columns (`email_primary`, `phones`, `contact_name`, `contact_role`) are excluded at the function level — buyer Discover never fetches them; that surface is gated behind /pricing in a later Phase-2 spec. Applied to Supabase Singapore via the now-standard `docker cp + docker exec sourcebd-etl-run-* python /tmp/_apply_stdin.py` pattern (per F3's note that `etl migrate` re-from-scratch trips on existing enums). Smoke test on production: `discover_suppliers(min_sources:=2, sort:='receipts')` returns 2,792 rows; default sort vs receipts sort return demonstrably different row orders, confirming the internal SBI tiebreaker is being consumed by Postgres. **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors"; `pnpm build` ✓ Compiled in 26.6s, **16 routes** (was 15 after F3) — new entry `/app/discover` (ƒ dynamic, 170 B / 106 kB First Load JS — pure RSC, the +170 B is the route segment glue only); shared first-load JS unchanged at 102 kB. **Security posture**: server enforces published-only + non-sanctioned filtering at the function level; `security definer` reads RLS-locked `sbi_scores` / `source_records` / `certifications` / `rsc_remediation` strictly within the function body and returns only the buyer-safe whitelist; middleware already gates `/app/*` to authenticated users; URL state is server-validated against allow-lists (any rogue `?entity=evil` is silently dropped before the RPC call); the `Reset` button is a plain `<Link href="/app/discover">` (no destructive POST). **Receipts Ring contract honoured**: the centre payload passed to `<ReceiptsRing sources={row.t13_source_count} size={48}/>` is the count of distinct Tier 1–3 sources, never the SBI numeric or any derivation thereof. **New / changed files**: `supabase/migrations/0023_discover_rpc.sql`, `app/(app)/app/discover/page.tsx`, `ops/_spec_b1_smoke.py` (one-shot smoke test for the RPC; kept for future debug). Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions — 2026-05-28 (B1)

1. **Internal SBI sort is enforced inside the SQL function, not at the Supabase JS layer.** PostgREST cannot express "order by joined column, do not return the joined column" — any `.order('total', { foreignTable: 'sbi_scores' })` call has to embed `sbi_scores(total)` in the SELECT, which would put the SBI numeric on the wire. A `security definer` SQL function with a narrow RETURNS TABLE is the only way to satisfy the ai-workflow-rules.md hard prohibition while still honouring the frontend-design-spec.md §5.4 default sort. The function name is the entire contract; any future change that adds an SBI column to the RETURNS TABLE is a PR-review blocker.
2. **Filter rail is a native `<form method="get">`, not a client component.** The spec says "client component for filter rail + facet counts" but facet counts are not in B1 scope (they will arrive when faceted aggregates ship as a separate spec). Submitting the form is one round trip that mutates the URL; the page re-renders server-side from the new `searchParams`. Skipping the `'use client'` boundary keeps the route at 170 B of client JS and avoids hydrating a filter component on every visit. If interactive facet counts are added later, only that subtree needs to become a client island.
3. **All `searchParams` are validated against allow-lists before the RPC call.** Entity types, cert kinds, and sort values are checked against the literal arrays declared in the file; `min_sources` matches `/^[1-5]$/`; `rsc_min` is clamped to 0–100. Anything else is dropped to `null`. Defence-in-depth on top of the function's own input handling — `security definer` functions are a juicy surface and the function's argument list is the only attack vector exposed to anon/authenticated.
4. **`source_tags` drives the result-card pill row, not `v_supplier_registry_ids`.** B1 cards are dense list rows; the dashed-inherited-pill variant from §4.1 belongs on the profile header (B2). Showing the denormalised `source_tags` array (top 4 + `+N more`) is what the spec §5.5 prescribes for the card. The inherited-pill nuance lands when B2 ships.
5. **No `bsci` in the cert filter checkbox group.** The `cert_kind` enum still has `'bsci'` for historical compatibility, but migration 0012 killed the BSCI source and the `BSCI` Tier-3 source is no longer in `public.sources`. Surfacing a BSCI checkbox that would always return zero results would be misleading; the four shipped cert kinds match the four Tier-3 scrapers actually wired in production (WRAP, OEKO-TEX, GOTS, SA8000).
6. **Established date is rendered as the raw text the data has.** `suppliers.established_date` is a `text` column (migration 0006) because the upstream BGMEA / BKMEA / brand sources publish it inconsistently (`"2008"`, `"15-Mar-2008"`, `"March 2008"`, etc.). The card stat line displays it verbatim — parsing into a `date` is out of scope for B1 and would silently drop rows that don't parse cleanly.

## Recently shipped (Phase 1 — Foundation App)

- ✅ **Spec F3 — Auth** shipped 28 May 2026. Third (final) Phase-1 foundation spec. Supabase Auth wired end-to-end with three roles (`buyer` / `supplier` / `admin`) stored on `public.profiles.role`; middleware-protected routes with explicit public allow-list; sign-in / sign-up / magic link / forgot-password / reset-password flows; sign-out endpoint; public Discover + supplier-profile pages render with contacts server-blurred. **DB migration `0022_profiles_auth.sql`** (applied to Supabase Singapore project via the etl Docker container + `_apply_one.py` pattern — the `etl migrate` re-from-scratch path trips on the pre-existing `entity_type` enum from migration 0001, so per-migration apply remains the convention): creates `user_role` enum (guarded with `do $$ ... pg_type lookup ... end $$` because Postgres has no `create type if not exists`), creates `public.profiles (id uuid pk references auth.users(id) on delete cascade, role user_role not null default 'buyer', created_at timestamptz default now())`, partial b-tree `idx_profiles_role`, RLS enabled with single policy `pol_profiles_self_read for select to authenticated using (auth.uid() = id)` (no `insert / update / delete` policies — profile rows are written exclusively by the trigger, never by user code, and role changes go through admin tooling that uses the service-role key which bypasses RLS by design), and `public.handle_new_user()` (security definer) + `on_auth_user_created after insert on auth.users for each row` trigger that auto-creates a `profiles` row with `role = coalesce((new.raw_user_meta_data->>'role')::user_role, 'buyer')` (so supplier signups via the role-select radio land in their own surface immediately; everyone else defaults to buyer). Verified post-apply: 1 table, 1 policy, 1 trigger, 1 enum, 3 columns of expected types. **Supabase client helpers** under `lib/supabase/`: `server.ts` (`createSupabaseServerClient()` — marked `server-only`, awaits Next.js 15's async `cookies()` API, uses `@supabase/ssr`'s `createServerClient` with `getAll` / `setAll` cookie adapter, throws on missing env vars) and `middleware.ts` (`createSupabaseMiddlewareClient(req)` — returns `{ supabase, res }` so the caller propagates the `NextResponse` that carries any refreshed `sb-*` cookies; per `@supabase/ssr` contract the setAll closure mutates `req.cookies`, rebuilds `res = NextResponse.next({ request: req })`, then copies cookies back to `res.cookies` so the browser sees the refresh). **`lib/auth.ts::getServerRole()` rewired** without changing its public signature: dev `DEV_ADMIN_BYPASS=1` short-circuit retained at the top (never honoured in production, double-guarded by `NODE_ENV !== 'production'`), then `await supabase.auth.getUser()` and `select role from profiles where id = user.id`, returning the role only if it's in the `{admin, buyer, supplier}` allow-list. All F1/F2 call sites (`/dev/components`, `middleware.ts`) continue to compile unchanged. **`middleware.ts` replaced** (F2 placeholder deleted): `runtime='nodejs'`, matcher `['/app/:path*', '/admin/:path*', '/supplier/:path*']` (allow-list paths never enter the function), one cheap `getUser()` call + one cheap `profiles.role` SELECT per gated request, role gating is `admin→/admin/*`, `supplier+admin→/supplier/*`, `buyer+admin→/app/*`, with `supplier` landing on `/app` being deflected to `/supplier` (rather than 401'd) since that's their actual home. Unauthenticated hits on any gated path redirect to `/login?next=<encoded path+query>` with the `next` param sanitised on the receiving side. The `res` returned by `createSupabaseMiddlewareClient` is always the final response so refreshed cookies propagate. **Auth pages under `app/(auth)/` route group** (shared `layout.tsx` provides centred 80vh `<main>` + `<Card>` chrome; no new primitives created — plain `<input>` elements styled with `border border-hairline rounded-input bg-bg-l0 px-3 py-2 text-sm outline-none focus:border-accent-indigo` per F1 token contract): `login/` (client component using `useActionState`; two `<form action={...}>` blocks dispatching to `signInWithPassword` and `signInWithMagicLink` server actions; OR divider; "Forgot password?" + "Create an account" links), `signup/` (email + password + `<fieldset>` role radio defaulting to buyer; password min-length 8 enforced client-side via `minLength={8}` + server-side in the action; `signUp` server action passes `data: { role }` into Supabase user metadata so the `handle_new_user` trigger reads it), `forgot-password/` (single email field → `resetPasswordForEmail` with `redirectTo: <origin>/auth/callback?next=/reset-password`; intentionally returns the same success message regardless of whether the email exists, per OWASP user-enumeration guidance), `reset-password/` (single new-password field calling `updateUser({ password })`; only works inside an active recovery session, errors out cleanly otherwise). **Server actions in `app/(auth)/actions.ts`** (`'use server'`): all five actions (`signInWithPassword`, `signInWithMagicLink`, `signUp`, `requestPasswordReset`, `updatePassword`) share a discriminated `AuthActionState = { error?, info? }` shape that `useActionState` consumes; `signInWithPassword` validates the `next` redirect target with `isSafeNext()` (must start with `/` and not `//` to block protocol-relative redirects to evil.com) before calling `redirect()`; `siteOrigin()` prefers `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` and falls back to `x-forwarded-proto + x-forwarded-host` so magic-link emails point at the correct host in both local dev and production behind the VPS reverse proxy. **Auth callback** `app/auth/callback/route.ts` (GET, `runtime='nodejs'`): exchanges the Supabase OTP / recovery `?code=` via `exchangeCodeForSession`, then redirects to a `safeNext`-validated `?next=` (defaults to `/app`). On exchange failure it bounces to `/login?error=<message>` so the user sees something actionable. **Sign-out** `app/auth/sign-out/route.ts` (POST only — protects against drive-by GET log-outs from `<img src>` exploits): calls `signOut()` and 303-redirects to `/`. **Public Discover** `app/(marketing)/discover/page.tsx` (server component, `dynamic='force-dynamic'`): SELECTs `id, slug, company_name, city, district, entity_type, source_tags` from `public.suppliers where is_published = true` ordered by name, capped at 24 — contact PII columns (`email_primary`, `phones`, `contact_name`, `contact_role`) and `sbi_scores.*` are **omitted at the SELECT level**, so blurring is enforced by the server bytes that leave the database, not by CSS that the client can flip. Renders a 3-col grid of `Card` items linking to `/suppliers/[slug]`. **Public supplier profile** `app/(marketing)/suppliers/[slug]/page.tsx` (server component, awaits Next.js 15 async `params`): same column whitelist plus `entity_type, country, website, bgmea_verified, bkmea_verified, bgapmea_verified, btma_verified`; `notFound()` on miss or `is_published=false`. Renders Sources / Verifications / Contacts / Website cards. The Contacts card shows two **server-rendered** blurred placeholder rows (`bg-surface-l2 text-transparent [text-shadow:0_0_8px_rgba(0,0,0,0.5)]`) plus a "Sign in to view contacts" CTA linking to `/login?next=/suppliers/<slug>` — there is no payload to un-blur because the columns were never fetched. **Validation**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors"; `pnpm build` ✓ Compiled in 44s, **15 routes** (was 8 after F2) — new entries: `/auth/callback` (ƒ), `/auth/sign-out` (ƒ), `/discover` (ƒ), `/forgot-password`, `/login` (ƒ now — server reads `searchParams`), `/reset-password`, `/signup`, `/suppliers/[slug]` (ƒ); shared first-load JS unchanged at 102 kB. **Security posture**: server enforces auth + ownership (middleware + RLS + SELECT-column omission); UI blurring is decorative; `next=` redirect targets are sanitised on both action dispatch and callback; password-reset response is constant-time-by-message (no user enumeration); sign-out is POST-only; profile rows are insert/update-locked at the policy level. **Two follow-ups parked, not blocking F3 exit**: (1) email-template branding in Supabase Auth dashboard (default templates work today); (2) actual end-to-end sign-in test against a real Supabase user once the marketing team provisions a buyer pilot — the schema, RLS, trigger, and code path are all verified, only the live click-through is deferred. **New / changed files**: `supabase/migrations/0022_profiles_auth.sql`, `lib/supabase/server.ts`, `lib/supabase/middleware.ts`, `lib/auth.ts` (body swapped, signature preserved), `middleware.ts` (replaced), `app/(auth)/{layout.tsx,actions.ts}`, `app/(auth)/login/{page.tsx,login-form.tsx}`, `app/(auth)/{signup,forgot-password,reset-password}/page.tsx`, `app/auth/{callback,sign-out}/route.ts`, `app/(marketing)/discover/page.tsx`, `app/(marketing)/suppliers/[slug]/page.tsx`. **Deleted**: `app/login/page.tsx` (F2 stub — superseded by the `(auth)/login` route inside the new layout group). Architectural decisions logged below.

### Architectural decisions — 2026-05-28 (F3)

1. **`@supabase/ssr` is the cookie bridge under the "Supabase JS client" umbrella in `architecture.md`.** The architecture doc names "Supabase Auth" + "Supabase JS client" generically without enumerating helper packages; `@supabase/ssr` is the first-party Supabase library that adapts the JS client to Next.js Server Components / Server Actions / middleware via a cookie store. Treated as part of the JS client surface, not a new tool. No `architecture.md` amendment required — if the user reads this as out of bounds, the alternative is a hand-rolled cookie bridge built on `@supabase/supabase-js` alone, which would duplicate framework code maintained by Supabase.
2. **`public.profiles` is the canonical role store; role is a Postgres enum, not free text.** `user_role` enum guarantees writes can never put garbage in the role column even via the service-role key. RLS only permits the row owner to read their own row; writes go through the security-definer trigger or admin tooling (service-role bypass). The `suppliers.claimed_by` FK target column (already present from migration 0001) lines up with `profiles.id` for the upcoming supplier-portal claim flow without further migration.
3. **Middleware re-issues the response from `createSupabaseMiddlewareClient` so refreshed `sb-*` cookies propagate.** Returning a fresh `NextResponse.next()` would silently drop refresh-token rotation, manifesting as 401s after ≈60 minutes. Encoded as the contract of the helper itself — callers always return `res`.
4. **Public Discover + supplier-profile pages omit contact PII at the SELECT level (server-blurred).** CSS / JS blurring is decorative only and never a security boundary per `code-standards.md`. `sbi_scores.*` is never selected from any non-admin surface (hard prohibition, predates F3). Future buyer-authenticated profile view will run a separate query that conditionally includes contacts only after the middleware role check passes.
5. **Buyer surface `/app/*` now requires any authenticated user.** F2 left `/app/*` open; F3 closes it because the F3 exit gate requires "Three roles can sign in/out; protected routes redirect." Public anonymous browsing of suppliers happens on `/discover` and `/suppliers/[slug]` (allow-listed by the middleware matcher). Suppliers landing on `/app` are deflected to `/supplier` rather than 401'd — same UX for someone who clicked a buyer-targeted CTA from a marketing page.
6. **Magic-link / signup `emailRedirectTo` is composed from `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` (preferred) or forwarded headers (fallback).** Hardcoding `localhost:3000` would break VPS deploys; trusting `Host` alone would let a malicious `Host` header point password-reset emails at an attacker's domain. The preference order matches Vercel / Next.js production guidance.
7. **Sign-out is POST-only.** A GET endpoint would let any third-party `<img src=https://sourcebd/auth/sign-out>` log users out on page load. POST + same-origin form submission keeps the action under the user's control.


- ✅ **Spec F2 — App shell** shipped 28 May 2026. Second Phase-1 frontend spec. Route groups landed exactly as `frontend-design-spec.md` §2 prescribes: `app/(marketing)/` (anonymous home at `/`), `app/(app)/app/` (buyer at `/app`), `app/(app)/supplier/` (supplier portal at `/supplier`), `app/(app)/admin/` (admin at `/admin`). Old `app/page.tsx` deleted and replaced by `app/(marketing)/page.tsx` (same URL, now namespaced by group). Shared authenticated chrome `app/(app)/layout.tsx` mounts `<Topbar/>` over `<Sidebar/>` + main scroll area; both shell pieces are pure F1-primitive consumers (Card/Button/cn) and add no new tokens, no new fonts, no new dependencies. **Sidebar** (`components/shell/sidebar.tsx`, `'use client'`) chooses one of three slot lists by inspecting `usePathname()`: buyer 8 slots (Discover/SmartMatch/Saved/Messages/RFQs/Orders/Compliance/Settings → MagnifyingGlass / Sparkle / BookmarkSimple / ChatCircleText / FileText / Package / ShieldCheck / GearSix), supplier 6 slots (Dashboard/Profile/Inquiries/RFQs/Documents/Settings → Gauge / Storefront / ChatCircleText / Tray / FileText / GearSix), admin 6 slots (Overview/Queue/Claims/Sources/Scoring/Users → Gauge / Tray / IdentificationBadge / Database / ShieldCheck / Users). Active slot keys off `pathname === href || startsWith(href + '/')`, sets `aria-current="page"`, fills the icon with `weight="fill"` and tints it `accent-indigo`, plus `bg-brand-forest-tint`. Inactive slots use forest-tint on hover (motion A3 via `duration-hover ease-smooth` from F1 tokens). Sidebar hides under `md` per §2.1's collapsed-by-default rule — mobile bottom-nav arrives in B1 once Discover ships. Phosphor icons imported from `@phosphor-icons/react/dist/ssr` to keep them out of the client bundle for the sidebar's children imports; the sidebar itself is the `'use client'` boundary. **Topbar** (`components/shell/topbar.tsx`, server) renders: SourceBD wordmark (left, `<Link href="/">`), centred plain HTML `<form action="/app/discover" method="get">` with `name="q"` search input (zero JS — submits to Discover which lands in B1; the search box gracefully no-ops until then), notification bell + avatar circle on the right (placeholders, no menu wired). Bell + avatar buttons get the same forest-tint hover. Topbar uses `sticky top-0 z-30` with `backdrop-blur` so content scrolls under a translucent L1 surface — pure F1 token consumption, no new shadow definitions. **Three role landing pages** (`app/(app)/{app,supplier,admin}/page.tsx`) are F1-Card placeholders that name the spec(s) shipping their real content (B5 / S1–S5 / A1–A6). The supplier placeholder explicitly mentions the α/β/γ rule (suppliers never see SBI numeric); admin placeholder reminds that admin is the only surface where SBI may eventually appear. **Health check** `app/api/health/route.ts` — `runtime='nodejs'`, `dynamic='force-dynamic'`, exports a `GET` returning `{status:'ok', commit:process.env.COMMIT_SHA ?? VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA ?? 'unknown', ts:ISO}`; no DB call, no auth, deliberately minimal so liveness probes can distinguish process-alive from service-degraded. **Error pages**: `app/not-found.tsx` (404, server, Card + "Back to home" Button), `app/error.tsx` (per-segment 500, `'use client'`, Card + Try again + Back to home), `app/global-error.tsx` (root-layout failure boundary — renders its own `<html>/<body>` because root layout is unavailable; reloads display + body fonts via `next/font/google` so the page still rasterises if the root font loader was the thing that crashed). All three pages use only F1 primitives + tokens. **Placeholder middleware** `middleware.ts` at repo root (`runtime='nodejs'` so `lib/auth.ts::getServerRole` — which is `server-only` — imports cleanly): matches `/admin/:path*` and `/supplier/:path*`, calls `getServerRole()`, redirects to `/login?next=<originalPath>` if the role doesn't match. Buyer surface `/app/*` deliberately left open in F2 (public Discover with blurred contacts arrives via the F3 allow-list per `phases.md`); only admin + supplier are gated today. Real Supabase session reading and role provisioning are explicit Spec F3 scope and NOT pulled forward. **Login stub** `app/login/page.tsx` lives outside both route groups so the middleware redirect lands somewhere styled rather than 404'ing; copy says "Sign-in arrives in the next foundation spec" + Spec F3 meta tag. Validation: `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm lint` "No ESLint warnings or errors" (one transient `<a>` finding in `global-error.tsx` swapped to `next/link` per `@next/next/no-html-link-for-pages`); `pnpm build` ✓ Compiled in 82s, 8 routes (`/`, `/_not-found`, `/admin`, `/api/health` ƒ, `/app`, `/dev/components` ƒ, `/login`, `/supplier`), shared first-load JS 102 kB. New files: `middleware.ts`, `app/{not-found,error,global-error,login/page}.tsx`, `app/api/health/route.ts`, `app/(marketing)/{layout,page}.tsx`, `app/(app)/layout.tsx`, `app/(app)/{app,supplier,admin}/page.tsx`, `components/shell/{sidebar,topbar}.tsx`. Deleted: `app/page.tsx` (moved into `(marketing)` group). Zero F1 primitives modified, zero token additions, zero new dependencies, zero Phase-0 touches (`etl|ops|supabase` untouched). No emojis, light mode only, no SBI render anywhere.

## To do (next, in order)
1. **Spec 09 follow-up — per-brand factory-named evidence under the authenticity rule** for the two remaining reserved brands, Inditex and Primark. **Rule (hard):** a `BRAND_*` source attribution may attach to a supplier row only when the brand's own publication **names that specific factory** (or, per the 2026-05-19 narrow OSH extension, the brand is the named OSH contributor AND the OSH list is embedded on the brand's own corporate domain). Both Inditex and Primark currently fail both limbs (see `logos.lock.md` notes). Activation for either requires a future surface change on the brand's corporate domain or a deliberate policy extension; no scheduled work.

### Dropped / deferred
- **Spec 08d — SEDEX-SMETA audit harvester** — KILLED 2026-05-19. All six candidate surfaces dead: (1) `sedex.com` has no public per-supplier directory — full `page-sitemap.xml` enumeration (327 pages × 11 locales) shows zero pages matching `find-a-member` / `supplier-directory` / `factory-list` / `audit-list` / `members/<id>`; `/find-a-member/` returns HTTP 404; only public member-touching pages are sales/onboarding (`/become-a-member/...`) and a buyer-logo testimonial page (`/meet-our-customers/`, no factory list, no Bangladesh mention, only Google Tag Manager iframe); (2) `/meet-our-customers/` HTML grep returned no factory / no Bangladesh / no OpenSupplyHub / no embed; (3) WP REST API search across `/wp-json/wp/v2/pages` and `/wp-json/wp/v2/posts?search=Bangladesh` returned only sales pages and generic supply-chain blog posts — no per-factory data; (4) `members.sedex.com` is the paid platform (auth-gated, anonymous curl returns HTTP 000); (5) brand-disclosure payloads already in our pipeline carry no SMETA audit-id column (verified for 08c via `_HEADER_ALIASES` in `etl/scrapers/brand_disclosures.py`, same headers apply); (6) Sedex SECR / AGM reports publish corporate governance only, no supplier rows. Structural reason: Sedex's business model is that SMETA audits are private property of the audited supplier, shared peer-to-peer through the paid Sedex platform between members — a public per-supplier directory would destroy the value proposition. Identical shape to BSCI/amfori. **Actions taken (minimal — far smaller blast radius than 08c)**: (a) queue advanced so Spec 08e SA8000 is now #1; (b) `_CERT_POINTS['sedex_smeta'] = 6` **retained** in `etl/scoring/sbi.py` as a wired-but-not-ingested entry (mirroring how `gots`/`grs`/`rcs`/`fairtrade`/`sa8000`/`iso9001`/`iso14001`/`iso45001`/`bci` are wired-with-no-dedicated-scraper, so the weight is available to future Tier-1/2 cross-source evidence even though we will not run a Sedex scraper); (c) `cert_kind` enum value `'sedex_smeta'` retained as a tombstone (same Postgres-can't-safely-drop-enum-values reasoning as `'bsci'`); (d) no migration required — `0001_phase0_core.sql` never seeded a `SEDEX` row in `public.sources`, so there is no row to delete; (e) no edit to `etl/core/upsert.py::_TIER_MAP` (`SEDEX_SMETA` was never added); (f) no edit to `etl/tests/test_sbi.py` (the pillar-3 cap test already keys on `'sedex_smeta'` and still passes — it now serves as the canonical wired-but-not-ingested fixture).
- **Spec 08c — BSCI (amfori) certification harvester** — KILLED 2026-05-19. All four candidate surfaces (amfori.org public, platform.amfori.org, BSCI-in-brand-disclosure payloads, amfori annual report) dead, plus follow-up checks for iframe embeds / member-or-factory-list PDFs / `/our-members` / `/resource-hub` / `/transparency` page all returned nothing usable. amfori's stated model is "shared pool" gated to paying members; per-supplier disclosure is a deliberate non-feature.
