Removed from active ingest: `etl/scoring/sbi.py` `_CERT_POINTS['bsci']` dropped, `etl/core/upsert.py` `_TIER_MAP['BSCI']` dropped, `public.sources` row deleted via migration 0012, BSCI references stripped from `ui-context.md` / `logos.lock.md` / `phases.md` / `architecture.md` / `project-overview.md` / `spec-08b-gots-harvester.md`. `cert_kind` enum value `'bsci'` intentionally retained as a tombstone (Postgres can't safely drop enum values without recreating the type and every dependent column).
- **`commercial_claims` table + factory-website MOQ extractor** — dropped 16 May 2026. Eligible-supplier coverage too low (928 / 8,879 ≈ 10%, 699 distinct hosts) to be worth publishing. Re-evaluate after Phase 1 supplier-portal claim flow ships, where claimed factories self-declare MOQ / lead-time / capacity directly (Tier 1, supplier-attested).

## Phase 0.5 — Compliance document moat (post-Phase-0, pre-launch)
9. **Spec 13 — RSC document mirror** (high-value, ~1 day work):
   - New table `compliance_documents (supplier_id, doc_type, source, original_url, mirror_url, fetched_at, file_size, sha256)` where `doc_type ∈ {fire, structural, electrical, boiler, cap}`.
   - Extend RSC scraper to enumerate per-factory document links (5 PDFs/XLS per factory: Fire, Structural, Electrical, Boiler safety reports + CAP).
   - Mirror to Bunny CDN with attribution badge (`Source: RSC-BD, fetched <date>`). ~3 GB total storage (1,928 × 5 × ~300KB).
   - Profile UI card: "Compliance Documents" with download buttons + last-updated + source badge.
   - Inngest weekly re-sync to detect updated CAPs.
   - Fallback: hot-link to RSC CDN if takedown request received.
   - **Strategic value**: turns SBI score from "trust us" into "verifiable evidence" — buyers never leave platform to fetch compliance proof.
10. **Spec 14 — CAP intelligence** (Phase 1, paid-tier feature):
    - OCR/parse CAP files → extract `open_findings`, `closed_findings`, `critical_findings`, `target_dates` into structured columns.
    - Filter: "factories with zero open critical findings".
    - CAP diff alerts to saved-supplier subscribers ("ABC Garments updated CAP on 12 Jun: 3 items closed, 1 new finding").
    - Same pattern then applies to WRAP/BSCI/OEKO-TEX/GOTS certificate documents.
11. **Spec 15 — RSC industry benchmarks (monthly reports + updates page)** (~1 day, high marketing value):
    - Sources: `https://rsc-bd.org/reports/` (monthly PDFs since 2022 + quarterly + annual aggregates) and `https://rsc-bd.org/updates/` (4 program-area popups: Inspection & Remediation, Boiler Safety, OSH Training, OSH Complaints).
    - New table `rsc_industry_metrics (report_month date, scope text, metric_key text, value numeric, unit text, raw_text text, source_pdf_url text, mirror_url text, fetched_at timestamptz, PRIMARY KEY (report_month, scope, metric_key))`.
    - New scraper `etl/scrapers/rsc_reports.py`: list-page scrape → enumerate PDFs → mirror to Bunny CDN → `pdfplumber` parse the standard "INSPECTION AND REMEDIATION UPDATE" table (consistent format across months: Total Covered Factory, Brand vs Independent, Initially Inspected, ≥90% Remediated, Recognition Letters, Initial Remediation Progress %, Initial/Follow-up Inspection Findings %, Escalation Stages 1/2/3, Boiler/Fire/SUPS counts).
    - New scraper `etl/scrapers/rsc_updates.py`: extract the 4 program-area summary boxes from the modals.
    - Inngest monthly cron (5th of each month) re-checks the reports index for the new month's PDF.
    - **Strategic value (3-fold)**:
      1. **Per-supplier benchmarking** — show "this factory: 100% remediation" against "industry-wide: 85.35%" badge on supplier profile.
      2. **Market intelligence dashboard** — multi-year trend charts on platform homepage (sales magnet, free-tier upsell hook).
      3. **PR/SEO content** — auto-generated monthly "State of Bangladesh RMG Compliance" blog post seeded from the parsed metrics + chart embeds.
    - Out of scope here: per-factory data inside the PDFs (we already have it via the live API); only **industry aggregates** land in `rsc_industry_metrics`.

## Architectural decisions log
- **2026-05-28** (Spec F1 close-out — Design system scaffold + Receipts Ring contract). Decisions: (a) **Hand-rolled shadcn primitives, not `npx shadcn` init.** The CLI would have generated its own `components.json` + `lib/utils.ts` + default tokens that diverge from the §21 forest-green ladder, and the install also touches `tsconfig.json` `paths` in a way that conflicts with the existing repo layout (root-of-repo `etl/` + `ops/` + `supabase/` directories). Building the five components directly against `class-variance-authority` + `@radix-ui/react-slot` + `@radix-ui/react-tabs` is ~120 LOC, gives us exact control over which variants exist, and means no `components.json` / no per-component "registry" file overhead. The architectural rule "no new tools beyond architecture.md" is honoured — Radix + cva + clsx + tailwind-merge are already the documented stack. (b) **Receipts Ring centre payload is a count noun, not a score.** Per the 2026-05-20 "α now, β later, γ never" decision and `ai-workflow-rules.md` hard prohibition, the centre value must be the number of distinct Tier 1–3 sources, not SBI total / pillar value / A-B-C-D grade. Implemented as a required `sources` prop (number, source count) on `ReceiptsRing` with no SBI accessor anywhere in the component or the dev gallery. Colour ladder hard-coded inline (0=#7A0B1F, 1=red, 2=amber, 3+=green) matching the §21 spec; saturates at 5 to keep the ring visually full once corroboration is unambiguous. The smallest optical size (12px) hides the numeric centre entirely and relies on the surrounding card label, because the digit becomes illegible below ~14px. (c) **Next.js bumped 15.1.6 → 15.5.18 mid-install.** pnpm emitted CVE-2025-66478 advisory at first `install` (the deprecation banner is loud and unambiguous). Patched in 15.5.x — bumped to the latest 15.x stable rather than jumping to 16.x because eslint-config-next 16 also requires a corresponding bump and Next 16 is too fresh to absorb a security-driven dependency change without separate review. (d) **`/dev/components` is server-side double-gated, not just hidden in nav.** `process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production'` returns `notFound()` (the route literally does not exist in prod builds, so no admin-with-prod-DB can stumble into it) AND `getServerRole() !== 'admin'` returns `notFound()` (covers staging-with-NODE_ENV=development scenarios). UI hiding is not a security control per `code-standards.md`; both checks happen server-side before the gallery JSX is rendered. (e) **`lib/auth.ts::getServerRole()` is a stub returning `'admin'` only when `DEV_ADMIN_BYPASS === '1'`** in dev. Real Supabase session wiring is explicitly deferred to Spec F3 (Auth + session) per its TODO marker — F1 must not depend on F3 because F3 has not been scaffolded yet, but the function signature and import path (`'@/lib/auth'` + `'server-only'`) are stable so F3 can swap the body without touching call sites. (f) **Token bridge lives in two places by design: CSS vars in `globals.css` AND Tailwind `theme.extend` mappings in `tailwind.config.ts`.** CSS vars are the runtime source of truth (overridable per-page or per-theme without a rebuild) and the Tailwind mappings make them addressable as utility classes (`bg-bg-l0`, `text-ink-primary`, `rounded-card`, `shadow-l1`, `duration-tab`, `ease-smooth`, etc.). Components consume utilities, never raw `var(--...)` in className, which keeps Tailwind's purge correct and means a future dark-mode swap only needs the `:root[data-theme=...]` overrides to land. (g) **`fadeIn` keyframe is in `globals.css` outside `@layer base`** so Tailwind's purge does not strip it (Tabs content references it via `motion-safe:animate-[fadeIn_var(--dur-tab)_var(--ease)]` which the JIT cannot statically resolve). Wrapped under `motion-safe:` so the global `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` block correctly suppresses it. (h) **`etl|ops|supabase|prototypes` excluded from both tsconfig and eslint** — those are Python / SQL / static-fixture trees with no relation to the Next app, and including them would either tank typecheck performance or trip on un-typed JS/HTML files. (i) **`pnpm` build scripts `sharp` and `unrs-resolver` left unapproved.** Both are postinstall native-module compilers; on Windows-dev they are not required for `next build` to succeed (verified by the green build above — Next bundled `sharp` via WASM fallback and `unrs-resolver` is only used by future ESLint flat-config). Approval can ship later under the deployment spec when we cut Linux production images, where it actually matters. (j) **Validation green: typecheck + lint + build all pass.** `/dev/components` builds as a ƒ (dynamic) route with First Load JS 116 kB (acceptable — 102 kB framework baseline + 14 kB gallery code). Static `/` is 102 kB. No Phase-0 / scraper / ETL / DB code touched this session.

- **2026-05-22** (Moat deep-audit findings F1 + F2 + F4b — EPB address branch + Tier-1/2 entity_type/verified backfill + OEKO profile enrichment). Decisions: (a) **Same view-bug shape as BGAPMEA (P1) → same fix.** 0017 is a verbatim recreation of the 0016 chain plus two EPB branches; no scraper change; no base-table write. EPB phone/email are explicitly NULL because the EPB Exporter Database public surface publishes neither (verified by re-reading `etl/scrapers/epb_web.py::_build_record` — only address keys exist in payload). (b) **Address branches alone don't fix entity_type or *_verified flags.** Separated F2 because the audit's unknown×tag matrix made it explicit: 128 BTMA + 54 EPB + 1 RSC suppliers had no entity_type and 422 BTMA + 1,074 BGAPMEA had no `*_verified=true` despite the supporting Tier-1/2 source_record existing — `_apply_source_specific` simply had no branch for those registers. Added four branches mirroring the BGMEA / BKMEA pattern; EPB and RSC sit in a combined `('EPB','RSC')` branch because neither has a corresponding `*_verified` column on `suppliers` (the schema only carries `bgmea_verified` / `bkmea_verified` / `btma_verified` / `bgapmea_verified`). (c) **Backfill ran the post-insert update SQL once over the existing 8,473 affected rows** rather than re-ingesting the source_records — re-ingest would do the same thing and burn 30+ min for the same outcome. The backfill is structurally identical to what the new branches will do on future scrapes, so future runs add nothing beyond what F2 already wrote. (d) **OEKO profile keys expire — enrichment must be inline.** Probe of two `oeko_profile_url` values stored in the DB returned `Profile key has expired` (HTML body 763 bytes, no useful content). Same URL re-fetched from a fresh search-result row works. Conclusion: the URL carries a short-lived signed token; storing it for later enrichment is structurally impossible. The fetch must happen during the search pass that issued it. F4b therefore extends `OekoTexScraper.fetch` rather than introducing a new "OEKO enrichment" scraper that would have iterated the stored URLs. (e) **OEKO branch is `kind='factory'`.** OEKO-TEX certifies a producing site (not a corporate HQ), so the profile's published address is the factory by definition. No `mailing` or `registered` branch added; if a customer profile ever shows multiple addresses we'll revisit, but the probed pages show exactly one. (f) **Profile fetch is best-effort, not blocking.** `_fetch_profile` catches `httpx.HTTPError` and returns the empty dict on failure; the record still upserts with the search-row data unchanged. Reasoning: a transient profile-server 5xx must not break the ingest of the registry row itself. The 0.4s `PROFILE_DELAY_SECONDS` is a polite throttle, not a rate-limit defence — OEKO's portal has no documented RPS limit, but 2,576 sequential fetches × 0.4s ≈ 17 min adds ~17 min to the scrape's runtime which is acceptable for a once-per-month enrichment pass. (g) **Top-level ScrapedRecord fields are deliberately populated alongside the `oeko_profile_*` payload keys.** The payload keys preserve provenance for the address view (migration 0018); the top-level `email` / `phone_raw` / `website` / `address_raw` fields trip the existing `_enrich_supplier` COALESCE path so the supplier-level columns get filled when previously empty. No supplier-level data is overwritten — COALESCE preserves existing non-null values from higher-tier sources, which honours the trust hierarchy (Tier-1 BGMEA / BKMEA / RSC / EPB contact data always wins over Tier-3 OEKO when present). (h) **Re-scrape deferred to user authorisation.** Code + view + image are deployed; the actual ≈30-min re-scrape of 2,576 records writes to production `source_records` + `suppliers` columns, so per the operational-safety rule it waits for explicit user kickoff. Smoke test on a 3-record slice confirmed end-to-end correctness without committing. (i) **Audit + verifier + backfill scripts kept as gitignored `ops/_*.py`** per the existing per-spec diagnostic-probe convention. The audit (`_moat_deep_audit.py`) is reusable shape: address-cohort + entity-type×tag + key-frequency probes — kept on disk for the inevitable next moat audit.

- **2026-05-21** (Moat audit P2 — SBI Pillar-3 NULL-expiry semantic flip). Decisions: (a) **NULL `expires_on` = unknown, not active.** The previous calculator (`if expires_on is not None and expires_on < today: continue`) silently equated "we don't know the expiry" with "the cert is currently valid forever". That violated Hard Rule #5 (no evidence → no claim) for every cert with no published validity date. New behaviour: NULL is skipped exactly like an expired date. Conservative direction (subtracts unproven points; never adds). (b) **OEKO-TEX is the only kind affected today.** Audit (`ops/_cert_expiry_dist.py`) showed `wrap` / `gots` / `sa8000` all carry expiry dates in 100% of rows; `oeko_tex` has expiry in 0% (2,856/2,856 NULL). The OEKO Buying-Guide list endpoint only publishes `country` / `oeko_standard` / `oeko_standard_label` / `oeko_customer_id` / `oeko_profile_url` — no dates anywhere in the raw payload. Cannot backfill from existing source rows. (c) **Calculator fix, not scraper fix, ships today.** The honest move when evidence is absent is to stop claiming it. Adding per-customer validity capture from the OEKO portal (`oeko_profile_url` detail page parse) is a separate, larger spec because it introduces new HTTP fetches, new parsing, new rate-limit considerations, and a re-ingest of ~2,856 rows — out of scope for a debug-mode audit fix. Queued under Follow-ups. Until that spec ships, OEKO-TEX contributes 0 Pillar-3 points by design — the integer reflects what we can prove. (d) **Tests updated, not deleted.** Three existing tests in `etl/tests/test_sbi.py` asserted "NULL counts as active" outcomes; those were the tests **enforcing the bug**. Rewritten to use a future date (`date(2030, 1, 1)`) where they previously used `None`, plus a new `test_pillar3_null_expiry_not_active` codifies the new contract. Hash-stability tests that construct `Cert("wrap", None)` purely to exercise canonical-input ordering are unchanged — they don't compute Pillar 3. (e) **Full rescore on VPS, not incremental.** Ran `etl sbi --force` (10,186/10,186 upserted) because the `inputs_hash` includes cert kinds + expiries but the semantic of "active" is a calculator-side decision — the hash would not invalidate on a code-only change. `--force` bypassed `skipped_hash`. Pre/post snapshots (`ops/_sbi_snapshot.py`): non-zero Pillar-3 suppliers 2,722 → 1,007; avg total 10.49 → 8.81; median 7 → 6; max 61 → 54. The 1,715-supplier delta exactly matches the OEKO-TEX-only cohort (suppliers whose only `kind` was `oeko_tex` and therefore lost their entire Pillar 3). Multi-cert suppliers retained WRAP / GOTS / SA8000 points. (f) **No buyer-facing impact.** Per the 2026-05-20 receipts doctrine, SBI is admin-only — no UI surface renders the integer. The fix tightens the internal sort signal toward truth; nothing public moves. (g) **Audit script reuse, not new ETL spec.** `ops/_oeko_expiry_probe.py`, `ops/_cert_expiry_dist.py`, `ops/_sbi_verify.py`, `ops/_sbi_snapshot.py` are kept as gitignored ops scripts.

- **2026-05-21** (Moat audit P1 — BGAPMEA address-view bug, migration 0016). Decisions: (a) **View-bug, not data-gap.** Audit said 2,722 suppliers lacked an address row; payload inspection of the BGAPMEA cohort proved every one of those 1,096 BGAPMEA records had `bgapmea_company_address` and `bgapmea_factory_address` populated — the view simply read the wrong keys (`fields->>'address'` / `raw_address`, which the BGAPMEA scraper never writes; only BTMA writes those). Pure view fix; no scraper change, no base-table write, no backfill. (b) **Phone + email on `registered` only, not duplicated to `factory`.** BGAPMEA payload publishes a single `bgapmea_phone` + `bgapmea_email_raw` per company without per-location attribution. Attaching the same pair to both `factory` and `registered` rows would imply a distinction the source does not make. Followed the BGMEA pattern (which splits `factory_phone` vs `mailing_phone` because the source publishes the split) inverted: when the source publishes one contact for the company, that contact attaches to the HQ row, and the factory row is address-only. (c) **RSC-only 677 deliberately left untouched.** Probe showed `rsc_location` uniformly NULL (741/741) and no fallback address keys. Fabricating a default or pulling from sibling factories that don't exist would violate the no-guesswork hard rule. The 605→677 delta versus the 0014 close-out figure is because the audit corpus grew from 9,932 → 10,186 suppliers between 21 May noon and 21 May evening (Spec 16 + Spec 16 cleanup passes plus normal traffic). Same residual structure: RSC-only + WRAP-only factories with no name-matchable parent. (d) **Fallback `address`/`raw_address` branch kept on BGAPMEA leg, not pruned.** Probe showed zero BGAPMEA records currently use those keys, but pruning the fallback would risk silently dropping rows if a future BGAPMEA payload shape (e.g. a downstream export reformatting) reintroduces them. The branch's predicate now also excludes rows that hit the new `bgapmea_*` keys so it cannot double-emit. (e) **Audit script reuse, not new ETL spec.** `ops/_moat_audit.py` + new probes `ops/_addr_payload_probe.py` + `ops/_moat_followup.py` are kept as ops scripts under `ops/_*.py`, matching the existing per-spec diagnostic-probe convention. Not promoted into `etl/`.

- **2026-05-21** (Phase-0 cleanup — RSC sibling-factory address inheritance, migration 0014). Decisions: (a) **View-only fix, no column backfill.** Considered writing the parent's address into `suppliers.address_raw / city / district / lat / lng` for the 171 inherited cases, but the design rule is that every typed column on `suppliers` traces to a `source_records` row of the supplier's *own* — writing a parent's address into the child's denormalised cache without backing it with a child-side source row would create un-provenanced data and break the "evidence → cache" invariant. Instead the inheritance lives **only** inside `v_supplier_addresses`, which is already the canonical evidence surface and is what the SBI calculator + profile UI should read. Reversibility is `drop view v_supplier_addresses_direct cascade; <recreate 0004>` — zero data to roll back. (b) **Recursive-view problem solved by splitting into `v_supplier_addresses_direct` + `v_supplier_addresses`.** The inherited branch needs to read existing address rows for the parent supplier, but a view cannot reference itself inside its own definition. Pattern: keep the entire 0004 UNION verbatim under the new name `v_supplier_addresses_direct`, then make `v_supplier_addresses` = `select * from direct union all <inherited(direct)>`. Column list unchanged, so `v_supplier_address_summary` and every downstream consumer keep working without code changes. (c) **Provenance encoding inside the inherited row.** Tagged with `address_kind = <parent_kind> || '_inherited'` (e.g. `factory_inherited`, `mailing_inherited`), `source_ref = <parent_ref> || '#inherited:<parent_slug>'`, and the parent's original `source_code` + `source_tier` preserved (rather than minting a new `RSC_INHERITED` source). Reasoning: (i) the inheritance is a derived view of an existing Tier-1/2 row, not an independent attestation; minting a Tier-6 cross-check source for it would either trip Hard Rule #6 (no Tier-6 record alone) or require a new tier value, both worse than encoding "this is inherited" in the kind suffix; (ii) the `#inherited:<parent_slug>` suffix makes the parent identity machine-readable from `source_ref` alone, so the profile UI can render "Address (per parent factory <parent_slug>)" with a clickable link to the parent's profile, and (iii) keeping the parent's `source_code` means existing Tier-based filters (`source_tier in ('tier1_safety_register','tier2_register')`) automatically pick up inherited rows at the correct tier without per-consumer logic changes. (d) **PostgreSQL ARE regex compatibility — no `(?:...)` inside complex patterns in plpgsql function bodies.** First two iterations of `rsc_extension_base_name` used `(?:\s+buildings?)?` non-capturing groups and `[\(\-]` bracket-escaped character classes; PG ARE rejected both with `invalid regular expression: parentheses () not balanced` at call time (not at function-create time — the function body's `regexp_replace` calls are lazily compiled). Final form uses one `regexp_replace` per suffix token (four separate calls for extension / expansion / new_building / new_location instead of a single alternation) and plain `[-(]` character class. Each pattern is small and unambiguous, so the function is also easier to audit. Recorded for future migrations: **prefer multiple narrow `regexp_replace` calls over one wide alternation inside a plpgsql function**. (e) **Recovery cohort sizing was 171 by Python audit, 184 by SQL at apply time.** The SQL function caught 13 extra siblings the Python regex in `_addr_recovery_audit.py` missed (mostly `Unit-N` variants with trailing whitespace/punctuation the Python pattern didn't tolerate). SQL is now the source of truth for the pattern set; the audit script is kept as a quick estimator only. (f) **Out of scope and deliberately not fixed**: the 596 RSC-only and 15 WRAP-only residual suppliers with no name-matchable parent in DB. Their addresses are genuinely absent at every source we ingest (Accord JSON, WRAP Power BI, BGMEA web, BKMEA web, BTMA, BGAPMEA, EPB). Surfacing a low-confidence "best guess" would violate Hard Rule #5 (no Tier-6 alone). They stay in the verification queue for Phase 1 manual enrichment. (g) **Migration filename renumbered from 0012 → 0014 in repo** because `0012_kill_bsci_source.sql` already occupies slot 0012 and `0013_seed_sa8000_source.sql` occupies 0013. The migration was already applied on VPS under the name `0012_rsc_inherited_addresses.sql` (staged into `etl/logs/`); the repo filename change does not affect the applied DB state because there is no schema-version table yet (per the 2026-05-19 Spec 10 close-out decision (h)).

- **2026-05-20** (SBI positioning — α now, β later, γ never). Founder-driven strategic decision after reviewing how marketplace-class B2B sourcing platforms (Foursource, Alibaba, Globalsources, Made-in-China, Indiamart, ThomasNet, Maker's Row) versus paid-rater platforms (EcoVadis, Higg/Worldly, MSCI ESG, ISS, D&B) handle supplier scoring. **Finding:** marketplaces never publish a proprietary numeric score — they show third-party badges, verified-status, tier-by-subscription, transactional signals, and buyer reviews; paid raters do publish numeric scores but charge the rated entity (£1.5k–£30k/yr) and operate a methodology committee + appeals process + analyst staff. The two roles are economically incompatible (marketplaces can't credibly charge suppliers to be rated AND charge buyers for the marketplace; the trust topology collapses). **Reasons supporting the demote-not-delete decision**: (a) **Liability transfer.** Cert badges and register links are facts issued by third parties — sue WRAP, not SourceBD. A proprietary score is SourceBD's opinion and creates platform defamation risk on the supplier side and reliance risk on the buyer side. CSDDD / UFLPA / LkSG explicitly hold buyers responsible for non-delegable diligence — a published score gives buyers nothing legally usable AND increases platform liability. (b) **Two-sided market trap.** Publishing a low score on a paying supplier loses the supplier; publishing a flattering score that later goes wrong invites suit; letting suppliers see their score invites gaming; not letting them see it invites opacity accusations. Alibaba's Gold-Supplier tier is purchased not earned — deliberate sidestep, turning the rating problem into a subscription. (c) **Aggregation destroys signal.** Buyers in different segments (organic cotton tees vs denim vs activewear vs accessories) value different evidence. A composite that pleases all of them is meaningless to each. Showing underlying facts lets each buyer apply their own weighting. The platform refuses to presume. (d) **Anchoring corrupts decisions.** A single number is cognitively sticky; buyers sort by it, shortlist by it, skip the underlying receipts, then blame the platform when it fails. Alibaba retired its earlier Trustpass rating for this reason. (e) **Asymmetric downside.** A 95%-accurate score puts SourceBD in BBC headlines on the 5% ("SourceBD-rated supplier accused of…"); receipts-only doesn't carry the platform's name into the headline. (f) **Trust topology.** Marketplaces borrow trust from named authorities (WRAP / BGMEA / RSC). Raters mint their own trust (Moody's 100-year reputation, NRSRO designation, analyst staff). SourceBD has neither yet — a proprietary score requires capital, time, and brand SourceBD doesn't have, while the same data moat compounds more defensibly as receipts. (g) **Category-blindness** of the current Pillar 3 formula was the proximate trigger (a 100% polyester knitter physically cannot hold GOTS but still gets 0 of its weight; a CMT-only factory cannot hold OEKO-TEX but still gets 0 of its weight; the formula is product-category-blind), but the deeper issue — above — is the marketplace-vs-rater positioning. Fixing category-blindness alone (per-supplier-category relevance mask) would have required a 7-category × 12-cert (~84-cell) weight matrix + a `product_category` taxonomy backfill across 9,932 suppliers + per-cert tuning debates + a publicly visible score migration (today's 73 becomes tomorrow's 61 or 88), so even the fix is expensive and judgement-heavy. The simpler, more honest move is to stop publishing the number entirely. **Decisions taken**: (a) **α now — SBI demoted to internal sort signal.** The calculator (`etl/scoring/sbi.py`), runner (`etl/scoring/runner.py`), `etl sbi` CLI, `public.sbi_scores` table, and the `enforce_sanctions_zero` trigger are all retained as-is — the Phase 0 Spec 11 contract is preserved. RLS on `public.sbi_scores` is to be tightened to `role='admin'` only (no `anon`, no `authenticated` SELECT); admin panel can read for internal triage; default Discover sort uses `ORDER BY sbi_scores.total DESC` server-side, the value is consumed in the query and never serialised to the client (only the resulting row order crosses the boundary). PR-review gate added (`code-standards.md`): any non-admin route that selects `sbi_scores.total` or any `pillar_*` column is rejected. (b) **β later — tier label deferred, not committed.** If Phase 1 buyer usage shows demand for a single user-facing aggregate, ship a categorical tier label (Bronze / Silver / Gold / Platinum) derived from counts of independent Tier 1–3 sources + active-cert presence + RSC coverage — a category, not a score, less defamation-prone and easier to defend under CSDDD audit. Design is TBD and gated on actual buyer evidence (do not build before there's demand). (c) **γ never — do not become a paid rater.** SourceBD will not pursue an EcoVadis / D&B-style standalone supplier-rating product. This is a different company with different capital requirements (methodology committee + ISO 27001 + SOC 2 + legal appeals process + regulatory disclosures) and would compromise the marketplace positioning. (d) **Receipts Ring rename.** The brand visual (Score Ring SVG, used per `ui-context.md` everywhere a supplier appears) is retained but renamed "Receipts Ring" and its payload changes from SBI total → count of distinct Tier 1–3 sources, ring fill `min(1, count/5)`, centre label `<N> sources`, colour ladder mapped to source-count (0 → dark red; 1 → red; 2 → amber; 3+ → green). Forbidden in centre label: SBI integer, pillar values, A/B/C/D grades, the strings "SBI" / "Score" / "Rating". (e) **Files updated this session (6 of 7 context files)**: `project-overview.md` (pitch + core user flow + features-in-scope + success criteria), `architecture.md` (new invariant + new "NOT in the architecture" entry), `ai-workflow-rules.md` (new hard prohibition), `code-standards.md` (security section RLS + PR-review gate), `ui-context.md` (Score Ring → Receipts Ring, "SBI Verified" tag → "Source-verified", data-sensitivity ban, marketing copy), `phases.md` (F1 components, B1 filter + sort allowance, S2 supplier dashboard explicit omission, M1 marketing copy, cross-cutting principles #2 revised + #7 added "Receipts, not opinions"). `logos.lock.md` unchanged — governs external-mark rendering only. (f) **No code or DB changes this session.** The RLS tightening + admin-only API exposure ship at the first Phase-1 spec that touches `public.sbi_scores` (no Phase-0 spec needs it; the table is currently only written, never read by any user-facing surface that exists yet). The `_CERT_POINTS` dictionary and pillar weights are kept exactly as-is — there is no point retuning a formula whose output never reaches a user, and the internal sort signal is fine as a baseline compliance-breadth proxy. (g) **Open follow-ups** (not Phase 0; track in a future Phase-1 design spec): publish the "How we verify" methodology page copy (replaces the prior "SBI methodology" copy in marketing surfaces); design the Phase-1 server-side default-sort key formally (likely the SBI total descending tie-broken by completeness % descending tie-broken by RSC remediation % descending); decide whether the v2 review system surfaces a peer-cohort percentile string ("top 25% of woven-shirts factories with active WRAP") as the eventual replacement narrative for an aggregate — still not a SourceBD-issued number, but a peer-relative descriptor. None of this blocks Phase 0 close-out or the Source Fashion London launch.

- **2026-05-19** (Spec 08e close-out — SA8000 certification harvester). Decisions: (a) **Implement-over-kill despite tiny BD population.** Discovery probes returned 7 BD rows total (4 active "Certified", 3 "Withdrawn/cancelled"), not the optimistic ≈40-80 from the queue entry. Decision to ship anyway: endpoint is clean (unauthenticated WordPress plugin AJAX, no nonce/CSRF), parsing is straightforward (table + label-value popup), and the 4 active certs include flagship factories (SQ Celsius is part of SQ Group, Dutch-Bangla Pack is in plastics/packaging) — high-signal corroboration on existing register rows even if low quantity. Confirmed by the 67% cross-merge attachment rate observed at ingest. (b) **Endpoint discovered via WordPress plugin JS, not page sitemap.** The first guess URL `/programs/sa8000/certified-facilities/` returned 404; full WP page-sitemap enumeration found `/sa8000-search/`. The page is a thin client over the plugin `cwp-sa8000-search`; reading `/wp-content/plugins/cwp-sa8000-search/assets/js/functions.js` revealed the `processSaRequest()` function calls `ajaxurl` (the WP-global) with `action=cwp_get_results` and a query string containing a parameter name with a literal space ("Description of Operations") — needs URL-encoding (`Description%20of%20Operations=`) or `curl` rejects it as "URL using bad/illegal format". Standard plugin pattern; reusable knowledge for future SAAS-style plugin scrapers. (c) **Playwright `BrowserContext.request` instead of `httpx` because Cloudflare blocks Python TLS.** Curl with browser-matching headers got HTTP 200 from VPS; httpx with the *same* headers got HTTP 403. Tested three httpx variants (http/1.1, http/2, with `Accept-Encoding: gzip, deflate, br`) — all 403. Cloudflare on `sa-intl.org` is JA3-fingerprinting; no header configuration can fix it from Python's stdlib TLS. **Decision: reuse the existing Playwright dispatch pattern from `etl/scrapers/brand_disclosures.py::_download_playwright`** — `ctx.request.get / .post` runs the request through Chromium's TLS stack and Cloudflare lets it through unchanged. Zero new tools (Playwright is already in `Dockerfile`'s base image and in `pyproject.toml`). Warm-up step `await page.goto(SEARCH_PAGE_URL, wait_until="domcontentloaded")` populates the Cloudflare cookie jar before any AJAX call so the first list GET succeeds without retry. (d) **Migration 0013 applied via `ops/_sa8000_apply.sh`, not `etl migrate`.** `etl migrate` re-runs every SQL file from scratch and trips on `DuplicateObject: type "entity_type" already exists` from migration 0001 long before reaching 0013 — same idempotency limitation already documented under the Spec 13 close-out entry. Workaround: a per-migration script that reads the single target file, opens a psycopg connection via `etl.core.db.db.conn()`, executes, commits. Schema-version-table fix remains queued as a separate small ETL-infra spec. (e) **`certificate_no` keyed on the raw cert ID (incl. slashes), not the slug.** The slug helper `_certificate_no_slug` is only used in `source_ref` (`sa8000-<slug>`) where punctuation has to be URL-safe. `certifications.certificate_no` is a free-form `text` column with no URL-safety constraint, so keeping the original SAAS-format ID (`IND.23.15506/SA/S`) preserves human readability for buyer-app cert display and matches how SAAS's own search results show the value. The UNIQUE `(supplier_id, kind, certificate_no)` still works because both raw form and slug form are deterministic from each other — no risk of duplicate inserts under either representation. (f) **Withdrawn certificates kept, not filtered.** All 3 "Withdrawn/cancelled" certs land in `public.certifications` alongside the 4 active ones, with status preserved in the `scope` text and the withdrawal date appended. Rationale: historical compliance evidence is still evidence — a factory that held SA8000 for 3 years (Lenny Apparels: 2020-02-20 → 2023-02-19) and let it lapse is materially different from a factory that never held it. The pillar-3 SBI calculator already ignores expired certs (`expires_on < today`), so the +5 Pillar 3 bonus correctly does NOT fire for the 3 withdrawn rows — verified by the SBI backfill counting `upserted=6` (one for each of the 6 distinct SA8000 suppliers; the Pillar 3 delta itself fired only for the 4 with active certs). (g) **`_extract_city` strips trailing tokens that are pure noise** (`Bangladesh`, digit-only postcodes, `N/A`, `NA`, `-`) and returns the last remaining comma-separated component, with a unit test covering the 3 observed address shapes (DOHS Baridhara with `Dhaka-1206`; multi-section with `Mymensingh, N/A, 2240`; Dhaka Panthapath). This is SAAS-specific (no other source has identical noise tokens) so the helper lives in `etl/scrapers/sa8000.py`, not in `etl/core/normalize.py`.

- **2026-05-19** (Spec 08d KILLED — SEDEX-SMETA audit harvester retired). Discovery probe (executed from the OneProvider VPS to bypass Cloudflare's bot block on `sedex.com`) found zero viable public surface across six candidate paths: (1) full `page-sitemap.xml` enumeration (327 pages × 11 locales) contains no `find-a-member` / `supplier-directory` / `factory-list` / `audit-list` / `members/<id>` page — `/find-a-member/` returns HTTP 404, and the only public member-touching pages are sales/onboarding (`/become-a-member/buyer-membership/`, `/supplier-membership/`) and a buyer-logo testimonial page (`/become-a-member/meet-our-customers/`); (2) `/meet-our-customers/` HTML (348 KB) grepped for `bangladesh|factory|supplier directory|find.*member|published.*audit|smeta.*list|directory|opensupplyhub|iframe` returned only one iframe — Google Tag Manager — and zero Bangladesh / factory references; (3) WP REST API (`/wp-json/wp/v2/pages?per_page=100` and `/wp-json/wp/v2/posts?search=Bangladesh`) returned only sales pages (Supplier membership, ROI calculator, CSDDD compliance) and generic supply-chain-visibility blog posts — no per-factory data; (4) `members.sedex.com` is the auth-gated paid platform (anonymous curl returns HTTP 000); (5) brand-disclosure XLSX/CSV/HTML payloads already in our pipeline carry no SMETA audit-id column — already verified for 08c by reading `_HEADER_ALIASES` in `etl/scrapers/brand_disclosures.py` (only country / factory_name / parent_group / city / address / tier / products / workers are recognised); (6) Sedex SECR / AGM / annual reports publish corporate-governance aggregates only, no supplier rows. Structural reason: Sedex's business model is that SMETA audits are private property of the audited supplier, shared peer-to-peer through the paid Sedex platform between members — a public per-supplier directory would destroy the supplier value proposition (suppliers pay Sedex precisely so buyers will pay to view their audit). Identical dead-source shape to BSCI/amfori. **Actions taken (minimal — far smaller blast radius than 08c because nothing was ever wired for SEDEX in the first place)**: (a) queue advanced — Spec 08e SA8000 is now #1; (b) `_CERT_POINTS['sedex_smeta'] = 6` **retained** in `etl/scoring/sbi.py` as a wired-but-not-ingested entry (same posture as `gots`/`grs`/`rcs`/`fairtrade`/`sa8000`/`iso9001`/`iso14001`/`iso45001`/`bci` already have weights without dedicated scrapers — keeps the slot available for future Tier-1/2 cross-source evidence and preserves arithmetic in the pillar-3 cap test, which now serves as the canonical wired-but-not-ingested fixture); (c) `cert_kind` enum value `'sedex_smeta'` retained as a tombstone (same Postgres-can't-safely-drop-enum-values reasoning as `'bsci'`); (d) **no migration required** — `0001_phase0_core.sql` never seeded a `SEDEX` row in `public.sources`, so no `public.sources` deletion needed (verified via grep: only the enum line at `0001_phase0_core.sql:37` references `'sedex_smeta'`); (e) **no edit to `etl/core/upsert.py::_TIER_MAP`** — `SEDEX_SMETA` was never added (08d was always queued, never shipped); (f) **no edit to `etl/tests/test_sbi.py`** — pillar-3 cap test already keys on `'sedex_smeta'` (was switched from `'bsci'` in the 08c kill); 23/23 SBI tests remain green; (g) no `ui-context.md` / `logos.lock.md` / `phases.md` / `architecture.md` / `project-overview.md` strip needed because SEDEX was never added to any of those (only ever lived in the To-do queue + tests fixture + scoring weights + enum). Numbering 08d preserved as a tombstone slot so future references stay stable. One-spec-per-session honoured: SA8000 (08e) is NOT started in this session.

- **2026-05-19** (Spec 08c KILLED — BSCI / amfori harvester retired). Discovery probe found zero viable public surface: (1) `amfori.org` is marketing-only — no supplier directory, no `/our-members`, no `/resource-hub`, no `/transparency` page with per-factory data; (2) `platform.amfori.org` redirects to `sso.amfori.org/.../openid-connect/auth?client_id=sustainability-platform` (Keycloak, member-only); (3) none of the ingested brand disclosures (H&M, ASOS, Next, M&S, Inditex, Primark) carry a BSCI/amfori audit-id column — confirmed by reading `_HEADER_ALIASES` in `etl/scrapers/brand_disclosures.py` (only country / factory_name / parent_group / city / address / tier / products / workers are recognised); (4) amfori annual report publishes aggregate stats only; (5) follow-up surface check (this session): no iframe embed of a public widget on `amfori.org`, no PDF or webpage listing companies/factories — amfori's own stated model is "shared pool" gated to paying members + "system recognition" for buyers (per `/en/solutions/social/about-bsci` and `/en/news` posts on "shift beyond system recognition for more credible data"). Per-supplier disclosure is a deliberate non-feature. **Actions taken**: (a) `etl/scoring/sbi.py::_CERT_POINTS` — `'bsci': 6` removed; (b) `etl/core/upsert.py::_TIER_MAP` — `'BSCI': 'tier3_cert'` removed with inline kill-note; (c) `etl/tests/test_sbi.py` — `'bsci'` substituted with `'sedex_smeta'` in both pillar-3 cap tests (same +6 weight, preserves arithmetic); (d) new migration `supabase/migrations/0012_kill_bsci_source.sql` deletes the `public.sources` row `code='BSCI'` (pre-flight: 0 `certifications` rows with `kind='bsci'`, 1 `sources` row to delete); (e) `cert_kind` enum value `'bsci'` intentionally retained as a tombstone (Postgres cannot safely drop an enum value without recreating the type and every dependent column — high-risk DDL for zero practical benefit since no code path will ever produce a `'bsci'` row again); (f) BSCI references stripped from `context/ui-context.md` (logo strip), `context/logos.lock.md` (row marked killed), `context/phases.md` (parser list), `context/architecture.md` (Tier-3 line), `context/project-overview.md` (cert list), and `context/feature-specs/spec-08b-gots-harvester.md` ("Out of scope" queue). Queue advances: next-up is now **Spec 08d — SEDEX-SMETA audit harvester**, then **Spec 08e — SA8000**. Numbering of 08d/08e preserved (no renumbering) so future references stay stable.

- **2026-05-19** (Spec 08b close-out — GOTS certification harvester). Decisions: (a) **No schema migration required.** `cert_kind` enum already contained `'gots'` (migration 0001 line 37), `public.sources` already seeded the `GOTS` row at `tier3_cert` (migration 0001 line 64), and `_TIER_MAP` in `etl/core/upsert.py` already mapped `"GOTS" → "tier3_cert"`. The only gap was the scraper itself — all DB plumbing was pre-wired by Phase 0. (b) **Public JSON API beats scraping the SPA.** `global-standard.org/find-suppliers-shops-and-inputs/certifiedsuppliers` is a Joomla page that loads `/cs-gtb/config.js` containing `const csd__backend_url = "https://www.global-trace-base.org/website-api/v2/certified-suppliers"`. The backend is unauthenticated, returns clean JSON, supports ISO-2 country filtering (`country=BD` works; `country=Bangladesh` is silently ignored and returns global), and paginates by `limit` + `offset` (NOT `page` + `pageSize` — first probe wrongly used the latter and got identical results because the backend ignored unknown params and returned the default page). Direct API ingest avoids Playwright entirely. (c) **`source_ref` keyed on `system_id`, `certificate_no` keyed on `gtb_license_number`.** The two GOTS identifiers serve different purposes: `system_id` (e.g. `SCO021512`) is the stable supplier-account id that persists across certificate renewals; `gtb_license_number` (e.g. `GOTS-23594`) is the human-facing license id that may change on re-cert. We anchor `source_ref` on `system_id` so re-runs idempotently update the same `source_records` row; we anchor `certificate_no` on `gtb_license_number` so a renewed license becomes a new `certifications` row instead of overwriting the prior period's evidence. Fallback `f"gots-{system_id}"` when `gtb_license_number` is missing keeps the UNIQUE `(supplier_id, kind, certificate_no)` constraint satisfied for the small minority of rows without a license number. (d) **Scope filter not applied.** GOTS only certifies organic-textile operations — no footwear, no leather, no pharma. Unlike WRAP (which certifies cross-industry and required the `_NON_RMG_CATEGORIES = {"footwear"}` filter), GOTS BD rows are all in-scope RMG by definition. All 884 rows ingest without category filtering. (e) **0.2s detail-call throttle.** 884 sequential GET requests × 0.2s = ~3 min minimum; chose 5 RPS as a polite ceiling without coordination with the operator. Connection reused via `httpx.AsyncClient` with `Referer: https://global-standard.org/` to mirror the browser's referer chain (the API does not enforce it but it's good citizenship). (f) **Detail-call failure is non-fatal.** Network blip or transient 5xx on the detail endpoint falls back to the list-row payload (which has the supplier name + product_category but not the license number, address, or expiry); the scraper logs `gots.detail.failed` and continues with a `f"gots-{system_id}"` fallback cert_no. None fired during the live run (884/884 succeeded). (g) **Idempotency confirmed via concurrent constraints**: `source_records (supplier_id, source_id, source_ref)` UNIQUE + `certifications (supplier_id, kind, certificate_no)` UNIQUE + the `_write_certification` `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE` clause. A re-run would report `seen=884`, `upserted` possibly non-zero (the cross-source dedup engine recomputes), but zero new certification rows.

- **2026-05-19** (Spec 10 close-out — Cross-source dedup + merge engine refinement, data-engine portion). Decisions: (a) **Schema option A chosen over the addendum's `bh_factory_relationships` table.** The addendum models a buying-house ↔ factory commercial relationship (one BH places orders with N factories), which is a different graph than what Spec 10 actually needs — corporate-parent sibling-factory grouping (one corporate group owns N factories with no BH involved). Adding a single `suppliers.parent_group_name TEXT` column (with partial b-tree index) is a strict subset of the work the addendum would have required, scales to 10× current corpus without a join, and the column-level NULL semantics correctly express "no known parent group" without a sentinel row. The BH↔factory commercial table can land later as a separate spec when we have an actual BH→factory source ingested (not in Phase 0). (b) **Auto-merge band `[92, 100]` stays disabled.** Re-validated against the current corpus: the same false-positive class flagged in Spec 08 (e) (short-prefix + 3-4 generic industry suffix tokens fooling both `token_sort_ratio` and `token_set_ratio`) is still present — short distinguishing prefix + many shared generic tokens routinely produce dual-scorer ≥92 between genuinely distinct companies (e.g. "N.K. Accessories" vs "T.K. Accessories", "Mega Knit Composite" vs "Meghna Knit Composite"). All ≥85 candidates therefore enter the `fuzzy_match_review` queue for human review; nothing auto-merges. The 264 open rows are within the manageable admin-review envelope. (c) **Two-pass parent-group strategy: deterministic seeds first, algorithmic clusters second.** Pass A applies regex-anchored seeds for the 6 well-known multi-factory groups where the parent name appears verbatim in member names; deliberately conservative (NEVER overwrite an existing `parent_group_name`, conflicts logged not silenced). Pass B buckets by `first_sig_token` (first whitespace-split token len ≥ 4 not in a ~60-word industry stoplist) and surfaces buckets ≥ 3 as `group_parent_review` queue rows for human disambiguation rather than auto-assignment — necessary because most algorithmic clusters (700 found) are coincidental shared-prefix groups like "bangladesh" (28), "pacific" (21), "dhaka" (18), "jeans" (20), "fair" (20), not real corporate groups. (d) **`CLUSTER_MIN_SIZE = 3` for seed assignment.** Palmal Group has only 2 namable members in our current corpus (one of its known factories doesn't carry "Palmal" in its registered name); under the ≥3 rule Palmal was correctly skipped. Lowering the threshold to ≥2 would produce many noise pairings (e.g. two unrelated factories that happen to share a generic prefix). Palmal coverage will improve organically as more sources land. (e) **DBL Group and full Palmal Group are not name-detectable from current sources** and are deliberately out of scope. DBL's member factories (Matin Spinning Mills, Mawna Fashions, Jinnat Apparels, etc.) do not carry "DBL" in their registered names anywhere in our BGMEA/BKMEA/EPB/RSC/BTMA/BGAPMEA payloads; correctly surfacing them would require a Tier-1/2 curated corporate roster source (e.g. BGMEA group-of-companies index) which we do not ingest. Documented inside `ops/spec10_seeds.py` docstring; will be addressed by a future curated-roster spec. (f) **District co-location rule from the addendum is mostly inert and not used.** Empirically, only ~6 same-district clusters exist among the 700 algorithmic clusters; the rule would barely affect outputs and adds a false-precision implication (district equality is neither necessary nor sufficient for corporate-group membership). The clustering fallback uses `first_sig_token` alone and routes ambiguous results to the human queue. (g) **Orphan idle-in-transaction connection sweep is now part of the DB ops playbook.** Three `idle in transaction` connections from a Spec 11 backfill (PIDs 349806 / 350751 / 350758, 1.5 days old) were holding `AccessShareLock` on `public.suppliers` and silently blocked the Spec 10 ALTER TABLE for >5 min until `pg_terminate_backend()` was invoked manually via `ops/_terminate_blockers.py`. Going forward: any future `ALTER TABLE` on a hot table runs the `_locks_probe.py` script first; if any non-self backend holds AccessShareLock with `state = 'idle in transaction'` for >1 min it is terminated before the DDL runs. (h) **DDL through the Supabase pooler requires `SET statement_timeout = 0; SET lock_timeout = '5min'`** in an autocommit connection. The pooler's default `statement_timeout = '58s'` + `idle_session_timeout = '58s'` is correct for app-side queries but kills long-blocked DDL with a useless `QueryCanceled` error before locks can be acquired. New helper `ops/apply_sql.py` (psycopg, autocommit, sets both, executes the whole .sql file as one statement) is the canonical migration path until we add a schema-version table to `etl/cli.py::migrate`.

- **2026-05-19** (Spec 13 close-out — RSC document mirror, Phase 0.5). Decisions: (a) **Source URLs come from the already-cached Accord JSON API, not from a second crawl of `rsc-bd.org/<factory>` SPA pages.** `etl/scrapers/rsc.py` (Spec 06) already persists the 5 per-factory document URLs (`fire_inspection_url`, `structural_inspection_url`, `electrical_inspection_url`, `boiler_inspection_url`, `cap_url`) into `public.rsc_remediation` on every RSC run. Re-scraping the SPA would (i) require Playwright per factory (~1,617 pages) for no new information, (ii) duplicate the legal-/terms-of-use risk surface, and (iii) miss documents where the SPA truncates a long doc list. Reading from our own cache is faster, cheaper, and lossless. (b) **RSC SSL helper deduped into `etl/core/ssl_rsc.py`.** Spec 15 added an inline `_build_rsc_ssl_context()` in `rsc_reports.py` to handle the incomplete Sectigo chain on `rsc-bd.org`. Per the Spec 13 constraint "do NOT duplicate — import or refactor into `etl/core/`" and per Hard Rule #4 (no new tools), the helper was lifted verbatim into `etl/core/ssl_rsc.py` with an `@lru_cache(maxsize=1)` decorator so the AIA fetch happens once per process, and `rsc_reports.py` now imports the shared symbol. The new doc scraper host-routes: standard certifi context for `accord2.fairfactories.org` and the Bunny domain (both serve complete chains), RSC SSL context only for hosts ending in `rsc-bd.org`. (c) **Bunny HEAD returns 401, not 404, for missing objects** — verified empirically during the dry-run. `etl/core/bunny.py::exists()` is written as `status == 200` so 401 correctly resolves to "object does not exist → upload" without an explicit 404 check. Documenting here so a future contributor doesn't "fix" the helper to special-case 404. (d) **Per-migration apply script required until a version-tracking table exists.** `etl migrate` iterates every `.sql` file in `supabase/migrations/` and runs each on every invocation; migration 0001 already created the `entity_type` enum, so a fresh `migrate` call now raises `DuplicateObject: type "entity_type" already exists` long before reaching `0010_compliance_documents.sql`. Workaround for this spec was `ops/_spec13_apply.sh` (read target file → psycopg execute via the docker image with the env file mounted). Adding a `schema_migrations(name text pk, applied_at timestamptz)` table + skip-if-present logic in `etl/cli.py::migrate` is queued as a separate small ETL-infra spec; not in scope here. (e) **Mirror path scheme `rsc-docs/<supplier_slug>/<doc_type>-<fetched_date>.<ext>`.** Slug + doc_type pin the document to a specific factory+category (1:1 in the source schema); the fetched-date suffix lets us land newer revisions side-by-side rather than overwriting, which is what the buyer-app UI will want for compliance audit-trails. The DB `unique (supplier_id, doc_type, sha256)` enforces content-level idempotency: same factory + same category + identical bytes = one row, regardless of how many dates we re-fetch on. A future Inngest weekly cron will surface "new revision" rows naturally as new (date, sha256) pairs land. (f) **Origin 404 handling is silent + accept.** 6 documents (~0.085% of 7,055) returned HTTP 404 from Accord during the bulk run (e.g. `ananta-jeans-wear/structural` — audit PDF removed from origin). The scraper logs `rsc_docs.failed` and continues; no DB row is created, no retry burns the queue. These will re-resolve on the next weekly run if/when RSC republishes them, or stay quietly absent if not — exactly the right behaviour for an upstream-deleted artefact.

- **2026-05-19** (Spec 15 close-out — RSC industry benchmarks). Decisions: (a) **Sectigo intermediate workaround is client-side and tool-free.** `rsc-bd.org` serves an incomplete TLS chain (leaf only, missing the `SectigoPublicServerAuthenticationCADVR36` intermediate). Rather than `verify=False` (which would silently accept any cert and violate OWASP A02) or adding a new dependency, `_build_rsc_ssl_context()` reads the AIA `caIssuers` URL embedded in the leaf cert (`http://crt.sectigo.com/SectigoPublicServerAuthenticationCADVR36.crt`), fetches the DER blob over plain HTTP (the cert itself is self-validating against the root in `certifi`), converts to PEM via stdlib `ssl.DER_cert_to_PEM_cert`, and loads it into a fresh `ssl.create_default_context(cafile=certifi.where())` via `ctx.load_verify_locations(cadata=pem)`. On any error the code falls back to the default certifi context and emits a `warn` log — full chain verification is still attempted on every connection. Zero new tools, zero new deps, no `verify=False`. (b) **2025 monthly reports use prose, not tables.** RSC stopped publishing the previously-stable "INSPECTION AND REMEDIATION UPDATE" summary table in Jan-2025 and now embeds all numerics in narrative sentences across pages 4-15. Rather than guess at table extraction (pdfplumber finds tables on pages 2-3 of 2025 PDFs but they're empty), `_NARRATIVE_PATTERNS` was expanded 5 → 19 regexes anchored on the exact RSC phrasing for each metric (coverage, inspection_remediation, fads, structural, escalation, incidents). The multi-group "X factories are in different stages of the escalation process, with Y in Stage 1 and Z in Stage 2" pattern is dispatched via a sentinel `metric_key='_multi_escalation_overview'` that emits three rows (`factories_in_escalation_total`, `stage_1_factories`, `stage_2_factories`) from a single match. The generic `"factories covered"` fallback was moved to the END of the list so the precise 2025 `"Covered factory account number: N"` regex wins when present and the loose fallback only fires on 2024-era reports that lack the precise phrasing. Coverage outcome: pre-fix 12/40 reports parsed @ 5 metrics avg; post-fix **33/40 reports parsed @ 8.9 metrics avg** (median = 9, max = 17 for the 2026 with-EPZ tabular format). The 7 unparsed reports are all pre-July-2023, scanned-image-only PDFs where `extract_text()` returns empty; they're cataloged with `parse_status='failed'` for a future OCR-enabled spec. (c) **Updates-page image-URL snapshot, not OCR.** As of May 2026 RSC's `/updates/` modals no longer contain HTML `<table>` rows; each modal now embeds a single JPG dashboard image (e.g. `RPD-Data-Apr-2026.jpg`, `Boiler-Safety-Data-Apr-2026.jpg`). OCR is the obvious way to extract structured numbers, but Tesseract/PaddleOCR are not in the architecture.md tool list and Hard Rule #4 forbids adding tools without explicit approval. **Decision:** snapshot the image URL per (section × month) as a provenance row in the existing `rsc_industry_metrics` table with `metric_key='dashboard_snapshot_image_url'`, `value_num=1` (presence sentinel), `unit='count'`, `source='rsc_updates_page'`, `raw_label=<img src>`, `source_url=<img src>`. This preserves the existing UNIQUE constraint and re-run idempotency, gives the UI a stable URL to render the dashboard image with attribution, and turns structured-number extraction into a clean, scoped follow-up spec (OCR + parsing) rather than a tool-architecture change buried inside Spec 15. (d) **Per-widget click loop instead of text-locator.** Previous code used `page.get_by_text(title)` which resolved to multiple visible nodes (the trigger card AND the modal title) and caused pointer-event intercepts between adjacent modals. New code iterates `.elementor-widget-premium-addon-modal-box` widgets and uses widget-scoped locators (`.premium-modal-trigger-container`, `.premium-modal-box-modal-body`, `.premium-modal-box-modal-close`), closing each modal fully before opening the next. (e) **`urljoin` for root-relative hrefs** — `/reports/` HTML uses `<a href="/wp-content/...">` and `urlparse(REPORTS_INDEX_URL).hostname` doesn't combine with the relative href on its own, so direct downloads 404'd. Fix is one-liner `urljoin(REPORTS_INDEX_URL, href)`. (f) **`download_pdf` retry on `httpx.RemoteProtocolError`** — RSC's nginx occasionally closes the keep-alive socket mid-response (3 of 55 PDFs failed with `Server disconnected without sending a response` on the first attempt). 3× exponential-backoff retry (`1.5 * (attempt+1)` seconds) brings success to 100% of fetchable PDFs.\n\n- **2026-05-19** (narrow OSH extension to the per-factory authenticity rule, Option A): a `BRAND_*` source pill is also permitted on a supplier profile when **(a) the brand is the named Open Supply Hub contributor AND (b) the OSH facility list is officially embedded on the brand's own corporate domain** (the brand publishes the OSH iframe as its first-party disclosure surface). Both conditions are **runtime-enforced** by the scraper — it must capture a live XHR from the brand's corporate page referencing the contributor id before any record is upserted, and it must obtain the per-contributor `x-oar-client-key` embed token from that captured request rather than hard-coding it. Rationale: this preserves the spirit of the authenticity rule (brand makes a first-party, on-domain attestation of the factory list) without requiring the brand to publish a standalone tabular file when it has chosen OSH as its disclosure mechanism. Scope: applied to M&S 19 May 2026 (contributor 10061, list "Fashion, Home and Beauty Tier 1 Facility List - February 2026", 68 BD facilities upserted). Did NOT apply to Primark (no own OSH contributor, no per-factory disclosure on `primark.com`) or Inditex (publishes via OSH only as third-party, **no on-domain embed of an OSH iframe on `inditex.com`** — condition (b) fails). Limitation: a brand that is a public OSH contributor but does not embed the list on its own domain still falls outside this extension; that path remains reserved.

- **2026-05-19** (Spec 09 closure revision): demoted Inditex/ASOS/Primark/Next from "document-mirrored" → `reserved` in `context/logos.lock.md`, and re-stated the **per-factory authenticity rule** as hard policy for all Tier-4 brand sources. Rationale: a brand-wide Modern Slavery Statement names policies and partner orgs, not the individual Bangladesh factories that supply the brand; attaching such a PDF to a specific supplier profile would falsely imply "this brand publicly named this factory" when it did not. The mirrored MSS PDFs remain in Bunny as brand-level archives but produce **zero** supplier attribution and **must not** render on supplier profiles. Only H&M satisfies the rule for now (188 named BD factories in the published XLSX). M&S re-classified from "blocked" → `reserved` for consistency — same authenticity bar applies; extraction effort is bespoke per brand. To-Do #3 rewritten to require named per-factory rows before any of the 5 reserved brands counts as shipped.

- **2026-05-19**: Spec 09 (Brand supplier-list disclosures miner) — closed at partial coverage. Decisions: (a) **Document mirror is independent of structured parsing.** The framework treats discovery + Bunny mirror as success even when the parser yields 0 BD rows, because the mirrored disclosure file itself is durable evidence (and reusable for future parser improvements, OCR, or human spot-checks). Inditex/ASOS/Primark/Next MSS PDFs are preserved at `sourcebd-docs.b-cdn.net/brand-disclosures/brand_{code}/<date>.pdf` and dated in `etl_runs`; only the supplier-row materialisation is deferred. (b) **MSS ≠ supplier list for most brands.** Inspection of the mirrored PDFs (`ops/_spec09_pdfprobe.py`) confirmed: Inditex 15-page MSS has 3 unrelated tables, ASOS 11-page MSS has 5 unrelated tables, Primark 25-page MSS has 8 unrelated tables — all are tier definitions, partner-org lists, or grievance-mechanism breakdowns; none are tabular factory rows. These brands publish their actual supplier lists separately (Inditex transparency portal, ASOS standalone FwI Excel, Primark interactive map page, Next Tier-1 list page) — those URLs were not on the brand landing pages we crawled, so a per-brand bespoke discovery pass is queued as Spec 09 follow-up rather than guessed. (c) **H&M is the lone Tier-4 brand with a true tabular disclosure** (XLSX with country + factory name + parent company + address columns) — the framework's `_find_header_row` + `_match_columns` correctly extracts all 188 BD rows on first attempt, and the publish-eligibility logic correctly enqueues each match into `verification_queue` as `brand_disclosure_match_review` for admin confirmation before any cross-source link is made (no Tier-4 row publishes alone). (d) **Playwright was unavoidable.** Primark `/modern-slavery-act` returns 403 to plain `httpx` (Cloudflare-protected); ASOS `/our-supply-chain/` returns 404 to plain `httpx` then renders the disclosure link via JS. Both succeed after `_render_playwright` (Chromium headless `--no-sandbox`, locale=en-GB, 1366×900 viewport, `domcontentloaded` + `networkidle` + 4 s settle, then `page.content()`). The base class tries plain HTTP first and only falls back to Playwright on exception or empty-candidates — keeps the common case cheap, the hard case unblocked. (e) **Content-Type ext inference** (`_infer_ext`) handles extension-less CDN URLs like Primark's `primark.a.bigcontent.io/v1/static/Modern-Slavery-Statement-Final-2025` (no `.pdf`). HEAD probe reads Content-Type (`application/pdf` → `pdf`, `application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.spreadsheetml.sheet` → `xlsx`, etc.); falls back to `Content-Disposition` filename parse. Without this, regex-based ext detection misses ~all CDN-routed PDFs. (f) **`enforce_publish_tier` savepoint fix in `_maybe_publish`** — surfaced by Spec 09, fixes a latent Phase-0 bug. The publish-tier trigger refuses `is_published=true` for Tier-4-only rows; previously the trigger refusal poisoned the outer upsert transaction because we caught the exception with a bare `try/except` (PostgreSQL aborts the entire txn on any error unless a savepoint is active). Now wrapped in `savepoint sp_publish` so the publish-attempt failure is isolated and the surrounding upsert commits. Pre-fix every brand-only new supplier would have failed with `current transaction is aborted`; H&M live now confirms the fix works in production. (g) **M&S is genuinely blocked, not a parser limitation.** `corporate.marksandspencer.com/sustainability/interactive-supplier-map` serves supplier data only through a JS-rendered ArcGIS-style map widget API (no `<a href .pdf>`, no `<a href .xlsx>`, no static disclosure file). The scraper raises `RuntimeError` with a clear message and exits clean; CLI returns non-zero, etl_runs row marks `failed`. Bespoke map-API client tracked as Spec 09 follow-up. (h) **Scope close criterion**: Spec 09 success was *"establish brand-disclosure ingestion pattern + bring H&M live + mirror what's reachable"* — all three met. Per-brand URL hunts are bounded discovery work, not framework work, so they ship as small targeted follow-ups rather than re-opening Spec 09.

- **2026-05-19**: Spec 11 (SBI score calculator) + Spec 12 (Phase 0 acceptance gate). Decisions: (a) **Pillar sub-formulas were composed**, not lifted from a single spec section, because `SourceBD_Data_Pipeline_Spec.md` only gives the top-level pillar weights (25/30/30/15 = 100) and the Pillar-2 ladder; Pillars 1/3/4 sub-formulas were designed against the actual data we have and approved by the user before implementation. **Pillar 1 (Legal, max 25)**: EPB 8 + RJSC 4 + BGMEA 5 + BKMEA 4 + BTMA 2 + BGAPMEA 2 + multi-register bonus 4 (when ≥2 of EPB/BGMEA/BKMEA/BTMA/BGAPMEA), capped at 25. **Pillar 2 (Safety, max 30)**: returns 0 when the supplier has no `rsc_remediation` row (no safety evidence → no reward, including the DIFE default); otherwise fire ladder (≥100→15, ≥80→12, ≥60→8, >0→3) + structural ladder (≥100→10, ≥80→8, ≥60→5, >0→2) + DIFE default 5. **Pillar 3 (Certifications, max 30)**: best-per-kind dedup, expired certs ignored, weights `{wrap:10, oeko_tex:7, bsci:6, sedex_smeta:6, gots:5, grs:5, rcs:5, fairtrade:5, sa8000:5, iso9001:3, iso14001:3, iso45001:3, bci:3}` capped at 30. **Pillar 4 (Market credibility, max 15)**: breadth (`min(7, max(0, len(source_tags)-1)*2)`) + tenure (≥20yr→4, ≥10→3, ≥5→2, ≥1→1) + size (`employees_total` ≥2000→2, ≥500→1) + capacity present 1 + any `*_verified` 1. (b) **UFLPA bonus 0–10 is NOT stored**. `public.sbi_scores` has no column for it and the `enforce_sanctions_zero` trigger caps `total` at `least(100, p1+p2+p3+p4)`; persisting a US-buyer-specific bonus globally would mis-rank for non-US buyers. The bonus is computed at display time by the buyer-app UI when the buyer's billing country = US, against UFLPA-list cross-checks already stored in `public.sanctions_screening`. (c) **Sanctioned-zero remains a DB trigger concern** — calculator does not read `suppliers.is_sanctioned`. This keeps the calculator a pure function of evidence, and lets the sanctions list update atomically zero a score without recomputation. Live result: `sbi_sanctioned_zero=0` because no supplier is currently flagged (sanctions backlog returned 0 active matches). (d) **Idempotency via sha256 `inputs_hash`** over a canonical JSON blob (sorted `source_tags`, sorted `bgmea_reg_numbers`, sorted `certs`, fire/structural pcts rounded to 2 dp, `today_year` not full date so re-running mid-month doesn't churn, `_formula_version=1`). Bumping `_formula_version` is the documented way to force a global re-score when the formula changes. (e) **Established_date type coercion**: `suppliers.established_date` came back from Supabase as a `str` rather than `datetime.date` (column-type quirk from earlier BGMEA backfill); runner now wraps with a `_d()` coercer that accepts `date`, ISO-8601 string, or returns None on parse failure rather than crashing the whole batch. (f) **Batch driver design**: one server-side query with `LEFT JOIN rsc_remediation … active is true` and a `LATERAL jsonb_agg` over `certifications`, returning everything needed for the calculator in a single fetchall — no per-row round-trips. 9,760 rows fetched + scored + upserted in ~15 s on the VPS. Per-batch `commit()` (default 500) so partial failures don't lose the whole pass. (g) **Phase 0 acceptance criterion #5** (≥90% SBI scored) now reads 100%. Phase 0 is closed.

- **2026-05-19**: Spec 08 dedup follow-on. Decisions: (j) **Normalizer overhaul** in `etl/core/normalize.py`. Removed industry-word stripping from `_LEGAL_SUFFIX_RE` (it was collapsing real distinguishing tokens like "Fashion", "Knit", "Wear" and causing both false-merge candidates AND false-non-match misses simultaneously); regex now matches ONLY true legal forms `ltd|lts|limited|pvt|private|co|company|corp|corporation|inc|incorporated|llc|llp` (added `lts` typo seen in "Lyric Industries (Pvt.) Lts"). Added `_POSSESSIVE_RE = re.compile(r"'s\b", re.I)` stripping BEFORE punctuation removal so "Sadia Fashion Wear's" → "sadia fashion wear" instead of "...wear s". Added `_ABBREV_RULES` covering `knit ?wear[s]? → knitwear`, `sweaters → sweater`, `wears → wear`, `inds|ind|indus|industry → industries`, `mfg|manf → manufacturing`, `bros → brothers`, `int|intl → international`, `fabs → fabrics`, `gmts|gar → garments`. Added `_collapse_initials(n)` that joins runs of ≥2 single-letter tokens so "j m fabrics" ≡ "jm fabrics" (folds the dotted-initial variants). Pipeline order: `PUNCT_FOLD → unidecode lower → strip 's → & → and → abbreviations → strip non-word → collapse spaces → loop 4× strip legal suffix → _collapse_initials`. Backed by `ops/_normalize_regression.py` — 16 positive/negative cases (Goumati Knit Wears↔GOUMATI KNITWEARS pass; Meghna≠Mega still fails-to-match as required; Univogue Unit-1≠Unit-III preserved). (k) **Slug-collision retro merge** via `ops/_renorm_backfill.py`. Plan vs apply split with per-group autocommit (the original single-mega-transaction version timed out on Supabase pooler after 15 min and silently rolled back; refactor uses one `begin/commit` per merge group so progress is durable). Tier hierarchy preserved via `_TIER_ORDER` (BGMEA/BKMEA/BTMA/BGAPMEA=1 outranks WRAP/OEKO_TEX/GOTS=2, RSC/RJSC/DIFE/EPB/BEPZA=0 outranks both). Per-row merge logic: `_SCALAR_COALESCE` for typed columns (contact, email, website, address, city, district, lat/lng, regs, employees_*, capacity_*, machines_*, established_date, annual_turnover), `_BOOL_OR` for verification + `is_sanctioned` flags, `_ARRAY_UNION` for `bgmea_reg_numbers, phones, source_tags, principal_products, factory_types`. FK-bearing rows: source_records/certifications/partner_factories use insert+on-conflict-delete with per-row savepoints (handles `(supplier_id, source_id, source_ref)` UNIQUE collisions when both sides have the same source_ref); sanctions_screening + verification_queue use bulk reassign. Final result on a freshly normalised corpus: **9,915 → 9,760 suppliers**, source_records preserved at 15,750 (zero UNIQUE conflicts), certifications preserved at 3,290, 0 scratch slugs left, 0 sanctions matches retained. WRAP-only RMG residual collapsed **67 → 30** at the deterministic-norm level — remaining 30 have no exact-norm peer; per the Spec 10 plan they surface in the fuzzy/manual verification queue rather than being auto-merged. Bug found in monitoring queries: SQL `LIKE '__rn_%'` uses `_` as single-char wildcard so it falsely matches any slug containing "rn" in positions 3–4 (e.g. `farnoor-garments`); the correct probe is regex `slug ~ '^__rn_'` — added `ops/_final_state.py` using regex. (l) **VPS execution route**: local Windows host's psycopg connections to `aws-1-us-west-1.pooler.supabase.com:6543` hang at the Postgres startup-message handshake (TCP SYN succeeds, but no SCRAM challenge ever arrives) — possibly an aborted-session backend lingering after the original mega-transaction; the VPS reaches the same pooler in <1s. Backfill therefore ran inside the existing `sourcebd-etl:latest` docker image on the VPS with `etl/` + `ops/` bind-mounted, `--env-file /opt/sourcebd/.env`, under a `tmux` session. Pattern (sync updated files via scp, launch in tmux, poll via `tmux capture-pane`) is documented for future ETL ops where local connectivity is flaky.

- **2026-05-18**: Spec 08 scope-policy hardening. Decisions: (g) **WRAP `Products="Footwear"` is out of scope.** SourceBD covers the RMG supply chain (apparel + textile + trims/accessories incl. bags). WRAP certifies cross-industry; the live ingest pulled in `Apex Footwear Ltd.` (Products="Footwear", WRAP-only, no register tag) which does not belong in the moat. Patched `etl/scrapers/wrap.py` to skip a row when EVERY token in its `Products` field is in the `_NON_RMG_CATEGORIES = {"footwear"}` set — multi-category rows that include any apparel/knit/woven/sweater token still pass. Apex Footwear deleted retroactively from `public.suppliers` (FK CASCADE handled source_records + certifications). Conservative single-category list deliberately — borderline cases (e.g. "Bags and Associated Parts") are kept since trims/bags overlap RMG. (h) **OEKO-TEX residual is clean.** Audit of the 1,088 OEKO-TEX-only suppliers (`ops/_spec08_scope_cleanup.py::step3_oeko_only_inspect`) shows **0 leather-only** rows — distribution is dominated by STANDARD 100 (1,030), STeP (25), ECO PASSPORT (10), MADE IN GREEN (9), ORGANIC COTTON (8); all textile-domain. No OEKO scraper changes needed. (i) **WRAP-only RMG residual = 67 distinct (name, city) rows** after Apex deletion, surfaced via `ops/_spec08_scope_cleanup.py::list_wrap_only_rmg` for manual BGMEA/BKMEA/BGAPMEA cross-check — these are WRAP-certified factories that don't carry a register source_tag in our data but appear to be in-scope RMG by their `Products` field.
- **2026-05-18**: Spec 08 (WRAP + OEKO-TEX) shipped. Decisions: (a) **WRAP source**: Power BI `querydata` POST against the WRAP public dashboard rather than scraping the embedded report UI — the DAX query body is reproducible from the published report's modelId + visual config and returns a 435-row table for `Country=Bangladesh` in a single call. Cert is stored on `certifications` with `kind='wrap'`, `certificate_no=<WRAPID>`, `expires_on=<expiry>`, source_record `entry_ref=wrap-<WRAPID>`. (b) **OEKO-TEX source**: the consumer-facing Buying Guide POST (`services.oeko-tex.com/buying-guide/result/`) returns one row per (company × OETS standard); we issue 7 POSTs (one per `oets_standard` filter) with `land=12` (Bangladesh) `ort=397` (Far East) and de-dupe by `(WRAPID-equivalent label, standard)`. Cert row uses `kind='oeko_tex'` with composite `certificate_no='{idx}-{oets_standard}'` so the UNIQUE `(supplier_id, kind, certificate_no)` constraint allows multi-standard listings without collisions. (c) **Tier classification**: both sources land as `tier3_cert` (per `_TIER_MAP` in `etl/core/upsert.py`) — publish-eligible alone, which is correct since WRAP/OEKO-TEX certificates are independently issued by accredited bodies and are a recognised compliance signal even without a register tag. The 44 WRAP-only and 1,013 OEKO-TEX-only residual suppliers therefore stay published as Tier-3 anchors rather than being treated as orphans. (d) **Cross-source merge worked during live ingest** — fact verified by `ops/_spec08_show_crossmerged.py`: 1,440 / 2,453 (59%) of OEKO-TEX suppliers and 379 / 423 (90%) of WRAP suppliers were merged onto existing BGMEA/BKMEA/RSC/EPB rows by the 4-pass dedup engine at insert time. Earlier "0 cert/register overlap" diagnostic was reading the wrong join (cert-table side rather than supplier source_tags side); corrected. (e) **Retroactive merge pass on the cert-only residual**: built `ops/spec08_retro_merge.py` with a dual-scorer rule (require BOTH `token_sort_ratio ≥ 92` AND `token_set_ratio ≥ 92`, plus ≥2 shared significant tokens of length ≥4) and a queue-band rule (either scorer in [85, 92) → enqueue). Dry-run on the 1,037 cert-only residual produced 24 "auto-merge" candidates with ~50% false-positive rate (real-world distinct companies: Meghna vs Mega Knit Composite; Zaman vs Shaan Packaging; A.K.H. vs A.H. Packaging; N.K. vs T.K. Accessories — short distinguishing prefix + 3-4 common industry suffix tokens fooled both scorers). **Decision**: drop the auto-merge band entirely. All 258 dual-scorer candidates were inserted into `verification_queue` as `fuzzy_match_review` with confidence = min(sort,set)/100 and full diagnostic JSON (both scorer values, shared significant tokens, rule version) ready for the admin UI built in Spec 10. Zero DB rows mutated retroactively. (f) **Known normalization gaps surfaced by WRAP-only residual** (44 names): the `_LEGAL_SUFFIX_RE` regex in `etl/core/normalize.py` does not strip the typo "Lts" (vs "Ltd"), and possessive apostrophes split into two tokens ("Sadia Fashion Wear's" → "sadia fashion wear s"). Both are queue-band misses, not auto-merge misses; the queue rows surface them for human review. Fixing the normalizer is deferred to Spec 10 (it would have to be coupled with a full re-ingest backfill).
- **2026-05-16**: Spec 07 sanctions backlog tail closed (UK OFSI + EU consolidated + US DoL ILAB TVPRA). Three new scrapers, all Tier-5, all extending `BaseSanctionScraper`; `sanctions_list` enum already had the three values from migration 0001 so only one new source row was needed (migration `0008_ilab_source.sql` seeds `ILAB`). Decisions: (a) **UK OFSI** — use the stable `https://ofsistorage.blob.core.windows.net/publishlive/2022format/ConList.xml` Azure-Blob endpoint (no token, no auth, no JS). XML uses a default xmlns so `lxml.iterparse` matchers must use `{*}FinancialSanctionsTarget` and `find('{*}Tag')` wildcards. The XML is row-per-(target × address × alias) so we collapse on `GroupID`, take the first row whose `AliasType` startswith "primary name" as the canonical name (else first `Name6`), union the remaining `Name6` values as aliases, and keep only `GroupTypeDescription='entity'` + `GrpStatus='A'` (active). `entry_ref=ofsi-{GroupID}`. Result: 1,286 entity groups. (b) **EU consolidated** — use the FSF 1.1 XML endpoint `https://webgate.ec.europa.eu/fsd/fsf/public/files/xmlFullSanctionsList_1_1/content?token=dG9rZW4tMjAxNw` (the public token published in the data.europa.eu dataset metadata; **no `==` base64 padding** — EU's gateway returns 500 if padding is included). XML is FSF-namespaced, so we walk children with `etree.QName(child).localname` rather than fixed namespace strings (the EU schema also moves the namespace URI between releases). Filter `subjectType.code in ('enterprise','e')`; primary name from first `<nameAlias wholeName="...">`, country from first `<address countryDescription>` skipping "unknown". `entry_ref=eu-{logicalId}`. Result: 1,586 enterprise entities. (c) **US DoL ILAB TVPRA** — list page `https://www.dol.gov/agencies/ilab/reports/child-labor/list-of-goods` lists the latest XLSX as `<a href .xlsx>` matching `(tvpra|list[-_]of[-_]goods)`; we pick the highest year. Header row contains `Country/Area | Good | Child Labor | Forced Labor` (note the slash — `startswith('country')` rather than equality). DoL CDN serves a hard 403 to bare urllib UAs, so the request goes through the shared `HttpClient` browser-headers path. ILAB rows are *country × good* catalogue entries, not company names — they enter `sanctions_list_entries` for **audit-coverage / risk-lookup** purposes only; the hardened 2-significant-token matcher correctly produces zero entity matches for them, which is the desired behaviour. `entity_name='{country} — {good}'`, `entry_ref=ilab-{slug(country)}-{slug(good)}`, `status` joins the per-row Child/Forced labour flags. Result: 457 rows from the 2022 list. (d) Across all three new sources the full rescreen reports **0 active matches** against the 19,186-supplier corpus, confirming the hardened matcher (≥2 shared significant tokens of length ≥4 + rapidfuzz ≥95) generalises beyond the US axis without producing false positives, and ILAB's catalogue rows do not pollute screening even though they share the table.
