- **2026-05-16**: Dropped queued `commercial_claims` website-MOQ extractor (was To-Do #1 in this tracker). Coverage rationale: only 928 / 8,879 suppliers (≈10%) have an http(s) `website` and only 699 distinct hosts; even with perfect extraction the resulting column would be populated for <8% of the DB — a sparse Tier-4 column that signals weakness, not depth. The 0-claims smoke run on 10 suppliers (rps 0.5, ~4 pages each) also confirmed our regex/heuristic recall is low on real BD-RMG sites: most factories don't publish MOQ on their public pages. MOQ / lead-time / capacity is better collected via the Phase-1 supplier-portal claim flow, where verified factory operators self-declare with Tier-1 attribution. Rolled back: deleted `etl/scrapers/website_claims.py`, `etl/tests/test_website_claims_extractor.py`, `supabase/migrations/0008_commercial_claims.sql`; reverted the `WebsiteClaimsScraper` import + SCRAPERS entry in `etl/cli.py`; dropped tables `public.commercial_claims`, `public.commercial_claims_runs`, and enum `commercial_field` from Supabase (both empty / 10 bookkeeping rows discarded).
- **2026-05-16**: Added `production_capacity_dozen_yearly bigint` to `public.suppliers` (migration `0007_supplier_capacity_dozen_yearly.sql`). Sibling, NOT replacement, of `production_capacity_pcs_day`. Decisions: (a) two columns, two units, two reporting bases — never blended into a single "capacity" number; conversion (×12 ÷ working-days) would invent figures we can't substantiate. (b) Sane range 0..200,000,000 dozen/yr (~2.4B pcs/yr) — generous outlier guard; largest BGMEA factories declare ~10M dozen/yr. (c) Partial b-tree index `where production_capacity_dozen_yearly is not null` for filter ranges. (d) UI must surface as `≈ X dozen / year (BGMEA, self-declared YYYY)` with explicit attribution; never show alongside pcs/day without unit labels. Backfill via the same `ops/backfill_profile_columns.py` (one new statement, max-merge, idempotent). Coverage delta: BGMEA capacity coverage 0 → 3,459 / 8,879 (39%); union with BKMEA pcs/day puts "some capacity figure" at ~57%. MOQ research done same day confirmed zero authority sources publish MOQ — next To-Do queues a `commercial_claims` table backed by company-website extraction (Tier 4, evidence-quoted) per the hard rule "direct from company or valid authority".
- **2026-05-16**: Surfaced BGMEA + BKMEA + EPB payload to typed columns on `public.suppliers` (migration `0006_supplier_profile_columns.sql`). Backfill (`ops/backfill_profile_columns.py`) is pure server-side SQL, statement-per-transaction, max-merge for numerics so reruns converge. Decisions: (a) `production_capacity_pcs_day` is BKMEA-only — BGMEA reports `production_capacity_dozen_yearly` which is a different unit *and* a different reporting basis (annual not daily); converting (×12 ÷ working-days) would manufacture numbers, so we leave NULL when only BGMEA data exists. (b) `employees_*` use max-not-sum across BGMEA + BKMEA because both sources describe the same factory headcount, not separate cohorts; reported zeros are noise (BKMEA stores `0` literally for unfilled fields) and treated as missing via `nullif(..., 0)`. (c) `principal_products` is the union of BGMEA `principal_products[]`, EPB `epb_categories[].name`, and BKMEA `bkmea_products` split on `[,;/]`. (d) BGMEA `employees` JSONB is a free-form `{label → string}` dict (most rows have only `Management`; some have `Employee Male/Female`); we max-merge any numeric value into `employees_total` and only use the named `Employee Male/Female` keys for the gendered columns. (e) Added check constraints `employees_*_sane (≤0..200000)` and `capacity_sane (≤0..10000000)` — caught a real BKMEA outlier of 75M pcs/day on first run (per-row constraint correctly rejected; backfill clamps with `and val ≤ limit`). (f) Indexes: b-tree partial on `employees_total` and `production_capacity_pcs_day` (filter range queries), GIN on `principal_products` and `factory_types` (containment / array ops). Idempotency verified: second run = 0 rows changed across all 14 statements.
- **2026-05-12**: Single Next.js monorepo (marketing + buyer app + supplier portal + admin). Supabase for DB/auth/storage. Python for ETL.
- **2026-05-12**: Source trust hierarchy enforced via `source_records` join table + DB trigger blocking publish without Tier 1–3 evidence.
- **2026-05-12**: SBI score = 4 pillars (Legal 25 / Safety 30 / Certs 30 / Market 15). +UFLPA bonus 0–10 for US buyer accounts.
- **2026-05-12**: Sanctioned suppliers → score forced to 0 by DB trigger, red banner in UI.
- **2026-05-12**: No LLM in production logic v1. Smart Match is rule-based.
- **2026-05-12**: Vercel + Hetzner replaced by single OneProvider VPS running Docker (web + etl as separate compose services). Docker added to approved tools.
- **2026-05-12**: Idempotency via `source_records (supplier_id, source_id, source_ref)` UNIQUE + sha256 raw_hash. Reruns are safe.
- **2026-05-12**: Supabase pooler (port 6543) requires `prepare_threshold=None` on every connection — server-side prepared statements are not supported by the transaction pooler.
- **2026-05-12**: BKMEA detail page requires browser User-Agent + `Referer: https://member.bkmea.com/member-home` header or returns 0 bytes. Encoded in `_BROWSER_HEADERS` constant.
- **2026-05-12**: Supervisor pattern (ops/supervisor.sh) — Phase A list scraper with retry, Phase B enrichment loop with empty-streak termination — proved effective for unattended overnight runs in detached tmux.
- **2026-05-14**: Decision — host RSC compliance documents (Fire/Structural/Electrical/Boiler/CAP) inline on supplier profiles via Bunny CDN mirror with attribution. Phase 0.5 priority. Reasoning: makes SBI score auditable ("trust us" → "verifiable"), keeps compliance teams on platform (workflow lock-in), low storage cost (~3 GB), low legal risk (publicly downloadable, attributed).
- **2026-05-14**: Decision — ingest RSC monthly reports (PDFs since 2022, ~50 reports) + updates-page program metrics into `rsc_industry_metrics` time-series table (Spec 15). Industry-aggregate scope only — per-factory data already covered by the live API scraper. Drives per-supplier benchmark badges, market-intelligence dashboard, and SEO content engine. Approach: scrape reports index → mirror PDFs to Bunny → `pdfplumber` parse the consistent summary table.
- **2026-05-14**: Spec 15 code complete — migration `0003_rsc_industry_metrics.sql` (rsc_industry_metrics, rsc_monthly_reports, content_drafts), scrapers `rsc_reports` (PDF → KPI parse) + `rsc_updates` (Playwright modal scrape), content generator `etl/content/monthly_digest.py` (markdown newsletter + ~1,000-word blog post per month with MoM+YoY deltas), CLI commands `etl run rsc_reports`, `etl run rsc_updates`, `etl digest <YYYY-MM|latest>`. **Deployment to VPS pending** — SSH session was hanging at time of build.
- **2026-05-14**: Live RSC scraper finished but only enumerated **20 factories** (not the expected ~1,928). Pagination on `rsc-bd.org/factories/` not being expanded by the Playwright loop. Logged as bug; the 20 enriched rows are healthy. Need to inspect the SPA's pagination control and either click-through or hit the underlying API endpoint directly.
- **2026-05-14**: RSC pagination bug fixed — discovered the public `rsc-bd.org/factories/` page is a thin client over `https://accord2.fairfactories.org/api/v1/factories` (visible in `factory-scripts.js`). Rewrote `etl/scrapers/rsc.py` to call the JSON API directly (`httpx`, 200/page, follow `pagination.next`, drops Playwright). Run completed in ~66 minutes: **2,300 records seen, 2,300 upserted, 0 skipped**. Net DB result: 2,232 RSC-tagged suppliers (some RSC factory IDs collapsed by fuzzy match into the same supplier with multiple `source_records`), 2,232 `rsc_remediation` rows, 369 multi-source (RSC+BKMEA) suppliers, 569 distinct parent groups, 994 with completed safety training, 811 at 100% remediation. Top groups by factory count: Biswas (20), Ha-Meem (16), Palmal (16), Mondol (14), NASSA (14), Standard (13), DBL (13).
- **2026-05-14**: SSH-from-VS-Code-PowerShell hang root-caused — `cmd /c "ssh ..."` wrapper silently swallows stdout. Working pattern: `ssh.exe -i $env:USERPROFILE\.ssh\sourcebd_vps -o BatchMode=yes -n root@<ip> '<remote-cmd>'` invoked directly from PowerShell (the `-n` no-stdin flag is essential). For chained remote commands: upload `.sh` script via `scp.exe` and run via `bash /tmp/foo.sh`. Permanent ops rule recorded in `/memories/scraping-ops.md`.
- **2026-05-14**: BGMEA Associate Members PDF parser rewritten — original single-column regex (`Reg: N`) returned 0 records because the PDF is two-column with the format `Name (Reg-N)`. New implementation uses `pdfplumber` x-coordinate word grouping (`_column_lines(page, x_lo, x_hi)`) to split the layout, then walks lines per column anchored on `^(?P<name>.+?)\s*\(Reg-(?P<reg>\d+)\)\s*$`. Result: 1,678 records parsed, 1,559 BGMEA-tagged suppliers, 1,445 net new buying_house entities. `payload['bgmea_member_type']='associate_buying_house'`.
- **2026-05-14**: New `ScrapedRecord.entity_type` override field added (defaults None). `_insert_supplier` now uses `rec.entity_type or _entity_type_for(rec.source_code)` so individual scrapers can override the source-default entity type. Reason: BGMEA carries both buying houses (PDF) and factories (web), needed two different defaults from same source_code.
- **2026-05-14**: BGMEA general-members web scraper built (`etl/scrapers/bgmea_web.py`, ~330 lines). Crawls 214 list pages of `https://www.bgmea.com.bd/page/member-list`, then per-member fetches `https://www.bgmea.com.bd/member/{id}` and parses 3 server-rendered tab-panes (`#company_info`, `#address_info`, `#final_info`) for: BGMEA Reg, EPB Reg, directors[], contact/mailing/factory addresses with phone/email/fax, established date, factory types, employees breakdown, machine count, production capacity, certifications, principal products, annual turnover history. `entity_type='factory'` forced. `source_ref='general:{reg}'` namespaced to avoid collision with PDF associate refs. Run: 4,273 detail pages × ~0.7s = ~50 min at rps=2; result +3,452 BGMEA-tagged suppliers, total 5,011 BGMEA / 7,872 overall. Heavy cross-source merge: only 2,053 net new (most existing RSC/BKMEA factories matched via 4-pass dedup).
- **2026-05-14**: Per-authority address view shipped (`supabase/migrations/0004_supplier_address_views.sql`). Two views over `source_records.fields` (no schema changes): `v_supplier_addresses` returns one row per `(supplier, source, kind)` where kind ∈ {factory, mailing, registered}, includes phone/email tied to that address; `v_supplier_address_summary` aggregates per supplier as jsonb array. Reasoning: BGMEA stores distinct factory+mailing addresses, BKMEA stores distinct factory+mailing addresses — collapsing to a single `suppliers.address_raw` lost information that buyers need (e.g. FUTURE CLOTHING LTD has BGMEA Dhaka mailing + Cantonment factory, BKMEA Gazipur factory). Coverage: 6,920/7,872 suppliers (87%) expose ≥1 verified address; 15,656 address rows total. Future profile UI renders "Addresses on file" panel grouped by authority + tier badge.
- **2026-05-15**: EPB exporter list scraper shipped — adds 8,879 EPB-tagged suppliers (total DB now 19,186). EPB is a Tier 1 government source, so qualifies suppliers for publish-eligibility on its own.
- **2026-05-15**: Spec 07 US axis complete. Three Tier 5 sanctions sources live: UFLPA Entity List (160 entries), CBP US Withhold Release Orders (67), OFAC SDN (9,670 entries). OFAC SDN scraper filters `sdn_type == 'entity'` at parse time — vessels, aircraft, and individuals are dropped. 9,289 pre-filter rows purged from DB by `ops/sanctions_purge_non_entity.py`.
- **2026-05-15**: Sanctions matcher hardened in `etl/core/sanctions.py`. Two new gates before rapidfuzz scoring: (a) supplier name must have ≥2 "significant" tokens (length ≥4) — discards 1-word company names that produced false positives like a single shared word matching a 3-word OFAC entry; (b) candidate target must share ≥2 significant tokens with the supplier — eliminates O(N×M) full fuzzy comparison and removes the spurious-match class entirely. Score threshold raised to 95. **Full rescreen result across all 19,186 suppliers: 0 active matches, 0 flagged suppliers** (3 historical inactive rows kept for audit trail).
- **2026-05-15**: `context/logos.lock.md` committed — single source of truth for every external mark allowed in product. Visual contract: square 6 px radius; sizes xs 16 / sm 20 / md 32; monochrome (`--ink-700` on `--ink-100`) by default; original colour permitted only inside source-detail drawer at md size; tier ring (1 px outer) on sm + md only (xs flat); brands rendered as typography wordmarks (`<BrandWordmark name="H&M" />`), never as bitmap logos; stale state at 60 % opacity after 90 days; all marks `role="img"` + `aria-label="Source: <full name>"`; honours `prefers-reduced-motion`. Registry contains 18 source rows from `public.sources` plus 6 reserved `BRAND_*` slots that activate when Spec 09 lands. PR-gate: no registry row → no render.

## Follow-ups / parking lot
- **Verification queue backlog (snapshot 21 May 2026).** `public.verification_queue` pending rows: **1,390 total** = `group_parent_review` 1,051 (oldest 2026-05-19) + `fuzzy_match_review` 264 (oldest 2026-05-17) + `brand_disclosure_match_review` 75 (oldest 2026-05-18). All three queues await the Phase 4 admin UI (Specs A3/A4) for human disambiguation. No automated decision is acceptable for any of these queue types — each row exists precisely because deterministic matching could not produce a confident answer (Hard Rule #5/6). The queue is growing during Phase 0 ingest, as designed; it is not technical debt.
- **OEKO-TEX per-customer validity capture (future Phase-0 spec).** Today's `etl/scrapers/oeko_tex.py` only ingests the Buying-Guide list endpoint, whose payload has no validity dates — so every `certifications.expires_on` row for `kind='oeko_tex'` is NULL (2,856/2,856). Post-2026-05-21 P2 calculator fix, NULL expiry = not active, so all OEKO-TEX certs currently contribute 0 to SBI Pillar-3. To recover up to +7 per OEKO-certed supplier with evidence, extend the scraper to drill each `fields->>'oeko_profile_url'` and parse the OEKO portal's per-customer validity dates, write them into `certifications.expires_on`. Scope: new HTTP path (rate-limit guard) + parser + idempotent re-ingest of the existing 2,856 source_records (use the established `etl/core/` `BaseScraper` Pass-0 self-source idempotency). Acceptance: ≥80% of active OEKO certs get a non-NULL `expires_on`; rescore confirms Pillar-3 distribution recovers proportionally.
